> **Prototype reference:** artifact slug `dealspro-studio`. All figures in the prototype are SAMPLE data. Publishing in the prototype is a mock — no Drop goes live, no message is sent, no real Stripe/QR/backend exists there.
> **First-release path (unchanged, the only launch scope):** 1 Restaurant → 2 AI / manual draft → 3 Video + smart-menu preview → 4 Approval → 5 Publish now or schedule → 6 Stripe checkout → 7 QR redeem → 8 Resolve cancellations, refunds, invalid QR and inventory exceptions. Everything else ships incrementally.
> **Standing rule (unchanged):** AI proposes; humans approve and execute. LLMs must never directly charge customers, modify inventory, delete orders, or bypass publishing controls. Exactly two deliberate human decisions, always: (a) the restaurant agrees to the exact offer terms; (b) Mahendra authorizes EVERY publish. No auto-publishing, ever.
> **Build approach (RESOLVED Nov 8, 2026):** NEW BUILD FROM SCRATCH, decided. This supersedes the Oct 30 preservation rule (no starting over, no DB replacement, no parallel checkout) for this build. BUILD HOLD still stands: do not build anything until Mahendra says so.

## Build order — Now / Next / Build later / Delete

**Source:** the delete pass (`dealspro-delete-pass-report.md`, Nov 22, 2026 — KEEP 278 / DEFER 271 / DELETE 6 of 555) plus §51 (V2.19 — 16 cases, all Now, the first Drop's schedule) plus §52–§53 (V2.20 — 22 cases, 19 Now / 3 Next) plus §56 (V2.21 — 10 cases, 4 Now / 5 Next / 1 Build later). **Now** is the pass's KEEP plus §51–§53 + §56 (Now part); **Delete** is the pass's DELETE (applied in V2.17), and the pass's 271 DEFERs are split into **Next** and **Build later** by the rule below. **Now 317 · Next 163 · Build later 117 · Delete 6 = 603.** Section totals below sum to 603 because they include the 6 retired IDs; the file itself holds 597 cases.

**The rule.**
- **Now** — the first Drop end to end in staging: one restaurant → draft (Hunter) → video + smart-menu preview → two human gates → publish → Stripe checkout → QR redeem → pickup → cancel/refund/exception handling, with atomic money, the daily reconciliation, and the audit log. Build this first.
- **Next** — needed to run Drops 2–5 and repeat the validation once the first Drop proves out: operating more than one Drop (workspace, Drop list/search, video library, recurring Drops), showing more than one Drop to customers (homepage, cities/nearby, cuisine filter, Top 5, food-ready notifications), and handling small-volume reality (support queue, pickup at burst, operator throughput, messaging compliance for live sends).
- **Build later** — only matters at scale: 10+ restaurants, multiple zones, creators, chains, tax/1099, automated payouts, the MCP door, monitoring dashboards, alerts systems, team/coverage.
- **Delete** — the 6 retired IDs (UC-134, UC-173, UC-307, UC-328, UC-349, UC-554), dropped in V2.17 as duplicates or conflicts. Retired, never reused; listed here so the count closes to 555.

**Judgment calls.** The rule leaves a few placements on a line; each is named here so a later reader can move one without re-arguing the rule.
- §1 split: funnel stages and restaurant removal (UC-197–201) are Next — Drops 2–5 need the next few restaurants tracked and removable. Per-lead owner/backup (UC-202) and the restaurant self-serve create link (UC-203–204) are Build later — one operator needs no backup, and restaurants creating their own Drops is a scale feature.
- §12 sharing copy (UC-114, UC-115, UC-117, UC-118) is Next — Drops 2–5 need more than WhatsApp + SMS to reach customers. The Instagram in-app-browser Apple Pay friction stays a known issue, not a reason to push the copy to Build later.
- §17 AI promo copy (UC-172) and the promotion kit (UC-240) are Next — Hunter drafts Drops 2–5, and promo copy plus a kit for the restaurant are part of drafting, not scale.
- §20 split: the repeat / don't-repeat call (UC-218) is Next — it is the decision after Drops 2–5. Money by city (UC-217) is Build later — one city for Drops 2–5.
- §35: preview states (UC-350), take-menu-offline (UC-352), and PROMOTE-code stability (UC-357) are Next — the smart menu shows 3–4 Drops from Drop 2 on. Cache-friendly serving (UC-351) is Build later — a performance optimisation for rush volume, not needed at small volume. The 0-live alert (UC-353) goes with the alerts systems; resolution logging (UC-358) is P1 attribution logging; the GitHub workflow case (UC-365) is build process, not product, and the spec-home decision lives in the build plan.
- §37 (UC-391, UC-392, UC-432) is all Build later — cross-channel stitching, phone-change merge, and the consent-scope hierarchy are P1 refinements; one channel and Drop scope carry Drops 2–5.
- §42 UC-461 and UC-453 are Build later per the rule (monitoring dashboards, alerts systems), with the report's flags carried over: the locked UC-568 2-second budget is measured by the staging test-gate check meanwhile, and the first Drop's money safety net is the daily reconciliation (UC-442) plus webhook monitoring (UC-438).
- §44 UC-500 is Build later — the pickup identity check has no solution specified in the spec, a recorded gap rather than a buildable requirement, so it cannot sit in Next until a solution is picked.
- §48 UC-551 (Preparing), UC-556 (edges), UC-557 (scorecard) are Next — Preparing completes the kept five-state machine, the edges are real-customer reality in Drops 2–5, and the scorecard is how Drops 2–5 are judged.
- §13 UC-133 (exportable statements) is Build later — statements need settled history.
- §38 UC-403 (prep lead time) and UC-434 (five-state confirmation display) are Build later — refinements of the accept flow.
- §56 split: the greeting (UC-613), the 4:30 PM alert and its content (UC-617, UC-618), and the Indian-only bundle view (UC-621) are Now — they are the first Drop's customer surface: the page the Drop lives on and the text that announces it. The dish sub-chips (UC-614), the 16-city row and Explore TX (UC-615, UC-616), and the two shares (UC-619, UC-620) are Next — filtering, multi-city browsing, and sharing are how Drops 2–5 reach more customers. The Indian Restaurants shelf (UC-622) is Build later — a browse shelf, not a first-Drop need.

## Biggest changes in V2.0

1. **Numbering defect fixed.** V1.0 listed 194 entries under IDs UC-001–UC-190 because UC-121–UC-124 were used twice (§12 Distribution and §13 Financial operations, different titles). V2.0 renumbers §13–§18 sequentially so every entry has a unique ID: §13 = UC-125–UC-134, §14 = UC-135–UC-144, §15 = UC-145–UC-154, §16 = UC-155–UC-164, §17 = UC-165–UC-179, §18 = UC-180–UC-194. No title, priority, or section assignment changed in the renumbering. Old §13–§18 IDs are shown in brackets for traceability.
2. **City hierarchy is now a first-class structure (new §1 cases UC-195–UC-196).** State → Metroplex → Sub-city picklists everywhere; no free-text city. Default Texas › Dallas–Fort Worth Metroplex. Restaurants are viewed by city as Doing Business vs Pipeline.
3. **Pipeline is a real funnel, not a list (UC-197–UC-202).** Stages are To pitch → Pitched → Talking → Getting ready (renamed from "Onboarding" — Getting ready is STILL pipeline, NOT doing business; a restaurant moves to Doing Business only when its first Drop goes live). Stage changes are manual and explicit: tap the exact target stage, confirm "Move X from [old] to [new]?", nothing changes until confirmed, no auto-advance. Every lead carries last touch, next step, owner + backup.
4. **Restaurants can be removed (UC-199–UC-201).** V1.0 had no restaurant-delete use case at all (only UC-007 activate/deactivate and UC-049 flag unavailable, which only retag). Now: Remove from pipeline (outright delete, audit-logged, Undo); Remove from Studio (hide with history kept, private create link revoked, Restore); permanent delete only at 0 Drops / 0 orders / 0 media — mirroring the UC-059 Drop-delete rule.
5. **Demand is a top-level view (new §19, UC-212–UC-215).** Customer info is visible in the Studio as an operator read view (phone, order history, spend, pickup record, per-channel opt-ins, who to ping / never ping). Card details and home address are NEVER in the Studio.
6. **Money is a top-level view (new §20, UC-216–UC-218).** Per-Drop P&L with sell-through and a repeat / don't-repeat call, Money by city, settlement math on redeemed orders only.
7. **No single point of failure (new §21, UC-219–UC-223).** Coverage strip, named backup approver, 2-hour escalation to the backup, owner + waiting clock on every queue item, defined roles. Refunds, commission changes, and create-link revocation NEVER escalate — they wait for the operator by design.
8. **Mobile-first (new §22, UC-224–UC-226).** Bottom nav + raised New Drop FAB + More sheet, 44px+ tap targets, bottom-sheet forms, no sideways scrolling. Laptop is secondary. No native app is planned (see open item 7).
9. **Restaurant-created Drops (UC-203–UC-204).** Each restaurant has a unique private Drop-create link (no login); submissions land in Review & Queue marked Restaurant-created with the restaurant named, and can never publish directly.
10. **Commission field (UC-207–UC-208).** Per-restaurant % per redeemed order, shown on card/workspace/Money, change after confirmation flags confirmed Drops for re-confirmation. Form (%, flat, monthly) is still UNDECIDED — see open items.
11. **Bundle vs Non-bundle serves (UC-205–UC-206).** Required choice at Drop creation. Non-bundle = Serves 1. Bundle = Serves 2, 3, 4, 5, or 6+. Serving must show on the Drop.
12. **Distribution channels locked (UC-209–UC-211).** WhatsApp, Instagram, Facebook, SMS alert, RCS, TikTok, QR. NO email as a distribution channel. Creator distribution is optional per Drop (owned only / selected creators / area pick list).

## Biggest changes in V2.1 (Nov 8, 2026 — after V2.0)

1. **Full rebuild from scratch DECIDED — V2.0 open item 1 RESOLVED.** Mahendra confirmed Nov 8, 2026: new build from scratch is decided. The Oct 30 preservation rule (no starting over, no DB replacement, no parallel checkout, Studio V2 at /admin/v2/drops on the existing backend) is SUPERSEDED for this build. The V2.0 conflict is not carried forward as an open question. BUILD HOLD still stands: do not build anything until Mahendra says so.
2. **Tech stack is now specified (new §23, UC-227–UC-233).** Staging vs prod is part of the tech stack (two environments from day one: staging + prod, same code, different env vars); video is a MUST for every Drop; Studio login is by magic link (Supabase Auth, no passwords); secrets live in Vercel for the initial version, split by environment, never in code or GitHub; the domain stays hosted on IONOS with DNS pointing to Vercel. QR scaling is specified as an atomic database/redemption problem, not a QR-image problem.
3. **Every URL is tiny — non-negotiable (UC-233, plus revisions to UC-030, 110, 111, 112).** All generated URLs use short server-generated codes; no UUIDs, no long query strings, in anything a customer, creator, or restaurant has to type, tap, or share. Applies to Drop URLs, QR/pickup URLs, creator links, and restaurant create links. Attribution tags must use short tag values so they do not defeat the tiny-URL rule.
4. **Smart menu is now the restaurant page (UC-174 REVISED, UC-175–UC-176 REVISED, UC-234–UC-242 NEW).** One page per restaurant with **3 to 4 Drops** — this SUPERSEDES the old 2–3 target (V2.0 open item 5, now RESOLVED); 3 is the sweet spot, 4 is the max, never a filler Drop. Partner restaurants get TWO tiny URLs, unique to their location: (1) promote to users (the smart menu page), (2) add Drops with Mahendra's approval (private create link). Edge cases are locked as rules: 0 live Drops → "Next Drop coming soon" + alert signup, never an empty page; 1–2 Drops is fine; pickup window unmissable per Drop; sold-out Drops stay visible until the pickup window ends, then drop off; creator credit travels with the visit from a smart menu link, paid on pickup; a restaurant promotion kit is provided; one video per dish, reused across Drops.
5. **Mobile-first is MOST IMPORTANT, and no native app (UC-243 NEW; open item 7 RESOLVED as a locked case).** The Studio must be manageable from Mahendra's mobile phone; laptop is secondary. Mobile-first web Studio, installable via Add to Home Screen. A native app is built only if multiple daily operators, reliable push alerts, or offline QR scanning become required.
6. **Video and QR rules tightened (UC-021, 036, 038, 105 REVISED).** Video is a must, not optional — no Drop without a real video hero. QR tickets are generated on demand per paid order (short URL + unique code), redemption is atomic (scan once, mark redeemed, cannot be reused), and inventory decrements atomically so simultaneous purchases cannot oversell.

## Biggest changes in V2.2 (Nov 8, 2026 — top 10 critical scale gaps)

1. **Scale — first scale is now a section (new §24, UC-244–UC-253).** V2.1 was a strong single-Drop, single-operator, single-zone spec. It breaks at scale in four places first: (1) operator throughput, (2) creator and restaurant payouts, (3) pickup operations at a burst, (4) trust and compliance when strangers, not friends, are on both sides. §24 adds the 10 cases needed *before* 10 restaurants / multiple zones / 10+ creators / 10 Drops in one day: batch review + operator throughput (UC-244), automated creator payout (UC-245), restaurant settlement with reconciliation and hold (UC-246), pickup dashboard + unclaimed orders (UC-247), creator fraud / attribution conflicts (UC-248), support queue (UC-249), messaging compliance + fatigue (UC-250), chain/group model (UC-251), creator self-service (UC-252), payout identity + tax / 1099 (UC-253). Priorities from the scale-gaps report: 7 × P0, 2 × P1, 1 × P1 (UC-251, P0 the moment a chain is the second restaurant).
2. **Stripe Connect is the DECIDED payout rail (Nov 8, 2026 — Mahendra is using Stripe Connect).** Connect is the rail for BOTH creator payouts (UC-245) and restaurant settlement (UC-246) and for tax/1099 handling (UC-253): Connect onboarding, bank accounts, split payouts, and tax-form / 1099 reporting run through Connect, not through ad-hoc Zelle at scale. This resolves the payout-rail decision from the scale-gaps report. What Connect does NOT solve — and still needs DealsPro rules on top: payout schedule/timing, minimum payout, refund/chargeback holds, attribution rules, and support. Those remain open decisions (see open item 14).
3. **Payouts are now systems, not promises.** V2.1 *calculated* creator share and restaurant owed (UC-216–217, UC-125–134) without ever disbursing them. V2.2 adds the disbursement: accrued creator share batches on a fixed schedule via Stripe Connect with per-order receipts (UC-245); restaurant settlement per period with a payout reference, bank/Stripe reconciliation, and an automatic hold when an unresolved refund/chargeback exceeds a threshold or payout details are unverified (UC-246); payout blocked until tax/identity is on file (UC-253).
4. **Pickup and support get operational systems, not just alerts.** UC-247 adds a live pickup queue (order, customer first name + phone last digits, quantity, ready/not-ready, redeemed/not-redeemed, time remaining), a Ready flow, redeem-by-reference when a QR will not scan, per-window restaurant capacity, and unclaimed-order resolution (refund / hold for late pickup / forfeit per stated policy — never silent). UC-249 adds a support ticket queue tied to order/Drop/restaurant, with one-tap resolutions for the five recurring problems and a customer-facing help page.
5. **Trust at scale is explicit.** UC-248 flags creator self-dealing (own phone/device buying through own link), same-phone/device bursts, and links used only by never-picked-up orders; flagged orders are held from creator payout pending review, and the creator is not paid on their own purchases. Attribution conflicts resolve by one stated rule (proposed: last tagged link before purchase wins; smart-menu visit tag wins over a later untagged visit — decision still open; **RESOLVED in V2.7: last-tagged-link-wins LOCKED**). UC-250 adds messaging compliance: 10DLC / WhatsApp Business registration status shown in Studio, a blocked sender halts marketing sends automatically, every send is labelled transactional vs marketing and audited with recipient, channel, Drop, consent basis, template, and delivery status, and sends are blocked with no opt-in, STOP on file, or a repeat marketing message inside the stated window.

## Biggest changes in V2.3 (Nov 8, 2026 — cancel policy, PROPOSED)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided.** Mahendra has NOT yet tapped "agree" on this policy. Every case in §25 is written as the proposed policy from the Nov 8 conversation, and must be treated as a proposal until he agrees it. Do not build §25 as locked policy, and do not present it to restaurants or customers as agreed terms.

1. **Cancel policy is now a full policy, not a set of generic cases (new §25, UC-254–UC-269).** §18 (UC-180–UC-194) already had generic cancellation/refund mechanics; §25 is the policy layer on top of them: a Ready-based cancel cutoff, refund timing, whole-Drop cancels, no-show rules, money rules, the Ready mechanism, transactional messaging, and pickup-issue handling. §18 cases are unchanged; §25 refines them.
2. **The Ready timestamp is the cancel cutoff.** Before the pickup window starts, or during the window before the restaurant marks the order Ready: full refund, automatic. After Ready, before pickup: no self-serve cancel — support case, operator decides. After pickup: no refund except a real issue (wrong item, bad food, missing item). The whole policy hangs on one timestamp, which is why the Ready mechanism is specified in the same section (UC-264–UC-266).
3. **Restaurant marks Ready with no login and no app (UC-264–UC-266).** A private pickup link by text/WhatsApp, an order list, a big Ready button per order plus Ready All, and an automatic "ready for pickup" text to the customer. Backups: reply READY to the pickup text, or the Ready button in the Studio Orders & Pickup board. If a restaurant never marks Ready, the end of the pickup window is the cutoff and the restaurant is flagged.
4. **Whole-Drop cancels and no-shows have stated rules, not ad-hoc decisions.** Restaurant cancels a Drop after payment, or DealsPro cancels one: every customer gets a full refund automatically plus a message. A restaurant Drop-cancel earns a reliability strike — two strikes = probation, no new Drops until the operator reviews. No-show: grace to the end of the window, one reminder, then unclaimed with no refund; the operator may override once as a goodwill refund, as an exception, not a rule.
5. **Money rules are explicit (UC-262).** Refunds always go to the original card, never cash/credit by default; Stripe processing fees on a refunded order are not charged to the customer; creator and restaurant are never paid for a cancelled/refunded order — commission counts only on picked-up orders. Every cancel, refund, and override is audit-logged with who did it and why.
6. **Numbering note.** V2.3 assigns UC-254–UC-269 to cancel policy. The scale-gaps report had *proposed* UC-254–UC-266 as IDs for its later-scale gaps — those IDs were never adopted, and are superseded by this assignment. If the later-scale gaps are adopted later, they take the next free numbers (UC-270+).

## Biggest changes in V2.4 (Nov 8, 2026 — claim → restaurant confirm → Ready, PROPOSED)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided.** Mahendra has NOT yet agreed this flow. Every case in §26 is written as the proposed flow from the Nov 8 conversation, and must be treated as a proposal until he agrees it. Do not build §26 as locked policy, and do not present it to restaurants or customers as agreed terms.

1. **A claim is not an accepted order until the restaurant says so (new §26, UC-270–UC-282).** The moment a user claims/pays, the claim goes to the restaurant immediately (UC-270) and the order sits in "Claimed — awaiting restaurant confirmation" (UC-271) until the restaurant explicitly accepts it (UC-272). Silence is never acceptance. The customer is told the restaurant has accepted (UC-274) and later that the order is Ready (UC-275, reusing the UC-264/265 Ready mechanism) — each step is a message, not a status the customer has to go and check.
2. **The 2–3 hour pickup window is shown, counted down, and never left silent (UC-276–UC-282).** The customer always sees the exact window (date + start/end time, big type), a live status timeline (Claimed → Accepted → Ready → Picked up), and a countdown / time remaining. If the restaurant is late to accept or late to mark Ready, the customer gets a delay message with a new time — never silence. If the restaurant does not accept within the defined window, the order escalates to the operator and the customer is told what is happening.
3. **Accept deadline is TO DECIDE (open item 17).** §26 requires a defined accept window and an escalation on missing it, but does not invent the number — how long a restaurant has to accept before the order escalates (and the customer is told) is still Mahendra's call. Do not hard-code a deadline into the build until he sets it.
4. **No fake "ready".** Ready exists only when the restaurant actually marks it (UC-282, UC-264/265). The system never auto-marks an order Ready at the window start, on a timer, or because the restaurant opened the pickup link.

## Biggest changes in V2.5 (Nov 8, 2026 — reconciliation with the Studio catalog, at Mahendra's instruction "You reconcile the file")

**Status: the file is now the single source of truth at 369 cases.** The Studio catalog (v2.3, 369 cases, UC-001–UC-369) and this file (now V2.5, 369 cases, UC-001–UC-369) agree on the TOTAL. They do NOT agree on numbering: the Studio renumbers everything sequentially from UC-001, while this file keeps its V1.0-derived IDs plus appended blocks (UC-195–UC-369). The two catalogs must never be compared ID-to-ID — compare by section and by content, using the Studio-source mapping on every V2.5 case. The Studio artifact was NOT edited in this reconciliation; a future Studio rebuild must be driven from this file.

1. **87 cases added, none dropped, none renumbered (new §§27–35, UC-283–UC-369).** File UC-001–UC-282 (§§1–26) are byte-identical to V2.4. The 87 are: 65 scale cases from Studio §§25–32, carried over as file §§27–34 (§27 Stripe Connect 11, §28 throughput 8, §29 pickup at burst 8, §30 creator/fraud/attribution 8, §31 support 8, §32 messaging compliance 8, §33 chains 7, §34 tax/1099 7), plus 22 Studio detail cases from Studio §§19–24 as file §35. Each V2.5 case names its Studio source ID so any case can be checked back against the Studio COV array.
2. **Studio §33 was NOT re-added — it duplicates file §25/§26.** Studio §33 (Studio UC-343–UC-369, 27 cases) covers the same cancel / Ready / claim-accept ground as file §25 (UC-254–UC-269) and §26 (UC-270–UC-282). A case-by-case mapping is in the "Reconciliation note" after §35; every Studio §33 case maps to a file case. Re-adding them would have specified the same policy twice in different words. File §25/§26 remain the policy layer, and the Studio's §33 numbering does NOT carry over — in this file UC-343–UC-369 are §34/§35 cases. The Studio's "the agreed policy" label is WRONG: the cancel policy and claim-confirm flow are still PROPOSED (open items 15 and 17).
3. **§§27–34 expand file §24, they do not replace it.** File §24 (UC-244–UC-253) states each scale gap once; §§27–34 state the operating detail the Studio built for each gap (payout schedule/hold/minimum, batch-review rules, pickup board, creator self-service, support tickets, 10DLC, chains, 1099). Read §24 for the decision and §§27–34 for the mechanism.

(b) Attribution: UC-313 (Studio) visit-opener-wins vs UC-248 (file) last-tagged-link-wins — **RESOLVED in V2.7: last-tagged-link-wins LOCKED, visit-opener-wins REJECTED**. (c) Review-time target: UC-299 (Studio) ≤5-minute average vs UC-244 (file) median flags at 30 min (first Drop) / 10 min (repeat). (d) Payout values: UC-287/UC-289 (Studio) Fridays, 2-day hold, $10 minimum vs UC-245/UC-246 (file) weekly proposed, schedule/minimum/hold to confirm. All four sit under open item 14. A fifth difference is noted, not a conflict: UC-307's 30-minute post-window grace refines UC-261's grace-to-window-end wording; grace length is PROPOSED (open item 15).

They remain deferred decisions in the scale-gaps report, not missing cases.

The remaining reconciliation work is for a future Studio rebuild to be driven from this file (including fixing the Studio's stale §1–§18 section-start labels and its "agreed policy" wording) — not a file task.

## Biggest changes in V2.6 (Nov 8, 2026 — top-10 audit gaps, at Mahendra's instruction)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided.** Every case in §§36–40 is written from the Nov 8, 2026 conversation working the top 10 gaps from the end-to-end walkthrough gap audit (`end-to-end-walkthrough-gap-audit.md`, 110 gaps G-01–G-110 + 15 contradictions C-1–C-15). Mahendra has NOT yet agreed §§36–40 as locked policy. Do not build them as locked, and do not present them to restaurants or customers as agreed terms, until he agrees them. The numbers inside them are proposals unless marked as Mahendra's stated number.

1. **The DealsPro website and homepage finally have a spec (§36, UC-370–UC-381).** Audit gap #1: the thing customers land on had no use case at all. §36 specifies the customer-facing website as a whole (separate from the Studio, which is operator-facing), and the homepage layout as a written spec — city-scoped ("This week in Frisco" style), live Drops now, coming-soon Drops, restaurants by city/zone, new vs returning visitor, search/browse, the 0-live-Drops empty state, sold-out display, and routing to a restaurant smart menu vs a single Drop page. The homepage spec is M2's expected design, as Mahendra asked for it — it is a proposal for him to correct, not a lock.
2. **An accept deadline now exists, and the operator controls the dates with a 48-hour minimum lead (§38, UC-394–UC-403).** Audit gap #2. Mahendra's reasoning, recorded verbatim in substance: without a deadline there is no time to market the Drop, and customers need to be able to pick up ahead of time. Two different deadlines are now separate cases, not one blurred rule: (a) the **claim-accept deadline** — how long a restaurant has to accept a paid claim (PROPOSED default: 2 hours from claim, and no later than 30 minutes before the pickup window opens if the claim arrives late — TO CONFIRM, open item 18); (b) the **48-hour lead** — a pickup window must start at least 48 hours after publish, so there is time to market (Mahendra's stated number, Nov 8, 2026 — PROPOSED as a lock until he confirms it, open item 18; **LOCKED in V2.7 — Mahendra picked 48 hours minimum on Nov 8, 2026 — see "Biggest changes in V2.7"**). The deadline field must be present in BOTH create flows (operator-created and restaurant-created), and a Drop cannot publish with a window less than 48 hours out unless the operator explicitly overrides with a reason, audit-logged. This answers open item 17's "accept deadline TO DECIDE" with a proposed number — the item stays open until Mahendra confirms the number.
3. **Customer identity and opt-in recognition is now a system, not an outline (§37, UC-382–UC-393).** Audit gaps #3 and #10 — the big one. Research first, then cases (research note in Traceability, below). Phone is identity: the customer enters their phone number at checkout, no login, no password, no account. The system looks the number up server-side, recognises a returning phone, pre-fills their name, shows past orders, and NEVER asks opt-in again if they already opted in on that channel — the opt-in check happens server-side before every ask, at Drop level and on the restaurant page alike. The QR/ticket still goes to the phone number they enter, even if they are already opted in. Opt-in is stored per phone + channel (SMS ≠ WhatsApp ≠ RCS), with source, timestamp, and disclosure version — the Twilio Consent Management API model. OTP verification of phone ownership on first claim is PROPOSED, not locked (open item 19).
4. **Claim-to-payment race: a per-customer cap exists now, and the reservation hold is specified (§39, UC-404–UC-409).** Audit gap #6. **Correction to Mahendra's recollection, recorded honestly:** he said a 3-or-4 cap "was added in the past" — a full search of this catalog found NO per-customer cap case and NO reservation-hold case anywhere (UC-105 is atomic decrement at payment only; UC-249 lists "I paid but the Drop says sold out" as a support problem, not a prevention). So the cap is added here as NEW, not cross-referenced as existing. Proposed default cap: **3 units per phone per Drop** (Mahendra said 3 or 4 — 3 is the proposed default, TO CONFIRM, open item 20). Proposed reservation hold: **10 minutes** while the customer pays (the scale report's proposal — TO CONFIRM, open item 20). Two buyers cannot both pay for the last unit: the second is told sold out before payment completes and is never charged.
5. **Restaurant confirmation is now a concrete mechanism, not a human gate with no channel (§38, UC-399–UC-402, refining §26).** Audit gap #7. Channel: text/WhatsApp to the restaurant contact + the live pickup queue (UC-247) + the Studio board. Record format: who confirmed, when, by which channel, per order. Decline and no-response go to UC-273/UC-281 with a decline-reason taxonomy. Offer confirmation (the restaurant agreeing the exact offer, UC-067) gets its own deadline rule in the same section — a Drop whose offer is not confirmed by its scheduled publish time does not publish.
6. **Operator alerts now have a delivery channel (§40, UC-410–UC-417).** Audit gap #8. In-Studio-only alerts are not enough for a mobile-first operator. Alerts go by SMS/WhatsApp to the operator's phone (push via mobile web is secondary only — unreliable on iOS), with the backup after the 2-hour escalation, and an alert is not cleared until the item is acted on. Alert channels and quiet hours are TO CONFIRM (open item 21).

## Biggest changes in V2.7 (Nov 8, 2026 — two locks + Studio §41 reconciliation, at Mahendra's instruction)

See open item 22 for the numbers still to confirm, and open item 23 for the readiness items this section closes or partly closes.

Because they are inserted into §§37–39 by topic, UC-430–UC-434 appear in file order before UC-410–UC-429 — IDs remain unique and sequential 1–464 by value, not by file position.

## Biggest changes in V2.9 (Nov 8, 2026 — dynamic cuisine filter + hero-dish discovery, at Mahendra's instruction "add that to the spec by cuisine and make it dynamic if available show otherwise no — I mean if live drop show otherwise no")

**Status: §43 is NEW and PROPOSED as a section, not locked** — Mahendra asked for the cuisine filter to be dynamic, so the rule in UC-465 (show a cuisine chip only when a live Drop in that cuisine exists in the selected city) is the decision; the rest of §43 is M2's proposed mechanism for it. Do not build §43 as locked policy, and do not present the cuisine list or the hero-dish tag as agreed terms, until he confirms them (open item 24).

1. **A cuisine chip exists only while a live Drop in that cuisine exists (new §43, UC-465–UC-478, 14 cases; UC-465 is P0, the rest P1).** The rule is Mahendra's, stated exactly: if there is a live Drop in a cuisine in the selected city, show that cuisine; if there is no live Drop, do not show it — not greyed out, not with a 0 count, not as a dead chip. Coming Soon Drops do not count as live (UC-473), a sold-out Drop stops counting the moment it sells out and an ended Drop stops counting when its window ends (UC-476), and a city with no live Drops at all shows no cuisine chips (UC-474). The filter applies everywhere it appears — homepage, smart-menu / restaurant list, and search/browse — and it is re-computed when the city changes (UC-468), so a cuisine that is live in Frisco and not live in Plano appears in Frisco and disappears in Plano.
2. **Cuisine is a controlled property, not free text (UC-466).** The list is Indian, Pizza, Thai, Mexican, Chinese, Other — set at restaurant onboarding and per dish/video, with dish-level cuisine winning over restaurant-level cuisine when a restaurant serves two cuisines (UC-475). Adding a new cuisine to the list is an operator decision, not a restaurant free-text entry.
3. **Hero dish is a label, not a Drop type (UC-469).** One hero dish per restaurant, operator-set and changeable — the restaurant's famous dish, marked with a small tag on the Drop page and smart menu. It is a normal Drop with a tag on it, so it follows every existing Drop rule (48-hour lead, video, claim, QR, pickup) and needs no new Drop type, no new screen, and no UI redesign — the cuisine filter and the hero tag are additions to the existing homepage/smart-menu/Drop surfaces (§36), not new surfaces.
4. **The filter never shows an empty list (UC-470/UC-471).** Filtering by cuisine on the restaurant list hides restaurants with no live Drop in that cuisine, even if they have a Coming Soon Drop; a search with no live results in that cuisine shows the UC-374 0-live empty state with alert signup, never an empty list. A cuisine change after publish is a material change and requires re-confirmation, like a price change (UC-472, UC-208 auto-void rule).

## Biggest changes in V2.10 (Nov 8, 2026 — restaurant-direct cancel/change, at Mahendra's instruction "this is very critical")

**Status: §25's new model is PROPOSED, not locked** — Mahendra instructed this change on Nov 8, 2026 ("customer deals with the restaurant directly after placing the order; send the restaurant number to call for any changes; let the restaurant cancel the order if they want; DealsPro syncs back"), and called it very critical. The instruction is recorded as the new model; it is NOT recorded as a lock/agreement of §25 as a whole — §25 stays PROPOSED (open item 15) until he taps agree on the policy, including this model. Do not build §25 as locked policy, and do not present it to restaurants or customers as agreed terms, until he agrees it.

1. **The operator is out of the middle of cancels — the restaurant decides, DealsPro records (new §25 extension, UC-479–UC-488, 10 cases, all P0).** After claim/payment, the customer deals with the restaurant directly for any change or cancel (UC-479–UC-480): the confirmation text and the order page show the restaurant's published/order phone number, the order code, and the pickup window, with the instruction "For any changes, call the restaurant directly at [number], quote order [code]". The restaurant decides yes/no — it is their food and their kitchen. A change is a cancel plus a new claim if units remain; there are no partial changes and no swaps (UC-481).
2. **A phone call is never the system of record — the restaurant's tap is (UC-482).** The restaurant records its decision ONLY by tapping Cancel on its private pickup link (UC-264) or replying CANCEL [code] to the pickup text. No tap, no refund, no sync: a verbal "yeah, cancelled" on the phone, with no tap, leaves the order live, the QR valid, and the money unmoved. The tap triggers, atomically: QR invalidated instantly, inventory restored, refund issued automatically to the original card (UC-262 money rules, 5–10 business days), and the customer gets a text "Cancelled, refund on its way" (UC-487).
3. **Three windows, one rule each (revised UC-255/UC-256/UC-261).** (a) Before the window opens: restaurant cancels in-system, full refund automatic, no operator needed. (b) During the window, before Ready: same, full refund automatic *if the restaurant agrees*; if the restaurant says no on the phone, the order stays live and the restaurant tells the customer — DealsPro does not override a restaurant's no. (c) After the window, didn't pick up (no-show): default NO refund (food was made and held); the restaurant — not the operator — may override once as a goodwill refund by tapping "Refund as goodwill" with a reason, audit-logged; if the restaurant says "too late" on the phone, nothing happens in DealsPro and the order closes as unclaimed.
4. **Ready locks the cancel — for the restaurant too (revised UC-257, cross-ref UC-275).** After the restaurant marks an order Ready, there is no restaurant self-serve cancel either: it becomes a support case and the operator decides, because the food is made. Ready is the lock for both sides, not only for the customer.
5. **The old model is superseded, not deleted.** The V2.3 operator-decides / customer-self-serve-cancel model (customer cancels from the order page or by replying CANCEL; operator decides after Ready; operator overrides a no-show once as goodwill) is SUPERSEDED by the restaurant-direct model. Each revised case (UC-254–UC-257, UC-261, UC-263, UC-267) keeps its old text in a short superseded note, so the change is traceable and reversible if Mahendra changes his mind. §18 (UC-180–UC-194) generic cancellation/refund mechanics are unchanged; §25 remains the policy layer on top of them.
6. **Privacy and audit follow the money (UC-485, UC-488, revised UC-263/UC-449).** The restaurant phone shown to customers is the published/order number, never the owner's personal mobile, and it appears only on the order page and the confirmation text — never on the public Drop page. Every restaurant cancel, refund, and goodwill override is audit-logged (who, when, order, reason, before/after) under §42 (UC-449/UC-450), appears in the Money dashboard and the daily reconciliation (UC-441/UC-442), and is visible to the operator in the Studio (UC-488) — so a dispute a month later ("the restaurant said they'd cancel") is answered from the tap record, not from a phone call nobody recorded.

7. **Pickup is now a provable fact, and the customer is reminded before and during the window (new §44, UC-489–UC-502, 14 cases; pickup state and unclaimed P0, reminder timing P1).** Every order has one server-side pickup state — Claimed → Accepted → Ready → Picked up / Unclaimed (UC-489) — recorded at the QR scan (UC-490) or the manual ticket-code fallback (UC-491) with timestamp and redeemer, and a picked-up order is terminal: never re-redeemed, cancelled, or refunded as unclaimed (UC-502). Reminders are transactional texts on the channel the customer ordered from: 24 hours before (UC-492), 2 hours before (UC-493), at window start (UC-494), at the midpoint if not yet picked up (UC-495), and a final reminder 30 minutes before the window ends if not yet picked up (UC-496) — each with restaurant, dish, quantity, window, address, order code, and the restaurant phone number, and each suppressed after pickup/cancel/refund (UC-498). At window end an unpicked-up order is automatically marked Unclaimed with one missed-pickup text, a restaurant summary, and a P0 operator alert (UC-497) — **which conflicts with UC-307's 30-minute grace + UC-308's operator decision; see open item 26, do not build both.** Reminder delivery is logged and a failed reminder escalates (UC-499). The pickup identity check — a forwarded QR screenshot collecting the order — is recorded as still open with no solution invented (UC-500, readiness R-05). The operator board shows pickup state, reminder history, and time remaining with a "not yet picked up" filter (UC-501).

**Parallel-work conflict, recorded:** a §45 "Restaurant order management" framing (UC-489–UC-502, restaurant no-login order view with tap-to-call, accept/decline, Ready, cancel, redeem) was written into this file's header, summary, Biggest-changes item 7, and audit table by another agent in the same hour as §44, but no §45 cases were added to the catalog body — the IDs UC-489–UC-502 are the §44 cases above. The framing has been replaced here by the §44 description that matches the actual cases. If the §45 restaurant order-management view is wanted, it needs its own new IDs after UC-502, written as cases, not as framing — do not renumber §44 to make room for it.


## Biggest changes in V2.11 (Nov 10, 2026 — Top 5 in your area this week + interactive matcher, at Mahendra's instruction "add it to the studio and the specs as this as a requirement and show me how it shows in the real time ranking")

**Status: §46 is NEW and PROPOSED as a section, not locked** — Mahendra asked for the top 3 or 5 Drops in the customer's area like Netflix for this week, ranked in real time, with a 2-question interactive matcher that shows the one best Drop in the local area. The ranking weights, the recompute latency, the "Selling fast" threshold, the Top 5 vs Top 3 mobile question, and the matcher wording are all M2's proposals until he confirms them (open item 27). Do not build §46 as locked policy, and do not present any ranking number as agreed, until he confirms them.

1. **The homepage gets a Netflix-style shelf — Top 5 in [city] this week (new §46, UC-516–UC-529, 14 cases; shelf, ranking, card, sold-out, and matcher trigger P0, preference memory / "Because you picked X" / tie-breaks P1).** The customer homepage (§36) opens with at most 5 live Drops in the selected city, 3 visible on mobile first (UC-516), each card showing video hero, dish, restaurant, Serves, total price with no fee surprise, pickup window, and units left (UC-519). A sold-out Drop stays visible until its window ends, then drops off and the next-ranked live Drop moves up (UC-520). With fewer than 3 live Drops the shelf shows what exists and invents nothing; with 0 live Drops there is no shelf at all, only the UC-374 empty state (UC-521).
2. **Ranking is earned, never paid (UC-517–UC-518, UC-528–UC-529).** The order is computed server-side from four real signals — sellout velocity (PROPOSED 40%), pickup reliability (PROPOSED 30%), repeat-buyer rate (PROPOSED 20%), recency (PROPOSED 10%) — with no paid placement, no bidding, no sponsored slot, and no manual reorder. It re-computes on every signal-changing event (claim, pickup, missed pickup, repeat purchase, new Drop live, window end) with a PROPOSED latency under 60 seconds, and every ranking change is audit-logged with the triggering event. No badge is ever faked: "Selling fast" only above a PROPOSED threshold (50% claimed in the first 24 hours live), "Only X left" is live inventory, "Sold out" is live inventory state. Tie-breaks go to the earlier pickup window, then the earlier go-live (UC-529).
3. **The matcher is the fallback, not the front door (UC-522–UC-525).** Under the Top 5, "Not seeing what you're craving?" opens exactly two questions — Q1 cuisine/mood (Indian, Pizza, Thai, Mexican, Chinese, Surprise me), Q2 Serves (Just me / 2 / Family 4+) + pickup day (Tonight / Friday) — and shows ONE best live Drop with a plain-language reason why. Preferences are remembered by phone (UC-524) and never re-asked unless the customer taps Change. If no live Drop fits, the matcher says so honestly, shows the closest match only if one exists and labels it as such, and offers a cuisine+city alert signup (UC-525). After a purchase, a "Because you picked X" row shows the Top 3 for that customer from their remembered preference (UC-526).
4. **The operator sees why each Drop ranks where it does (UC-527).** The Studio shows, per city, the live Top 5 with each Drop's four signal values, the Drops just outside the Top 5 (#6–#8, "next up"), and the Drops that cannot rank with the reason each is out — plus a preview of exactly the shelf a customer in that city sees. The view is read-only for ranking: a Drop's rank changes only when its signals change, never by dragging it.
5. **Numbering note — the §45 gap is preserved, not closed.** UC-503–UC-515 remain RESERVED for the unmerged §45 draft (restaurant order management, `section-45-draft-uc-503-515.md`). This version adds UC-516–UC-529 only, so the file now holds 516 counted cases with highest ID UC-529 and a 13-ID gap at UC-503–UC-515. Do not present 516 as continuous, do not renumber §45 or §46 to close the gap, and do not merge §45 from this version — §45 still needs Mahendra's yes.

## Biggest changes in V2.12 (Nov 10, 2026 — hero-dish tag placement, at Mahendra's instruction "I like the hero dish tags on top of the picture next to the drop red tag")

**Status: the PLACEMENT is Mahendra's decision (Nov 10, 2026); the tag WORDING is still PROPOSED** — the tag sits as an overlay on top of the hero picture/video, immediately next to the red DROP badge, on every surface where a hero-dish Drop appears. The wording "Hero Dish" (vs "★ Hero Dish") was never locked — it is proposed, TO CONFIRM (open item 24e). The red DROP badge is unchanged and uncovered (red-accent lock, Oct 26, 2026).

## Biggest changes in V2.13 (Nov 15, 2026 — food-ready notifications & customer check-in, at Mahendra's instruction "add these use cases to the studio and file")

**Status: §47 is NEW and PROPOSED as a section, not locked** — Mahendra asked how to make pickup seamless — customers get a message when food is ready, restaurants see the order and push a button to send it — and asked for a deep search first. The research (Toast/Square/DoorDash/Uber Eats verified live Nov 15, 2026) found his idea IS the industry default: one staff tap on Ready sends one message. The section specifies that tap plus the two layers around it that the research ranked highest. The numbers inside it (SMS-first, 2+1 message cap, quiet hours, batch-confirm wording) are M2's proposals until he confirms them (open item 28). Do not build §47 as locked policy, and do not present any of its numbers as agreed, until he confirms them.

1. **One-tap batch-ready messaging (new §47, UC-530–UC-538, 9 cases; per-order + batch Ready, confirm screen, idempotency, template, channel order, delivery receipts, no-premature-ready rule, ready board).** The restaurant's private pickup-link view (UC-509 in the unmerged §45 draft — the tap §47 assumes) marks one order or a whole batch Ready; the status change sends one personal ready message per order (SMS first, Twilio + A2P 10DLC, ~$1.18–$1.33 per 100 ready texts, verified live; WhatsApp utility template as fallback, price unverified). A batch send shows a confirm screen first ('Send ready to N customers?'), Ready is idempotent (one ready message per order per Drop), the template is ≤160 characters with no offers, delivery receipts are tracked with one auto-retry on the other channel, and Ready may only be tapped when the food is physically bagged and staged — a 'ready' sent before the food is bagged is a trust failure (research: customers told 'ready' then waiting 35–45 minutes for cold food). The in-store ready board (Preparing → Ready) is the always-on fallback so a failed text never strands an order.
2. **Two-way check-in (UC-539–UC-542, 4 cases).** The confirmation message carries a no-login check-in link ('On my way' / 'I'm here'); a tap marks the order Arriving/Here and re-sorts the counter queue by arrival, not order time — staff work by who's arriving next, and the kitchen bags just in time. Check-in is a prioritisation signal only, never a precondition — food is staged by batch whether or not the customer taps anything. An optional auto-reply ('We're bagging yours now') counts inside the message cap.
3. **Status page + message rules (UC-543–UC-547, 5 cases).** A no-login status page shows only Preparing → Ready, never a minute ETA. At most 2 messages per order per Drop (confirmation + ready) plus at most 1 reminder; ready alerts are transactional on checkout-phone consent, marketing opt-in stays separate and unchecked by default; no marketing 9:00 PM–9:00 AM local, STOP/HELP honored, one consistent sender. The operator sees per-order message state (sent/delivered/failed) on the Studio board.
4. **What §47 deliberately does NOT add.** No geofencing / live ETA (needs an app/SDK and location sharing — incompatible with no-app + phone-as-identity); no ML/AI ready-time prediction (needs per-stage inputs + history the platform does not have); RCS as a primary channel (dollar pricing unverified, needs SMS fallback anyway — later upgrade only).
5. **Numbering note — the §45 gap is preserved, not closed.** UC-503–UC-515 remain RESERVED for the unmerged §45 draft. This version adds UC-530–UC-547 only, so the file now holds 534 counted cases with highest ID UC-547 and a 13-ID gap at UC-503–UC-515. Do not present 534 as continuous, do not renumber §45, §46, or §47 to close the gap, and do not merge §45 from this version — §45 still needs Mahendra's yes. **Dependency:** UC-530/UC-531 are triggered by the UC-509 Ready tap, which lives in the unmerged §45 draft — see open item 28f.

## Biggest changes in V2.14 (Nov 16, 2026 — five-step pickup timeline, at Mahendra's instruction "I like the 5 step process claim to picked up add it the file studio use card to build")

**Status: §48 is NEW and PROPOSED as a section, not locked** — Mahendra liked the 5-step process from the Apple-style seamless-pickup research (Claimed → Accepted → Preparing → Ready → Picked Up) and asked for it to be added to the file as a Studio use case to build. The five states unify §26 (Claim → Accept), §44 (pickup state + reminders), and §47 (Ready messaging) into one canonical state machine — they do not replace those sections. The numbers inside it (scorecard targets, grace length) are M2's proposals until he confirms them (open item 29). Do not build §48 as locked policy, and do not present any of its numbers as agreed, until he confirms them.

1. **One state machine for every order (new §48, UC-548–UC-557, 10 cases; state machine + customer timeline + restaurant timeline + Preparing + Ready + Picked Up + Unclaimed + audit + edge cases + scorecard).** Every paid order has exactly one server-side pickup state with timestamps; states move only on real events, never on a timer, never skipped; Picked Up is terminal. The customer sees the same five states on one no-login page; the restaurant sees the same five states on its private pickup-link page. Ready is tap-only with SMS + single-use QR + 3-character code, bag behind the counter. Unclaimed adopts the research-recommended 30-minute grace as the PROPOSED rule — which conflicts with UC-497's window-end marking; see UC-554 and open item 29d, do not build both.
2. **What §48 deliberately does NOT add.** No POS integration, no app, no full-menu sync, no geofencing, no minute ETA, no photo ID — the timeline works on SMS + one web page, per the standing no-POS rule.
3. **Numbering note — the §45 gap is preserved, not closed.** UC-503–UC-515 remain RESERVED for the unmerged §45 draft. This version adds UC-548–UC-557 only, so the file now holds 544 counted cases with highest ID UC-557 and a 13-ID gap at UC-503–UC-515. Do not present 544 as continuous, do not renumber §45, §46, §47, or §48 to close the gap, and do not merge §45 from this version — §45 still needs Mahendra's yes.

## Biggest changes in V2.19 (Nov 23, 2026 — Drop timing, at Mahendra's instruction "add this requirements to the living spec")

**Status: 16 cases added, 0 revised, 0 dropped** — the Drop timing playbook from the Nov 22–23, 2026 deep research (`dealspro-drop-timing-playbook` page; report `~/workspace/research_notes/dealspro-drop-timing-playbook-20261123-0541/report.md`) is now §51 (UC-569–UC-584), all PROPOSED.

1. **The ranked schedules.** Option C Hybrid first (UC-569: publish 2 days ahead, claims open, final-call SMS 1:30 PM, hard cut-off 3 PM, prep 3–5:30 PM, pickup 6–8 PM + 15-min grace); Option A same-day second (UC-572); Option B 24-hour cut-off for complex dishes only (UC-573). The claim window is roughly one day of effective decision time (UC-574) — not one hour (reactance) and not one week.
2. **The early-lock correction.** The hypothesis is PARTIALLY SUPPORTED: dinner is a same-day decision, but the decision window is broad and late (roughly 2–5 PM), not a sharp 2–3 PM hour — a 3 PM cut-off alone would miss the peak deciders, which is why the Hybrid opens claims two days early.
3. **Two conflicts, handled in the open, not hidden.** (a) Option A conflicts with the LOCKED 48-hour lead (open item 18b) — it runs only as a time-boxed second test via the locked operator override (UC-396). (b) UC-577 (15-minute grace) is the proposed single unclaimed rule that would settle open items 26(b) and 29(d) and revise UC-497 — do not build both rules.
4. **After cut-off.** Full refund until cut-off, no refund after (UC-575); QR transfer at no charge (UC-576); no-show after grace — no refund, no credit, no exchange (UC-578); restaurant fault — full refund (UC-579). Food safety: hold hot at ≥135°F, 4-hour danger-zone clock (UC-580). SMS cadence ≤3 texts per Drop (UC-581), in tension with UC-250's 1/week marketing cap — open item 14(d) decides. Operating rules: real claimed count live (UC-582); rules stated in the first SMS before payment (UC-583). Batch-prep window 3 hours on three conditions (UC-584) — verify in the first restaurant's kitchen.
5. **Numbering note — counts.** This version adds 16 cases, so the file now holds 565 counted cases with highest ID UC-584, a 13-ID gap at UC-503–UC-515, and 6 retired IDs (UC-134, UC-173, UC-307, UC-328, UC-349, UC-554). The Nov 22 plan to write the Scout/Hunter decisions as §51 moves to §54 when written (corrected in V2.20 — the V2.19 note saying §52 is corrected here). Do not present 565 as continuous, do not renumber to close the gaps, do not reuse a retired ID, and do not merge §45 from this version — §45 still needs Mahendra's yes.

## Biggest changes in V2.20 (Nov 23, 2026 — location gate + differentiation, at Mahendra's instruction "add the location gate and differentiation conclusions too")

**Status: 22 cases added, 0 revised, 0 dropped** — the location gate (§52, UC-585–UC-594, 10 cases) and the differentiation conclusions (§53, UC-595–UC-606, 12 cases) from the Nov 22, 2026 work are now in the spec, all PROPOSED.

1. **Location gate (§52).** A confirmed location is required before claiming a Drop (UC-585) — at the moment of claim, before the claim is created; browsing is never gated. The gate is a combination of methods (UC-586): GPS detect, manual pick, saved home, IP guess (labelled a guess, never stored as fact), state → metro → city picker. GPS snaps to the nearest live city (UC-587); a confirmed location carries through the session (UC-588); city mismatch warns with distance and needs a twice-confirmed "claim anyway" (UC-589), never blocks; a second check guards payment (UC-590). IP is a provisional guess only (UC-591) — it cannot separate Frisco/Prosper/Plano. First-use flow: guess, tap, picker, save (UC-592). Precise geolocation is sensitive data under TDPSA — consent required (UC-593). Native vs web: same GPS, different reliability (UC-594).
2. **Differentiation (§53).** The five-strategy stack, ranked: verifiable dollar saving + portion guarantee (UC-595); real scarcity, slow-window only (UC-596); Top-5 curation, not a catalogue (UC-597); SMS-first, one-tap claim, no app (UC-598); QR pickup as a time promise (UC-599). Video-first (UC-600), local-independent as an honestly quantified tie-breaker (UC-601), retention without subscription (UC-602), AI chat/short video as distribution only (UC-603). Do NOT copy Groupon (UC-604) or DoorDash's lock-in (UC-605). The bundle sweet spot is undecided — do not hard-code a percentage (UC-606).
3. **Evidence grades are carried, not smoothed.** Peer-reviewed field experiments (Cui et al., Mukherjee et al.) are stated as such; vendor and secondary claims (SMS 98%, video 2.5×, streaks) are marked UNVERIFIED in the cases and must not be quoted as fact. The jam-study 10× is explicitly not the expected lift (UC-597).
4. **Numbering note — counts.** This version adds 22 cases, so the file now holds 587 counted cases with highest ID UC-606, a 13-ID gap at UC-503–UC-515, and 6 retired IDs (UC-134, UC-173, UC-307, UC-328, UC-349, UC-554). The Nov 22 plan to write the Scout/Hunter decisions as §51 moves to §54 when written. Do not present 587 as continuous, do not renumber to close the gaps, do not reuse a retired ID, and do not merge §45 from this version — §45 still needs Mahendra's yes.
