- V2.3 note: §25 (UC-254–UC-269) specifies the PROPOSED cancel policy that refines these generic cases — the Ready cutoff, refund timing, whole-Drop cancels, no-show rule, refund money rules, the Ready mechanism, transactional messaging, and pickup-issue handling. §18 cases remain the generic rules; §25 is the policy layer on top of them. §25 is a proposal until Mahendra agrees it (open item 15).

## §19 Demand & customers — P1 (NEW section)

- **UC-212** Show customer info in the Studio as an operator read view: name, phone, city + distance to nearest restaurant, order count, total spent, last Drop + status, pickup record (P1) — NEW. The Studio reads from the backend; it does not store a second copy of customer data. **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-408, mismatch fix):** Demand shows the consent-registry state exactly — per-channel opt-in status, source, and timestamp from UC-388 — not a paraphrase ('subscribed') and not a cached copy. If the registry says opt-out and Demand says opt-in, that is a bug.
- **UC-213** Show "Who to ping for the next Drop" (repeat buyers near a live restaurant first), first-time buyers in a pickup window today, and "Never ping" (no alert opt-in, or replied STOP) (P1) — NEW. Filters: All / Repeat buyers / First-time / Ready to ping + search.
- **UC-214** Show per-channel opt-in status (SMS / WhatsApp / RCS) per customer, and enforce it: marketing consent is SEPARATE from transactional QR texts; opt-in is asked AFTER a successful claim, unchecked by default, remembered, per channel; revocation via STOP (P0) — NEW.
- **UC-215** NEVER show card numbers, expiry, or CVV in the Studio, and NEVER show a customer's full home address (P0) — NEW. Card data stays in Stripe. Takeout pickup needs no home address. This is a hard rule, not a UI preference.

## §20 Money — top-level P&L & settlement — P1 (NEW section)

- **UC-216** Show a per-Drop P&L: sold/unsold, sell-through, gross on claimed, unsold value, DealsPro keeps so far / at sellout, restaurant owed at sellout, creator share at sellout (P1) — NEW. Money must be readable in 10 seconds: what came in, what DealsPro keeps, what the restaurant and creator are owed, whether that Drop actually made money.
- **UC-217** Show Money by city (state + metroplex + sub-city): active restaurants, gross, DealsPro keeps, owed (P1) — NEW.
- **UC-218** Give a repeat / don't-repeat call per Drop from sell-through (e.g. "On track — likely repeat", "Watch — sell-through still low", "Don't repeat at this price / window") (P2) — NEW.

## §21 Team & coverage / no single point of failure — P1 (NEW section)

- **UC-219** Show a coverage strip on Today: primary operator, named backup approver, and restaurant self-serve pickup (P1) — NEW.
- **UC-220** Escalate a queue item to the backup approver after it has waited 2 hours; the backup sees the same queue, checklist, and blockers, and acts inside the same rules (fully-checked Drops only, restaurant confirmation still required) (P1) — NEW.
- **UC-221** Show an owner + waiting clock on every queue item (P1) — NEW.
- **UC-222** Refunds, commission changes, and create-link revocation NEVER escalate — they wait for the operator by design (P0) — NEW.
- **UC-223** Enforce roles: Operator (approve, publish, refund, change commission, manage create links); Backup operator (approve & publish a fully-checked Drop after 2h escalation, mark ready, redeem QR, request changes — cannot refund, change commission, or revoke create links); Restaurant manager (confirm offers, redeem QR, mark ready — own restaurant only, cannot publish, approve own submissions, or see other restaurants / the customer list); Viewer (read-only) (P0) — NEW. Every backup action is audit-logged as the backup, not as the operator.

## §22 Mobile-first Studio — P0 (NEW section)

- **UC-224** Provide a mobile bottom nav (Today, Review with live badge, Drops, raised New Drop FAB, Orders, More) with all remaining sections in a More sheet; desktop keeps the top-tab layout (P0) — NEW. Nothing is desktop-only.
- **UC-225** Make every screen usable one-handed on a phone: 44px+ tap targets, bottom-sheet / full-screen drawers and forms, 16px form fields (no iOS zoom), stacked labeled cards instead of sideways-scrolling tables, toasts clear of the bottom bar, safe-area padding (P0) — NEW.
- **UC-226** Provide one-thumb quick actions on Today: Review queue, Scan QR ticket, New Drop, Money (P1) — NEW.
- **UC-243** NO native app yet: the Studio is a mobile-first web app, installable via Add to Home Screen (full-screen, one tap from the phone, no browser chrome) (P0) — NEW in V2.1. A native app is built ONLY if multiple daily operators, reliable push alerts, or offline QR scanning become required. Mobile-first is MOST IMPORTANT: Mahendra must be able to manage the whole Studio from his mobile phone; laptop is secondary.

## §23 Tech stack, staging vs prod & infrastructure — P0 (NEW section in V2.1)

- **UC-227** Build with staging vs prod as part of the tech stack from day one: two environments, same code, different env vars — staging (staging site, staging Supabase, Stripe test mode, SMS mocked) and prod (dealspro.ai, prod Supabase, Stripe live) (P0) — NEW in V2.1. Claude Code builds in staging; nothing goes to prod without Mahendra approving it. The full test Drop — Create → Publish → Claim → Test Pay → QR → Redeem → Outcomes — must pass in staging before any prod promotion.
- **UC-228** Video is a MUST in the stack: every Drop has a real video hero, stored in Supabase Storage, processed on upload (9:16, muted loop, poster frame, compressed) (P0) — NEW in V2.1. Video is a reusable asset per dish (UC-241); no price, date, or Claim text burned into the video.
- **UC-229** Log in to the Studio by magic link (Supabase Auth) — operator enters their email, clicks the link, and is in; no passwords (P0) — NEW in V2.1. See UC-155. This is for operators only; customers never log in — phone number is their identity.
- **UC-230** Keep all secrets in Vercel for the initial version, split by environment: staging keys in staging, prod keys in prod, never in code, never in GitHub (P0) — NEW in V2.1. Covers Supabase URL/keys, Stripe keys/webhook secrets, and Twilio keys. One wrong key in the wrong environment (e.g. test payments in prod) must be impossible by construction — secrets are per-environment, not shared.
- **UC-231** Host the domain on IONOS and point its DNS to Vercel: IONOS keeps the domain (dealspro.ai), Vercel serves the site; no domain move (P0) — NEW in V2.1. DNS records: dealspro.ai and staging.dealspro.ai both point to Vercel.
- **UC-232** Scale QR by scaling the database and redemption check, not the QR image: QR is a short URL + unique code per order (UC-036), generated on demand, cached; redemption is atomic (UC-038) and inventory decrements atomically (UC-105) in Supabase (P0) — NEW in V2.1. Prove with a simultaneous-purchase / simultaneous-scan test in staging before prod (see open item 13).
- **UC-233** Every URL the system generates is TINY — non-negotiable (P0) — NEW in V2.1. Short server-generated codes for Drops (`dealspro.ai/d/abc123`), QR/pickup (`dealspro.ai/q/abc123`), creator links (`dealspro.ai/c/dallasfoodnerd`), restaurant create links, and smart-menu URLs. NO UUIDs, NO long query strings, in anything a customer, creator, or restaurant has to type, tap, or share. Short codes are unique and unguessable enough that changing a letter does not yield someone else's order. This rule applies to every URL the system generates — Drop, QR, creator, restaurant, smart menu, all of it.

## §24 Scale — first scale — P0 (NEW section in V2.2)

Source: scale-gaps report, Nov 8, 2026 — audit of V2.1 against 10+ restaurants, multiple cities/zones, multiple operators, many creators, repeat Drops, high order volume at pickup windows. These 10 cases are needed *before* that scale, not after it. Later-scale gaps (UC-254–UC-266 in the report — zone graduation, saturation guard, monitoring, backup, privacy, abuse controls, chargebacks, video queue, reliability score, referrals, agent API, experiments, accessibility) are NOT added here; they are recorded in the scale-gaps report for a later version. V2.3 note: the report's proposed UC-254–UC-266 IDs were NOT adopted as numbers — V2.3 assigns UC-254–UC-269 to cancel policy (§25) instead. If the later-scale gaps are adopted later, they take the next free numbers (UC-270+).

- **UC-244** Batch review and operator-throughput dashboard (P0) — NEW in V2.2. Operator can select multiple fully-checked Drops and approve/publish them in one confirmed batch action; each Drop is still individually checklist-gated, and one failure blocks only that Drop, not the batch. Studio shows, per day and per operator: Drops reviewed, median minutes per Drop from submission to publish, queue age by item, and a red flag when median exceeds 30 min for a first Drop / 10 min for a repeat. Queue sorts by pickup urgency, then waiting time. This is the throughput answer to the Nov 2 bottleneck question: operator minutes are tracked as a number, not felt as a feeling.
- **UC-245** Automated creator payout on pickup, with schedule and minimum, via Stripe Connect (P0) — NEW in V2.2. Creator share accrues per picked-up order only (UC-211, UC-239). Studio batches accrued shares per creator on a fixed schedule (weekly proposed — Mahendra to confirm, see open item 14) and pays through **Stripe Connect** — Connect onboarding, bank accounts, and split payouts — not ad-hoc Zelle at scale. Each payout shows the orders it covers, the per-order math, and a payout receipt; no payout below a stated minimum rolls to the next cycle. Money calculating a share (UC-216) is not paying it — UC-245 is the disbursement.
- **UC-246** Restaurant settlement: scheduled payout, reconciliation, and hold, via Stripe Connect (P0) — NEW in V2.2. Each restaurant has a payout schedule (weekly proposed — confirm), a payout method on file in **Stripe Connect**, and a settlement statement per period listing gross, refunds, Stripe fees, commission, creator share, and net paid, tied to a bank/Stripe payout reference. Payout is held automatically when an unresolved refund/chargeback exceeds a threshold (threshold to confirm — open item 14) or when the restaurant's payout details are unverified; the hold, the reason, and the release are audit-logged and visible to the restaurant. Stripe payouts are reconciled against the bank record; any mismatch is flagged as "needs reconciliation" (UC-130) with the exact orders in dispute. This is the disbursement behind UC-125–134 and UC-216–217, which only calculate what is owed.
- **UC-247** Restaurant pickup dashboard and unclaimed-order handling (P0) — NEW in V2.2. Restaurant (or operator on its behalf) sees a live pickup queue for the current window: order, customer first name + phone last digits, quantity, ready/not-ready, redeemed/not-redeemed, time remaining. Restaurant can mark an order Ready. Operator can redeem by reference number when a QR will not scan (audit-logged, same one-use rule as UC-038). Quantity cannot exceed the restaurant's stated per-window prep capacity without an explicit override. At window end, unclaimed paid orders are listed as "unclaimed — resolve" with the choices refund, hold for late pickup, or forfeit per the restaurant's stated policy; no unclaimed order is silently kept or silently refunded. Alert when a window is live and orders remain unclaimed with little time left (extends UC-150, which only covers repeated redemption failures).
- **UC-248** Creator fraud and attribution-conflict handling (P0) — NEW in V2.2; attribution rule **REVISED and LOCKED in V2.7**. System flags: a creator's own phone/device buying through their own link; multiple orders from the same phone/device through one creator link in a short window; a creator link used only by orders that never pick up. Flagged orders are held from creator payout pending operator review; the creator is NOT paid on their own purchases. Attribution conflicts (two creators, same customer, same visit) resolve by a single stated rule — **LOCKED (Mahendra, Nov 8, 2026 — V2.7): last-tagged-link-wins. The creator whose tagged link was tapped last before purchase earns the credit; a smart-menu visit tag counts as a tag and wins over a later untagged visit, but loses to a later tagged link.** The rule is shown on the order, never decided case by case. Creator vetting/onboarding (who can become a creator, what removes them) is part of UC-252.
- **UC-249** Customer and restaurant support queue with resolutions (P0) — NEW in V2.2. Every support request is a ticket tied to an order/Drop/restaurant, with status, owner, and waiting clock (same pattern as UC-221). The five recurring problems each have a one-tap resolution path: resend QR by SMS, redeem by reference, refund per UC-182–183, rebook to a Repeat Drop, and "I paid but the Drop says sold out" (oversell/race — UC-105). Tickets are searchable by phone/order; a ticket cannot be closed without a resolution reason. A customer-facing help page, linked from every Drop and QR confirmation, states support hours and a first-response target. UC-152 identifies orders requiring assistance; UC-249 resolves them.
- **UC-250** Messaging compliance and fatigue controls (P0) — NEW in V2.2. Studio records, per message: recipient, channel, Drop, consent basis, template used, delivery status, and opt-out; every send is labelled transactional (QR) vs marketing (Drop alert) at send time. Sends are BLOCKED when: no opt-in for that channel (UC-214), STOP on file, the customer was already sent a marketing message about a Drop in the same zone within the stated window (proposed: 1 marketing message per customer per week unless they claimed — Mahendra to confirm, open item 14), or the sender registration is not approved. Twilio / WhatsApp Business sender status (registered / pending / blocked) is shown in the Studio; a blocked sender halts marketing sends automatically. This is the compliance layer on top of UC-214 opt-in enforcement: opt-in alone does not cover 10DLC filtering, sender blocking, or fatigue churn.
- **UC-251** Chain/group: shared terms, shared media, per-location Drops and payouts (P1 — P0 the moment a chain is the second restaurant) — NEW in V2.2. A chain has one parent record (one commission agreement, one payout method in Stripe Connect, one contact), with locations underneath. Video, dish, and price terms can be shared across locations; Drops, inventory, pickup, and smart menus stay per location (UC-235). Money can be viewed per location *and* consolidated per chain. Removing one location (UC-200) does not remove the chain. Without this, a 3-location chain is onboarded three times, negotiates commission three times, uploads the same video three times, and receives three separate payouts.
- **UC-252** Creator onboarding and self-service dashboard (P1) — NEW in V2.2. A creator applies / is invited, provides payout details (Stripe Connect onboarding) and tax information (UC-253), and gets a read-only view: their permanent link, Drops available in their area with commission per picked-up order, their picked-up orders, accrued earnings, payout schedule and history, and the attribution rule (UC-248). They cannot change price, quantity, or pickup window; they cannot see other creators' earnings or the customer list. Form to confirm (open item 14): a creator portal, or a link + WhatsApp at first scale — the catalog must state the answer, not leave it as "a shared list." Without this, Mahendra becomes the creator helpdesk at 10+ creators.
- **UC-253** Payout identity and tax collection, via Stripe Connect (P0 before the first scaled payout) — NEW in V2.2. Before a creator's first payout above the stated threshold (proposed: $600/year, the US 1099 threshold — confirm with preparer, do not hard-code tax law into the catalog without advice), the Studio collects and stores the required tax form/ID **through Stripe Connect**, which handles 1099/W-9; payout is blocked until it is on file. Same for restaurants: payout method + identity verification in Connect before the first settlement. Annual 1099 totals are exportable per payee. This is what makes UC-245 and UC-246 legally payable at volume; reconstructing a year's payouts from Zelle memos is not a plan.

## §25 Cancel, refund & pickup issues — P0 (NEW section in V2.3)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided (open item 15).** Every case in this section is the proposed cancel policy from the Nov 8, 2026 conversation. Mahendra has not yet agreed it. Build §25 as locked policy only after he agrees; until then it is a proposal. §25 refines §18 (UC-180–UC-194), which holds the generic cancellation/refund mechanics; §25 is the policy layer on top of them. The Ready timestamp (UC-264–UC-266) is the cutoff the whole policy hangs on. **V2.10 (Nov 8, 2026 — Mahendra: "this is very critical"):** the cancel model is now restaurant-direct — the customer calls the restaurant, the restaurant decides and cancels in-system, DealsPro syncs back (UC-479–UC-488). The V2.3 operator-decides / customer-self-serve model is SUPERSEDED; each revised case below keeps its old text in a superseded note.

- **UC-254** Cancel is restaurant-direct, not customer self-serve: the order page shows the restaurant's phone number and tells the customer to call the restaurant — there is NO customer Cancel button and NO reply-CANCEL for the customer (P0) — **REVISED in V2.10 — restaurant-direct model, supersedes operator-decides version.** The order page shows, live, the order's status and what happens next — full refund if the restaurant cancels before Ready, support case after Ready, or no refund after the window if not picked up — so the customer is never surprised by the outcome (UC-479/UC-480/UC-484). The customer never cancels in DealsPro; the restaurant cancels in-system (UC-480), and DealsPro syncs back. *Superseded (V2.3 operator-decides version): the customer cancelled from their order page (Cancel button) or by replying CANCEL to the order text; the Cancel button was hidden (not just disabled) once self-serve cancel no longer applied.*
- **UC-255** Restaurant cancels BEFORE the pickup window starts, after the customer calls it: full refund, automatic (P0) — **REVISED in V2.10 — restaurant-direct model, supersedes operator-decides version.** The customer calls the restaurant (UC-479); if the restaurant agrees, it taps Cancel on its pickup link or replies CANCEL [code] (UC-480). The tap triggers: the QR ticket is invalidated instantly and can never be scanned after that (refines UC-186); inventory is restored immediately so the tray is available again (refines UC-187); the refund goes to the original card and the customer is told it usually shows up in 5–10 business days depending on their bank. No operator action. A phone call with no tap is no cancel (UC-482). *Superseded (V2.3): the customer cancelled themselves from the order page or by reply CANCEL, with the same refund mechanics.*
- **UC-256** Restaurant cancels DURING the pickup window, BEFORE it marks the order Ready, after the customer calls it: full refund, automatic, if the restaurant agrees (P0) — **REVISED in V2.10 — restaurant-direct model, supersedes operator-decides version.** Same mechanics as UC-255 (QR invalidated, inventory restored, refund to original card, 5–10 business days). The only difference from UC-255 is the timing — the window has started but the food is not yet made. If the restaurant says NO on the phone, the order stays live: DealsPro does not override a restaurant's no, the restaurant tells the customer itself, and no refund is issued and no cancel is recorded (UC-486). *Superseded (V2.3): the customer cancelled themselves during the window before Ready, with the same refund mechanics; the restaurant's agreement was not part of the rule.*
- **UC-257** AFTER Ready, before pickup: NO cancel — not by the customer, and not by the restaurant either; support case, operator decides (P0) — **REVISED in V2.10 — restaurant-direct model, supersedes operator-decides version.** Once the restaurant has marked the order Ready, the food is made and Ready locks the cancel for both sides (UC-484, UC-275). The customer contacts support instead (UC-249); the operator decides case by case — usually a refund if the restaurant agrees, or credit for the next Drop. The restaurant's pickup link shows no Cancel action for a Ready order, and a CANCEL reply for a Ready order is rejected with a message telling the restaurant to contact the operator. The order page must say this plainly at this stage. *Superseded (V2.3): no self-serve cancel after Ready; support case, operator decides — the V2.3 text did not state that the restaurant also cannot cancel after Ready.*
- **UC-258** AFTER pickup: NO refund, except for a real issue (P0) — NEW in V2.3, PROPOSED. A picked-up order is not refundable as a cancel. The exception is a genuine problem — wrong item, bad food, missing item — handled as a support case (UC-249) with a photo if needed, decided by the operator (refines UC-185, which requires an exception process for redeemed orders).
- **UC-259** Restaurant cancels a whole Drop after orders are paid: every customer gets a full refund automatically, plus a message explaining why, plus first access to the next Drop from that restaurant (P0) — NEW in V2.3, PROPOSED. The restaurant earns a reliability strike for the cancel; TWO strikes = probation — no new Drops until the operator reviews the restaurant (ties to UC-154, repeated fulfillment problems, and UC-247 pickup reliability). The refund message is automatic and states the reason; no customer is left wondering.
- **UC-260** DealsPro cancels a Drop: same as UC-259 — full refunds automatic, message to every customer (P0) — NEW in V2.3, PROPOSED. No one keeps money for a Drop that did not happen. The cancel, the reason, and every refund are audit-logged (UC-263).
- **UC-261** No-show / unclaimed order: grace through the end of the pickup window, one reminder text, then marked unclaimed with NO refund (P0) — **REVISED in V2.10 — restaurant-direct model, supersedes operator-decides version.** The food was made and held for the customer, so the order is not refundable by default. The RESTAURANT — not the operator — may override ONCE as a goodwill refund, by tapping "Refund as goodwill" with a reason on its pickup link (UC-483); the override is the exception, not the rule, and is audit-logged with a reason (UC-263/UC-449). If the restaurant says "too late" on the phone, nothing happens in DealsPro and the order closes as unclaimed. The order is never silently kept and never silently refunded (consistent with UC-247). *Superseded (V2.3): the OPERATOR could override once as a goodwill refund; the restaurant had no goodwill-override action.*
- **UC-262** Refund money rules (P0) — NEW in V2.3, PROPOSED. (a) Refunds always go to the ORIGINAL card, never as cash or as DealsPro credit by default. (b) Stripe processing fees on a refunded order are NOT charged to the customer — DealsPro absorbs them, or they are reversed with the refund where Stripe allows it. (c) Creator and restaurant are NEVER paid for a cancelled or refunded order — commission counts only on picked-up orders (same rule as UC-207/UC-216 and the settlement formula in §13). Refunding an order reverses its Connect split (UC-245/UC-246). (d) Refund and cancellation statuses are displayed separately (refines UC-188), and duplicate refunds are prevented by idempotency and payment-state checks (UC-193).
- **UC-263** Audit-log every cancel, refund, and override with who did it, when, and why — including restaurant-direct cancels (P0) — **REVISED in V2.10 — restaurant-direct model, supersedes operator-decides version.** Extends UC-192 (record cancellation actor, timestamp, and reason) to cover goodwill overrides (UC-261/UC-483), whole-Drop cancels (UC-259/UC-260), support-decided refunds (UC-257/UC-258), and restaurant-direct cancels (UC-480/UC-482): actor is the restaurant (pickup-link identity, not a named person), the tap time, the order/Drop, the reason (including a goodwill reason), and the before/after values (UC-449/UC-450). The log is visible in Money and on the order, so a dispute a month later — "the restaurant said they'd cancel" — is answered from the tap record, not from a phone call nobody recorded. A phone call with no tap has no audit entry, because no cancel happened. *Superseded (V2.3): audit-log covered goodwill overrides, whole-Drop cancels, and support-decided refunds; restaurant-direct cancels did not exist.*
- **UC-264** Restaurant marks an order Ready from a private pickup link — no login, no app (P0) — NEW in V2.3, PROPOSED. When a Drop's pickup window approaches, the restaurant gets a text/WhatsApp with a private pickup link (same no-login idea as the private create link, UC-203). Tapping it shows that Drop's order list in pickup order — customer first name, quantity, ticket code — with a big Ready button per order and a Ready All button at the top. Tapping Ready marks that order Ready and the customer automatically gets a "ready for pickup" text (UC-267). A restaurant must be able to mark 15 orders Ready in under a minute, even during a rush.
- **UC-265** Ready fallbacks and the Ready timestamp (P0) — NEW in V2.3, PROPOSED. (a) Replying READY to the pickup text marks ALL orders for that Drop Ready. (b) The same Ready button exists in the Studio Orders & Pickup board for an operator or a restaurant on a counter tablet. (c) The Ready timestamp is recorded per order and LOCKS the cancel cutoff: before Ready = UC-256 full refund; after Ready = UC-257 support case. The timestamp is shown on the order and in the pickup queue (UC-247).
- **UC-266** A restaurant that never marks Ready: the end of the pickup window is the cutoff, and the restaurant is flagged (P1) — NEW in V2.3, PROPOSED. If no Ready timestamp exists when the window ends, the cancel cutoff defaults to the end of the window, and the restaurant is flagged for unreliable Ready behaviour (ties to UC-154 and the UC-259 reliability strikes). A restaurant that does not mark Ready is a restaurant that cannot be trusted with the next Drop.
- **UC-267** Transactional customer messages, automatic, on the channel they ordered from — including the restaurant's number and the restaurant-direct cancel message (P0) — **REVISED in V2.10 — restaurant-direct model, supersedes operator-decides version.** Messages, each automatic, each on SMS/WhatsApp — the channel the customer ordered from: order confirmed (with the restaurant's phone number, the order code, and "For any changes, call the restaurant directly at [number], quote order [code]" — UC-479) → ready for pickup → picked up, thank you → cancelled, refund on its way. The cancel message states the refund amount and the 5–10 business day timing (UC-255/UC-256) and is sent ONLY on the restaurant's in-system tap (UC-480) — a phone call alone sends no cancel message, because no cancel has happened. Marketing opt-in is SEPARATE and is never mixed into these messages (UC-214, UC-250). No message = the customer thinks they were charged for nothing; the refund message is not optional. *Superseded (V2.3): the same four messages, without the restaurant number / call-the-restaurant instruction in the confirmation, and with the cancel message following a customer self-serve cancel.*
- **UC-268** QR will not scan at pickup: manual ticket-code fallback with the same one-time redemption (P0) — NEW in V2.3, PROPOSED. The restaurant enters the order's ticket code instead of scanning; it runs the same one-use rule as UC-038 and the same redeem-by-reference path as UC-247, audit-logged, and cannot redeem an already-redeemed, cancelled, or refunded order. The fallback must work every time — it is the difference between a recovered pickup and a lost customer.
- **UC-269** Order not ready / wrong item at pickup: restaurant marks it in Studio, customer gets a delay/remake text (P0) — NEW in V2.3, PROPOSED. The restaurant flags the problem (running late, wrong item being remade) and the customer automatically gets a text with the new time — e.g. "Running 15 min late, sorry" or "Wrong item — we're remaking it now." Silence is what turns a small problem into a lost customer. If the remake cannot be done in the window, the order becomes a support case under UC-257/UC-258.

### §25 extension — Restaurant-direct cancel/change (NEW in V2.10, Mahendra Nov 8, 2026: "this is very critical")

**Status: PROPOSED, not locked (open items 15 and 25).** This extension replaces the V2.3 operator-decides / customer-self-serve cancel model with the restaurant-direct model. The rule in one line: the restaurant decides, but DealsPro records. The customer calls the restaurant; the restaurant taps Cancel in-system; DealsPro refunds automatically and tells the customer. No tap, no refund, no sync.

- **UC-479** After claim/payment, send the customer the restaurant's number to call for any changes (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. The confirmation text (UC-267) and the order page (UC-379) show, together and in plain type: the restaurant name, its published/order phone number, the order code, and the pickup window (date + start/end), with the instruction "For any changes, call the restaurant directly at [number], quote order [code]". The number is shown from the moment payment succeeds, so a customer who needs to change or cancel never has to search for it. The order page repeats the same instruction at every status step (UC-277 timeline) until the order is picked up or closed.
- **UC-480** The restaurant cancels in-system — pickup-link Cancel tap, or reply CANCEL [code] — per order, with no login (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. The restaurant's private pickup link (UC-264) shows a Cancel action per order (next to Ready, never instead of it) for any order that is not yet Ready and whose window has not ended; tapping it asks for a reason (a short list: customer asked, cannot fulfil, wrong quantity, other — free text for "other"), then confirms "Cancel order [code] and refund [amount] to the customer?". Replying CANCEL [code] to the pickup text does the same thing, with the reason asked by a follow-up text. The cancel is per order, never per Drop from this action (a whole-Drop cancel is UC-259, a different action with a reliability strike). The tap/reply is authenticated by the pickup link itself — the same no-login identity as Ready (UC-264) — so a restaurant can cancel from its counter phone during service with no password and no app.
- **UC-481** A change is a cancel plus a new claim — no partial changes, no swaps (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. A Drop is one dish, one quantity, one window (UC-012/UC-013/UC-276), so there is nothing partial to change: a customer who wants a different quantity, a different dish, or a different window cancels the order under UC-255/UC-256 (restaurant tap, full refund) and claims again — if units remain — as a new order with a new code and a new QR. The restaurant must not promise a swap on the phone ("we'll give you 2 instead of 3") without cancelling and re-claiming in-system, because a promised swap with no system record is an order DealsPro cannot fulfil, refund, or reconcile. If no units remain, the customer is told so on the phone and the original order stands.
- **UC-482** A phone call alone is never the system of record — no tap, no refund, no sync (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. The restaurant's verbal agreement on the phone ("yeah, we'll cancel it") creates no cancel, no refund, no inventory change, and no audit entry until the restaurant taps Cancel (UC-480). If the restaurant never taps, the order stays live, the QR stays valid, the customer can still pick up, and no refund is issued — even if the customer produces a call record. This is stated to the customer in the confirmation (UC-479: "Ask the restaurant to cancel it in their system — the refund is sent when they tap Cancel") and to the restaurant on the pickup link ("Tap Cancel to refund this order — a phone promise alone does not refund it"). Dispute rule: if a customer says "the restaurant said they'd cancel" and there is no tap, the operator's answer is the tap record (UC-263/UC-449) — the operator does not refund on a call record alone, and opens a support case (UC-249) to contact the restaurant instead.
- **UC-483** After the window, didn't pick up: the restaurant may refund once as goodwill, by tapping "Refund as goodwill" with a reason (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. This is the UC-261 no-show override, moved from the operator to the restaurant: after the window ends with no pickup, the order is unclaimed with NO refund by default (food was made and held). On the pickup link, for that order only, the restaurant sees "Refund as goodwill" (once, with a reason — e.g. customer called late, traffic, illness — and the amount is the full order amount, never partial). Tapping it issues the full refund to the original card (UC-262), invalidates the QR, and is audit-logged (UC-263/UC-449) as a restaurant goodwill override. The override is available once per order and never twice; a second request for the same order is a support case (UC-249), operator-decides. If the restaurant says "too late" on the phone, nothing happens in DealsPro and the order closes as unclaimed.
- **UC-484** After Ready, the restaurant cannot cancel either — Ready locks the cancel for both sides (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. Once an order is marked Ready (UC-264/UC-265), the pickup link shows no Cancel action for it, and a CANCEL [code] reply for a Ready order is rejected with a message telling the restaurant to contact the operator (UC-249 support case, UC-257). The reason is the same as for the customer: the food is made. A restaurant that marks an order Ready by mistake must contact the operator, not cancel it itself — the operator decides, with the restaurant, whether to refund, remake, or hold for pickup. This case is the cross-reference to UC-275 (Ready is the trigger for the UC-257 cancel cutoff) — the lock is now stated from the restaurant's side as well as the customer's.
- **UC-485** The restaurant number shown to customers is the published/order number — never the owner's personal mobile — and it is shown only on the order page and the confirmation text (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. The number comes from the restaurant record (UC-003 onboarding: published/order phone, a separate field from the owner's personal mobile and from the claim-notification contact, UC-270). It is never the owner's personal mobile unless the owner has explicitly set that number as the published order number. It appears ONLY on the order page (UC-379) and in the confirmation text (UC-267/UC-479) — never on the public Drop page, never on the smart menu, never in a marketing message — so a customer who has not ordered cannot call it. If a restaurant has no published order number on record, the order page shows "Call the restaurant" with no number and flags the restaurant in review (UC-062) until one is set; the order is not blocked, but the restaurant-direct cancel path is unavailable for it until the number is set (open item 25a).
- **UC-486** If the restaurant says no on the phone, the order stays live and the restaurant tells the customer (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. A restaurant may decline a cancel — for example, the food is already made, or the window is nearly over — and that no is final in DealsPro: no refund is issued, no cancel is recorded, the QR stays valid, and the customer can still pick up. The restaurant tells the customer itself on the phone; DealsPro sends no message contradicting it and no message confirming a cancel that did not happen. If the customer then contacts support saying the restaurant refused, the operator's role is to check the order status (live, not cancelled), tell the customer the order is still live for pickup, and — only if the restaurant agrees on a follow-up call — record the cancel by the restaurant's tap (UC-480). The operator never cancels on the customer's word alone.
- **UC-487** On the restaurant's tap, DealsPro syncs back instantly — QR invalidated, inventory restored, refund automatic, money rules applied (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. The tap (UC-480) or the goodwill tap (UC-483) is one atomic transaction: the QR is invalidated instantly (refines UC-186 — it can never be scanned after that, including at the counter in the same minute); inventory is restored immediately so the unit is available again (refines UC-187); a full refund is issued to the original card (UC-262 — 5–10 business days, Stripe fees not charged to the customer, Connect split reversed, creator and restaurant never paid for a cancelled/refunded order, commission only on picked-up orders); and the customer gets the "Cancelled, refund on its way" text with the amount (UC-267). The sync is visible in the same second in the Studio (order status Cancelled, inventory count restored) and in Money (UC-441/UC-442) — a cancel that is not visible in Money in the same second is a sync defect, not a delay.
- **UC-488** The operator sees every restaurant-direct cancel in the Studio and can investigate a dispute — but cannot override a restaurant's no without the restaurant (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. Every restaurant cancel, goodwill refund, and refused cancel (where the restaurant reports it, or a support case records it) appears in the Studio Orders & Pickup board and in Money, with the actor (restaurant), the tap time, the reason, and the refund (UC-263/UC-449). The operator can open a support case (UC-249) for a disputed cancel, contact the restaurant, and record the outcome — but cannot cancel an order on the customer's word alone (UC-482) and cannot override a restaurant's no (UC-486) without the restaurant's tap. The operator's own cancel powers are unchanged and narrow: whole-Drop cancels by DealsPro (UC-260) and support-decided refunds after Ready (UC-257) — neither is a way to cancel a live order because a customer asked on the phone.

## §26 Claim → restaurant confirm → Ready — P0 (NEW section in V2.4)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided (open item 17).** Every case in this section is the proposed claim → confirm → Ready flow from the Nov 8, 2026 conversation. Mahendra has not yet agreed it. Build §26 as locked flow only after he agrees; until then it is a proposal. §26 sits between payment (§4) and pickup (§10/§25): a paid claim is not an accepted order until the restaurant accepts it, and it is not Ready until the restaurant marks it Ready. It reuses the Ready mechanism from UC-264–UC-266 and the transactional messaging from UC-267, and it refines UC-247 (pickup dashboard) and UC-149/UC-152 (pickup alerts / orders needing assistance) with an explicit accept step and an accept deadline.

- **UC-270** Send the claim to the restaurant IMMEDIATELY when a user claims/pays (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED. The moment payment succeeds, the restaurant receives the claim — dish, quantity, customer first name, pickup window, ticket code, and claim time — by text/WhatsApp to the restaurant contact and on its live pickup queue (UC-247). No batching, no delay, no "check the Studio later": the claim information is pushed to the restaurant at claim time.
- **UC-271** Until the restaurant accepts, the order status is "Claimed — awaiting restaurant confirmation" (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED. This is a distinct, visible status in the Studio, on the restaurant pickup queue, and on the customer's order page — not "Confirmed", not "Accepted", not "Ready". A paid order with no restaurant accept is never shown to the customer as accepted, and never counts as a confirmed pickup.
- **UC-272** The restaurant must EXPLICITLY accept the claim — an Accept step, not silence (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED. Accept is per order (Accept button on the private pickup link, UC-264; reply ACCEPT to the claim text; Accept on the Studio Orders & Pickup board), with an Accept All for the Drop's orders as a convenience. The accept, its time, and who accepted are recorded per order. Opening the pickup link, reading the text, or doing nothing is never acceptance. A restaurant cannot accept an order it has already declined (UC-273) or that has been cancelled/refunded (UC-255/UC-256).
- **UC-273** If the restaurant CANNOT fulfil the claim, it declines it explicitly — and the customer is refunded and told (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED. Decline (Decline button / reply DECLINE with a reason) triggers, automatically: the order is cancelled, a full refund goes to the original card (UC-262 money rules, 5–10 business days), the QR is invalidated, inventory is restored, and the customer gets a message stating the restaurant could not fulfil the order and the refund is on its way. A declined order never sits in "awaiting confirmation". A decline counts toward the restaurant's reliability record (UC-154, UC-259 strikes).
- **UC-274** Once the restaurant accepts, the customer automatically gets a "Restaurant accepted your order" message (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED. Sent immediately on accept, on the SAME channel the customer ordered from (SMS/WhatsApp — UC-267), stating: restaurant name and pickup address, the exact pickup window (date + start/end time), what to show at pickup (QR / ticket code), and that the next message comes when the order is Ready. No accept = no accepted message; the message is never sent before the restaurant actually accepts.
- **UC-275** When the food is ready, the restaurant marks it Ready and the customer automatically gets a "Ready for pickup" message (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED; **REVISED in V2.10 (cross-ref, restaurant-direct model).** Ready uses the UC-264/UC-265 mechanism — private pickup link, Ready per order + Ready All, reply READY, or the Studio board — and the Ready timestamp is recorded per order. The ready message goes out immediately on marking Ready, on the same channel, with the pickup window and what to show. Ready is the trigger for the UC-257 cancel cutoff: after Ready, no self-serve cancel — and, under the V2.10 restaurant-direct model, no restaurant self-serve cancel either (UC-484): a Ready order can be cancelled only as a support case, operator-decides.
- **UC-276** The 2–3 hour pickup window is shown EXACTLY, everywhere, in big type (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED. Date + start time + end time, in the restaurant's timezone, shown in big type on the Drop page, the smart menu (UC-238), the claim confirmation, every status message, and the customer's order page. Never "today afternoon", never a time without a date, never buried in details. If the window changes after claim, the customer is messaged with the new window and the order page is updated — the old window is never left standing.
- **UC-277** The customer always sees a live status timeline: Claimed → Accepted by restaurant → Ready → Picked up (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED. The order page shows the four steps with the current step highlighted, each completed step timestamped, and the next step named ("Waiting for the restaurant to accept" / "Restaurant accepted — preparing your order" / "Ready — come and pick up"). The same timeline is visible to the operator in the Studio and to the restaurant on its pickup queue. A status never jumps a step: an order cannot show Ready before it shows Accepted, and cannot show Picked up before Ready.
- **UC-278** Show a live countdown / time remaining in the pickup window (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED. The order page shows how much of the 2–3 hour window is left ("Pickup window ends in 1 h 20 min", then "ends in 15 min"), updating live. Before the window opens it shows time until the window opens. After the window ends with no pickup, the order moves to the unclaimed flow (UC-261/UC-247). The countdown is computed from the window's end time, never from a fake urgency timer.
- **UC-279** If the restaurant is LATE to accept, the customer gets a delay message and the operator is alerted — never silence (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED. If the restaurant has not accepted within the defined accept window (deadline TO DECIDE — open item 17), the system: (a) alerts the operator (Studio + text) that an order is unaccepted past the window, and (b) messages the customer that the restaurant has not confirmed yet, what is happening, and when they will hear next. The customer is never left staring at "awaiting confirmation" with no update. **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-394, mismatch fix):** The delay message and the operator alert fire AT the accept deadline as a P0 alert — not after a grace period, and not only when the operator next opens the Studio. The alert is P0 because a missed accept deadline is a missed pickup.
- **UC-280** If the restaurant is LATE to mark Ready, the customer gets a delay message with a new time — never silence (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED. If an accepted order is not marked Ready as the window progresses (e.g. no Ready with 30 minutes left in the window, or the restaurant flags a delay per UC-269), the customer automatically gets a message with a revised ready time. The restaurant can set the revised time from the pickup link; if it does not, the operator sets it or contacts the restaurant. A delay message is sent at most once per order unless the time slips again, in which case a new message with the new time is sent — the customer always has the latest time.
- **UC-281** If the restaurant does NOT accept within the defined window, the order escalates to the operator and the customer is told what is happening (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED. On missing the accept deadline, the order is escalated: it appears at the top of the operator's queue with owner + waiting clock (UC-221), the operator contacts the restaurant, and the operator decides — hold for a late accept, or decline-and-refund under UC-273. The customer is messaged at escalation ("We are checking with the restaurant — we will update you within [time]") and again when the operator decides. The order is never auto-cancelled without telling the customer, and never left in "awaiting confirmation" past the deadline without an owner.
- **UC-282** Transparency rules: every status change triggers a message, and Ready is never faked (P0) — NEW in V2.4, PROPOSED. (a) Every transition — Claimed, Accepted, Ready, Picked up, Delayed, Declined, Cancelled — triggers an automatic message to the customer on their channel (UC-267), and updates the order-page timeline (UC-277). (b) The customer can always open their order page and see the current status, the pickup window, and whether the restaurant has confirmed. (c) NO fake "ready": Ready exists only when the restaurant actually marks it Ready (UC-264/UC-265). The system never auto-marks an order Ready at the window start, on a timer, or because the restaurant opened the pickup link. (d) Every accept, decline, Ready, delay, and escalation is audit-logged with actor, timestamp, and reason (UC-263, UC-192).

## §27 Stripe Connect & automated payouts — P0 (NEW section in V2.5 — reconciled from Studio §25)

Source: Studio catalog §25 (Studio UC-278–UC-288, 11 cases), carried over verbatim in substance and renumbered UC-283–UC-293. This section EXPANDS file §24's summary payout cases (UC-245, UC-246, UC-253) into the operating detail — it does not replace them. Studio values that are still decisions (Friday payout day, 2-day hold, $10 minimum) are recorded as the Studio's values; they do not override open item 14 until Mahendra confirms them.

- **UC-283** Use Stripe Connect (Express) as the only payout rail for restaurants and creators — decided Nov 8, 2026; no manual transfer method is offered as the system of record (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-278. Expands UC-245/UC-246/UC-253.
- **UC-284** Give every restaurant location and every earning creator its own Connect account, onboarded once through a Stripe-hosted flow reached from a tiny DealsPro link (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-279.
- **UC-285** Split each payment at charge time: Stripe fee, DealsPro commission, creator share (when attributed) and restaurant net each land in the right balance — no month-end manual splitting (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-280.
- **UC-286** Keep commission and creator share earned only on redeemed orders: shares for refunded or never-picked-up orders are released back at the hold instead of paying out (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-281. Same rule as the §13 settlement formula and UC-262.
- **UC-287** Run payouts on a fixed schedule (weekly, Fridays) covering orders picked up at least 2 days earlier — the hold window absorbs refunds and chargebacks from the Connect balance before money reaches a bank (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-282. The Friday day and the 2-day hold are the Studio's values; the schedule and hold threshold are still to confirm (open item 14) — do not hard-code them as decided.
- **UC-288** Allow an operator-approved early release per payee, audit-logged, when the balance is clean (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-283.
- **UC-289** Roll creator balances under the $10 minimum payout forward to the next run automatically (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-284. The $10 figure is the Studio's value; the minimum is still to confirm (open item 14).
- **UC-290** Reconcile daily against Stripe balance transactions: every payout leg ties to order IDs, and any exception (payout without orders, order without payout leg) becomes an owned task in Money, never a month-end surprise (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-285.
- **UC-291** Keep payment status and payout status separate everywhere; a paid order is not a paid-out order (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-286. Refines UC-129.
- **UC-292** Use Stripe test mode with test Connect accounts in staging and live accounts only in prod; no real payout can ever leave staging (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-287. Refines UC-227.
- **UC-293** Never store bank account numbers, SSNs or tax documents in DealsPro or Studio — Stripe holds them; Studio shows status only (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-288.

## §28 Operator throughput & batch review — P1 (NEW section in V2.5 — reconciled from Studio §26)

Source: Studio catalog §26 (Studio UC-289–UC-296, 8 cases), carried over and renumbered UC-294–UC-301. This section EXPANDS file UC-244 (batch review + throughput) into the operating detail. CONFLICT NOTE: UC-299's ≤5-minute review-time target differs from UC-244's median flags (30 min first Drop / 10 min repeat). Both are proposals; the target is still to decide (open item 14) — do not build both numbers.

- **UC-294** Order the review queue by pickup urgency first, then longest wait, with manual reorder always available (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-289. Refines UC-244's queue-prioritisation rule.
- **UC-295** Show queue health at a glance: items in queue, oldest wait, items past the 2-hour backup-escalation window (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-290. Refines UC-220/UC-221.
- **UC-296** Let the operator select multiple queue items and publish every fully-checked one in a single batch action — each item still passes the complete publish check (video gate, serves, price, quantity, confirmation) before it can be in the batch (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-291. Expands UC-244.
- **UC-297** Never batch-publish a restaurant submission: every submission gets its own individual operator decision (approve / request changes / reject), however long the queue is (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-292.
- **UC-298** Never batch-publish a blocked Drop; the batch result names what was published and what stayed, with reasons (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-293.
- **UC-299** Record review time per Drop (reviewer, minutes, outcome) and show the running average against a ≤5-minute target in the queue (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-294. CONFLICT: UC-244 flags median >30 min (first Drop) / >10 min (repeat); the ≤5-minute target here is the Studio's number. Target still to decide — see the section note above.
- **UC-300** Surface the Drop types that repeatedly take the longest in review so the checklist or form gets fixed instead of the operator working longer (P2) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-295.
- **UC-301** Keep batch actions audit-logged as one event listing every Drop it touched (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-296.

## §29 Pickup at burst, QR fallback & unclaimed orders — P0 (NEW section in V2.5 — reconciled from Studio §27)

Source: Studio catalog §27 (Studio UC-297–UC-304, 8 cases), carried over and renumbered UC-302–UC-309. This section EXPANDS file UC-247 (pickup dashboard + unclaimed) into the operating detail. NOTE on the grace rule: the Studio's 30-minute post-window grace (formerly file UC-307, DELETED in V2.17 — delete pass, Nov 22, 2026) refines file UC-261 (grace through the end of the window, then unclaimed); the two grace wordings differ and the grace length is still PROPOSED — it is not a locked rule until Mahendra agrees the cancel policy (open item 15). The grace wording is retained in open items 26 and 29d for his pick.

- **UC-302** Give each restaurant a pickup board — Paid → Ready → Picked up lanes, oldest first, big tap targets — that is the same order records as the Orders list, never a second copy (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-297. Expands UC-247.
- **UC-303** Scope the board per restaurant: managers run only their own counter's board, on a phone, without Studio access to anything else (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-298. Refines UC-223 roles.
- **UC-304** Move an order Paid → Ready → Picked up from the board in one tap each, with the change reflected everywhere instantly (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-299.
- **UC-305** Redeem by manual code entry (ticket short code or order number) when a QR cannot scan — the fallback runs the identical one-time atomic redemption and rejects an already-redeemed code with its original redemption time (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-300. Same rule as UC-268 and UC-247.
- **UC-306** Reject an unknown code with a specific instruction (check the confirmation text, try the order number) — a pack is never handed out against an unverified code (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-301.

- **UC-308** After grace + reminder, require an explicit operator decision per unclaimed order — refund or no-refund per the Drop's terms — and open a support ticket automatically so every unclaimed order has an owner (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-303. Refines UC-261 and UC-247.
- **UC-309** Count board lane totals live so a counter can see a rush building (e.g. 14 Ready at once) and call for help before it jams (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-304.

## §30 Creator self-service, fraud & attribution integrity — P1 (NEW section in V2.5 — reconciled from Studio §28)

Source: Studio catalog §28 (Studio UC-305–UC-312, 8 cases), carried over and renumbered UC-310–UC-317. This section EXPANDS file UC-248 (fraud/attribution) and UC-252 (creator self-service) into the operating detail. RESOLVED in V2.7: attribution is **last-tagged-link-wins, LOCKED** (Mahendra, Nov 8, 2026). UC-313 was revised to state the locked rule. The rejected alternative — visit-opener-wins (the share that opened the buying visit earns; a later tap creates no second credit) — is recorded in UC-313 so it is not re-proposed. Build the locked rule, not the rejected one.

- **UC-310** Give each creator a self-service view behind their own magic-link sign-in, scoped to themselves only: Connect onboarding status, earnings per Drop, payout schedule and next payout date (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-305. Expands UC-252.
- **UC-311** Show creators a pick list of operator-approved Drops in their area with the exact per-order share and any per-Drop creator cap; owned-only Drops never appear, and a Drop with distribution off has no creator link and no commission (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-306. Refines UC-209/UC-211.
- **UC-312** Keep one permanent tiny creator link dealspro.ai/c/<handle> — Drop links generate under it automatically; creators never manage per-Drop links (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-307. Same rule as UC-211 (proposal only).
- **UC-313** Attribute one order to exactly one credit, read from the server-side visit log: **last-tagged-link-wins, LOCKED (Mahendra, Nov 8, 2026 — V2.7)** — the creator whose tagged link was tapped last before purchase earns the credit; a later tap on another creator's tagged link supersedes an earlier one, and a later untagged visit creates no second credit and does not displace the last tag (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-308; **REVISED in V2.7.** REJECTED alternative, recorded, not live: visit-opener-wins (the share that opened the buying visit earns; a later tap creates no second credit) — Mahendra picked last-tagged-link-wins instead. The attribution rule is no longer to decide — see the §30 note above and UC-248.
- **UC-314** Check every creator-attributed order for self-dealing (buyer phone and payment fingerprint vs the creator): a self-purchase counts as a restaurant sale with the creator share zeroed and the creator flagged (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-309. Expands UC-248.
- **UC-315** Freeze only the contested order's creator share while an attribution dispute is open; restaurant payouts are never held for a creator dispute (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-310.
- **UC-316** Let the operator decide a dispute from the visit log in Studio, with the decision and its reason audit-logged and the held share released or reversed accordingly (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-311.
- **UC-317** Show creators their held / disputed amounts as held — never as earned — so self-service numbers always match what Connect will pay (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-312.

## §31 Support at volume — P1 (NEW section in V2.5 — reconciled from Studio §29)

Source: Studio catalog §29 (Studio UC-313–UC-320, 8 cases), carried over and renumbered UC-318–UC-325. This section EXPANDS file UC-249 (support queue) into the operating detail.

- **UC-318** Turn every customer or restaurant problem into a ticket with an owner, an age clock and an SLA — problems are resolved in a system, not in the operator's personal messages (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-313. Expands UC-249.
- **UC-319** Let a buyer open support from their order text / QR page, attached to that order automatically — never via a personal phone number (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-314.
- **UC-320** Give tickets real resolution actions, not just replies: refund (via Money), QR reissue (via Orders), pickup message to the customer or restaurant, each recorded on the order (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-315.
- **UC-321** Escalate a ticket that passes its SLA to the backup operator automatically, with the full order context attached (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-316. Refines UC-220.
- **UC-322** Hand a ticket between operator and backup in one action, audit-logged, without losing its history (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-317.
- **UC-323** Auto-create a ticket for system-detected problems: unclaimed orders after grace, failed scans repeated on one ticket, payout exceptions (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-318.
- **UC-324** Show open-ticket counts and the oldest ticket on Today so support debt is as visible as the publish queue (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-319.
- **UC-325** Keep one thread per order that the customer can see the resolution of (refunded, reissued, picked up) without exposing internal notes (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-320.

The cap is still to decide (open item 14) — do not build it as locked.

## §33 Chains & multi-location — P2 (NEW section in V2.5 — reconciled from Studio §31)

Source: Studio catalog §31 (Studio UC-329–UC-335, 7 cases), carried over and renumbered UC-334–UC-340. This section EXPANDS file UC-251 (chain/group model) into the operating detail.

- **UC-334** Model a chain as a parent brand with locations under it; a Drop is always created, sold and settled by exactly one location, never by the brand (P2) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-329. Expands UC-251.
- **UC-335** Share at brand level only what is genuinely shared: agreed commission terms, menu and approved dish videos, brand notes (P2) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-330.
- **UC-336** Keep per location, always: its own Stripe Connect account and payouts, its own two tiny URLs (promote + add), its own Smart Menu, pickup board, reliability record and removal state (P2) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-331. Refines UC-235 and UC-246.
- **UC-337** Onboard an additional location of a known brand with the brand paperwork pre-filled but every location step still required (Connect onboarding, pickup setup, first-Drop approval) (P2) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-332.
- **UC-338** Roll Money reporting up by brand while always breaking settlement out per location — a brand total is a view, never a payout (P2) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-333. Refines UC-217.
- **UC-339** Removing or pausing one location never affects its siblings' links, menus or payouts (P2) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-334. Refines UC-200.
- **UC-340** Show single-location partners as independent — the chain model appears only where a brand actually has more than one location (P2) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio UC-335.

## §35 Studio detail cases — Smart Menu, tiny URLs, video gate, tech stack, sign-in & QR at scale — P0 (NEW section in V2.5 — reconciled from Studio §§19–24)

Source: 22 detail cases from Studio §§19–24 that the file compresses into single cases or locked rules and never numbers separately. Each case below names its Studio source ID. The rest of Studio §§19–24 is NOT lost: every other Studio case in those sections is already covered by a file case or locked rule — see the coverage mapping in "Biggest changes in V2.5" (Studio §3 UC-031/032 → UC-021 + preview lock; §4 UC-043/044 → UC-038/UC-036; §9 UC-095/096 → UC-228/UC-021; §11 UC-117 → UC-105; §12 UC-118/119/126 → UC-111/UC-112/UC-119; §15 UC-162 → UC-353, UC-163 → UC-031-equivalent in §3; §16 UC-174/175 → UC-155/UC-230; §17 UC-185–187 → UC-174/UC-175/UC-176; §19 UC-206–215 → UC-235/UC-174/UC-236/UC-237/UC-238/UC-176/UC-239/UC-240/UC-203; §20 UC-222/224/225/228/229 → UC-233/UC-030/UC-036/UC-112; §21 UC-232/234–238/241 → UC-021/UC-241/UC-090/UC-064; §22 UC-246/248–251/253–255 → UC-227/UC-230/UC-231/UC-232 + open items 13/14; §23 UC-256/258–265 → UC-229/UC-155/UC-223; §24 UC-266–270/272–277 → UC-036/UC-038/UC-105/UC-232).

- **UC-348** Validate the private ADD code server-side; the link record (active / rotated / revoked) lives server-side, rotating issues a new tiny code and kills the old one instantly, and revoking the ADD link never affects the PROMOTE link (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §19 UC-216. Refines UC-203/UC-235.

- **UC-350** Preview the public Smart Menu in Studio exactly as customers see it — 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 and sold-out states — with Copy link on the card (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §19 UC-218.
- **UC-351** Serve Smart Menu pages cache-friendly (CDN / static shell) so a counter-QR rush does not hit the database per view; live inventory truth is checked at claim (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §19 UC-219.
- **UC-352** Take the Smart Menu offline and revoke the ADD link when a restaurant is removed from Studio, with its history kept and Restore available (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §19 UC-220. Refines UC-200.
- **UC-353** Alert the operator when a partner Smart Menu sits at 0 live Drops beyond the restaurant's normal rhythm (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §19 UC-221. Same alert as Studio §15 UC-162.
- **UC-354** Generate short codes server-side: unique, collision-checked, and unguessable (enough entropy that changing a character never resolves to another Drop, menu, or order) (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §20 UC-223. Refines UC-233.
- **UC-355** Never place a token or credential in a URL — the ADD code's validity is a server-side record, and no link of any kind carries a key parameter (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §20 UC-226. Refines UC-233.
- **UC-356** Resolve every tiny code server-side to its target record, so internal slugs and ids stay internal and can change without breaking printed links (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §20 UC-227.
- **UC-357** Keep the PROMOTE code for a location stable across Drop rotations, so printed posters and bio links never need reprinting when Drops change (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §20 UC-230.
- **UC-358** Log resolution of tiny codes (code, target, channel / creator where known) so Outcomes can attribute without long URLs (P1) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §20 UC-231.
- **UC-359** Allow a photo-only Drop to exist as a draft or in review, with publish disabled and the missing-video reason visible to the operator (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §21 UC-233. Refines UC-021.
- **UC-360** Pin the exact approved media version to a published Drop — later library edits never silently change a live Drop (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §21 UC-239. File UC-090 states this as Preview; this case makes it a numbered requirement.
- **UC-361** Show the poster and alert the operator if a live Drop's video becomes unavailable; never swap in a different dish's video (P0) — NEW in V2.5, reconciled from Studio §21 UC-240.
