## §36 DealsPro website & homepage — P0 (NEW section in V2.6)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided (open items 18–21 do not cover this section; §36 itself is proposed — Mahendra asked M2 to develop the homepage from M2's expected design, and has not yet agreed it).** Audit gap #1: the customer-facing website had no use case at all. This section specifies the website as a whole and the homepage layout as a written spec. The Studio is operator-facing (magic link, UC-229); the website is customer-facing — no login, phone is identity (UC-382), red DROP design (design lock), tiny URLs (UC-233). The live site named in MEMORY is www.dealspro.ai ("This week in Frisco", city-scoped, SMS alert signup with consent checkbox). "DealsPro home page" and "DealsPro website" are the same system: home is the front page of the website; the website also holds Drop pages, smart-menu pages, and order pages.

- **UC-370** The DealsPro website is the customer-facing site, separate from the Studio (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. One site, three page types: (a) home (dealspro.ai), (b) restaurant smart-menu pages (UC-174, UC-234–UC-242), (c) single Drop pages (UC-024–UC-026, §3 preview=live lock) plus the customer order page (UC-277 timeline). Same repo and same Vercel project as the Studio in the initial version (UC-227/UC-230/UC-231), same Supabase backend, two environments (staging site vs dealspro.ai, UC-227) — but a different audience and a different surface: customers never see the Studio, operators never work from a Drop page. A change to the website never changes a Studio screen, and vice versa, except through the shared Drop record.
- **UC-371** Homepage layout — city-scoped, "This week in [City]" (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. M2's expected design, for Mahendra to correct. Top to bottom: (1) header — DealsPro wordmark (red DROP accent), city picker showing the current city (default: Frisco, in DFW — the live site's scope; the visitor can change city, and the choice is remembered on that device); (2) hero line — "This week in Frisco" with the current week's pickup window dates; (3) LIVE NOW — Drops claimable right now, each as a card with video hero (with the UC-469 Hero Dish tag overlaid on top of the picture, immediately next to the red DROP badge, on a hero-dish Drop — REVISED in V2.12), restaurant name, dish, Bundle/Non-bundle + Serves, price, real scarcity ("Only N left"), and pickup window in big type; (4) COMING SOON — Drops published but not yet claimable / scheduled, with their pickup window and a "Notify me" alert signup (UC-214, unchecked by default); (5) RESTAURANTS — the partner restaurants in this city/zone, each with its smart-menu link, cuisine, and its next Drop; (6) HOW IT WORKS — three steps (Claim → Pay → Pick up with QR), in one line each; (7) footer — support link (UC-249 help page), opt-in / STOP information, and the DealsPro promise (finite, prepaid, pickup). No email signup anywhere on the homepage (NO email as a distribution channel).
- **UC-372** Homepage live-Drop feed — ordering and grouping (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Live Drops are ordered by pickup window (earliest window first), grouped by restaurant only when a restaurant has more than one live Drop; a restaurant's Drops stay together, in window order. Each card shows the same live state as the Drop page (one shared component, §3 lock): a claim in one place moves counts everywhere. A Drop that sells out on the homepage shows "Sold out" on its card in place of the Claim button (same rule as UC-176 on the smart menu) and stays visible until its pickup window ends, then drops off the homepage.
- **UC-373** Homepage for a new visitor vs a returning visitor (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. There is no customer login, so the homepage cannot greet a returning visitor by name. What changes for a returning visitor is device-level only: the city they last picked is remembered, and — if they previously completed a claim on this device/browser — a "Your orders" link appears in the header, opening their order page by phone number (UC-382/UC-384). Nothing else on the homepage personalises: the feed, the ordering, and the Drops shown are the same for new and returning visitors. Any design that shows a returning visitor different Drops or prices is forbidden — the homepage is one feed for the city, not a personalised feed.
- **UC-374** Homepage with 0 live Drops in the city — never an empty page (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Show "Next Drop coming soon in [City]" with the next scheduled Drop (if any) and its pickup window, the restaurants in the city, and an alert signup (SMS / WhatsApp, unchecked by default, per-channel, UC-214) — the homepage version of the UC-236 smart-menu rule. The signup records which city (and, if the visitor arrived from a restaurant page, which restaurant) they want alerts for (UC-387). A homepage with no live Drops and no signup is a dead end and is forbidden.
- **UC-375** Homepage sold-out and ended Drops (P1) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. A sold-out live Drop stays on the homepage until its pickup window ends, marked "Sold out" with no buy button — it proves demand (same rule as UC-176). An ended Drop (window passed) leaves the homepage and is never purchasable; a visitor arriving on its old link lands on the ended-state Drop page (UC-378), not on a blank page.
- **UC-376** Homepage navigation — to a smart menu vs to a single Drop page, with the creator/channel tag surviving (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Tapping a restaurant name or "View all Drops from [restaurant]" goes to that restaurant's smart-menu page (UC-234); tapping a Drop card goes to that Drop's page. The creator tag and the arrival-channel tag travel with the navigation, server-side (UC-111/UC-112/UC-239): a creator link that lands on the homepage and then buys any Drop from that visit is still the creator's sale — unless the customer taps a different creator's tagged link before purchase (last-tagged-link-wins, LOCKED, UC-248) — and the channel the visitor arrived through is the channel offered for opt-in (UC-386). A tag lost on navigation is a lost attribution and is forbidden.
- **UC-377** Homepage search and browse — by cuisine, price, serves, and pickup day (P1) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. A visitor can filter the live feed by cuisine, by price band, by Serves (1 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 / 6+), and by pickup day (today / tomorrow / this week). Filters never hide a live Drop permanently — clearing the filter restores the full feed — and a filter with no matching live Drop shows the UC-374 empty state for that filter, with the alert signup. No filter is a default: the homepage loads unfiltered, ordered by pickup window (UC-372).
- **UC-378** Ended / expired Drop page — what an old link shows (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. A customer with a link to a Drop whose pickup window has passed sees: "This Drop has ended", the dish and restaurant, and navigation to live Drops — the restaurant's smart menu first, then other live Drops in the city (the Oct 26 layout improvement, now a numbered case). The creator/channel tag survives to the next Drop they buy from that visit (UC-376). The ended page never offers a Claim button, never shows a price as if buyable, and never pretends the Drop is still live.
- **UC-379** The customer order page is part of the website, not the Studio (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Every paid order has an order page on the website (tiny URL, UC-233), reachable from the confirmation screen, the QR message, and "Your orders" (UC-373). It shows: the status timeline (Claimed → Accepted → Ready → Picked up, UC-277), the countdown (UC-278), the exact pickup window, the restaurant name/address/pickup instructions, the QR and the ticket code (UC-268 fallback), the cancel state and refund state (UC-254/UC-255, UC-188), and the support link (UC-249). The order page is the customer's single source of truth for their order; no order state is shown only in the Studio.
- **UC-380** Website on mobile and in-app browsers — fast, video-first, Safari nudge (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. The website is mobile-first for customers as the Studio is for operators: video heroes play muted and loop on mobile, the Claim button is a sticky bottom bar, tap targets are 44px+, and the claim sheet is a bottom sheet. In the Instagram in-app browser, where Apple Pay is blocked, the page shows the Safari nudge (open in Safari / real browser) before the payment step (UC-114) — card payment in-app is offered as the fallback, and the nudge copy, button, and fallback are a designed step, not an afterthought (audit G-05). The website loads fast enough that a creator-link tap does not lose the visitor: video poster first, video second, never a blank hero.
- **UC-381** Website environments, deployment, and promotion — staging site vs dealspro.ai (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. The website ships in both environments from day one (UC-227): staging.dealspro.ai (staging Supabase, Stripe test mode, SMS mocked) and dealspro.ai (prod Supabase, Stripe live). The full test Drop (UC-227/UC-363) runs on the staging website before any promotion. Nothing promotes to prod without Mahendra's approval (UC-364), and the website carries an environment badge in staging so a test claim is never mistaken for a real one. Refines UC-227 for the website specifically (G-107).

## §37 Customer identity & opt-in recognition — P0 (NEW section in V2.6)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided (open item 19).** Research note in Traceability, below. Audit gaps #3 (G-18, G-47, G-48) and #10 (G-36–G-41). Phone is identity (UC-229 note) is now specified as a system: how a phone is entered, normalised, looked up, verified, recognised on return, and how opt-in is checked before every ask. Marketing consent is SEPARATE from transactional order texts (UC-214) — nothing in this section changes that; this section specifies how the system knows who it is talking to, so it can honour it.

- **UC-382** Phone number is the customer's identity — no login, no password, no account (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. A customer is identified by their phone number, normalised to E.164 (+1 + 10 digits for US numbers) server-side at entry. There is no customer password, no email login, and no DealsPro account to create or recover. The phone number is the single lookup key for the customer record (name, order history, per-channel opt-in status) — the same phone-only identity pattern used by phone-first checkouts (see research note: IndiSell "phone number becomes the identity, an OTP replaces the password"; SimplyClub fast login "Identity … is phone-only — there is no email or customer-id fallback"). **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-398, mismatch fix):** The phone lookup uses a HASHED lookup key (hash of the normalised E.164 number) — the plain phone number is never the lookup key in logs, indexes, or URLs, and never appears in a log line for a lookup.
- **UC-383** At checkout the customer enters their phone number and first name — and ownership is verified (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. The claim sheet asks for phone number (required) and first name (required; full name is never required — pickup needs a first name and phone last digits, UC-247). Phone ownership is verified by a one-time code sent to that number on FIRST claim from that phone (6-digit code, 120-second expiry, per-phone rate caps against OTP flooding — the SimplyClub pattern in the research note), because a mistyped number sends the QR nowhere and there is no other way to prove the number is theirs. Whether OTP-on-first-claim is acceptable friction is TO CONFIRM (open item 19) — if Mahendra rejects OTP, the fallback is phone entry alone with the QR sent to that number and a "wrong number? re-enter" step on the confirmation screen, and this case must be revised before build. OTP codes are never stored in plain text and never shown in the Studio.
- **UC-384** A returning phone number is recognised — name pre-filled, past orders shown, nothing re-entered (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. When a phone number already exists in DealsPro, the server looks it up on entry: the customer's first name is pre-filled (editable), their past orders are shown ("Your orders", UC-373/UC-379), and they are never asked for their name again. Recognition is by normalised phone number alone — it does not depend on the device, the browser, or a cookie; a returning customer on a new phone browser is recognised the moment they type their number. A returning customer on the same device/browser is recognised without re-verifying by OTP (session/device recognition — PROPOSED, open item 19). Two people sharing one phone share one customer record and one order history; the system does not pretend to tell them apart (audit G-18). **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-400, mismatch fix):** A personal order-link and a device token may be used as a PRE-FILL HINT ONLY — they may pre-fill the phone/name on the claim sheet, but they never identify the customer. Recognition remains by normalised phone number alone (UC-382): a hint that disagrees with the typed phone is ignored, and a hint is never a substitute for the phone lookup.
- **UC-385** Opt-in is checked server-side before EVERY ask — if already opted in on that channel, never ask again (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. This is the core rule Mahendra asked for. Before any opt-in prompt is shown — on a Drop page, on a smart menu, after a claim, anywhere — the server looks up (phone E.164 + channel) in the consent record (UC-388). If the status is opt-in for that channel, NO prompt is shown, no checkbox appears, no "would you like alerts?" message is sent. The check is server-side, not a page-level "hide if we think they opted in": a prompt that is hidden only in the browser can reappear on another device, and a prompt that is asked again after opt-in is a violation of the product decision (MEMORY, Oct 26: "Already opted in: never ask again"). The check runs on every ask, every time, for every channel separately. **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-401, mismatch fix):** The server-side lookup runs BEFORE the prompt is rendered, not after — the page never renders an opt-in prompt and then hides it. A prompt that flashes on screen before the lookup returns is a violation of this case.
- **UC-386** Opt-in is offered at Drop level, AFTER a successful claim, on the channel they arrived through — and only if not already opted in (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. After a claim succeeds, the confirmation screen offers Drop-alert opt-in for the channel the customer arrived through (SMS arrival → SMS opt-in; WhatsApp arrival → WhatsApp opt-in; Instagram arrival → the customer is offered SMS or WhatsApp opt-in at checkout, because Instagram has no phone until checkout — audit G-37 is NOT solved for Instagram DMs, open item 19). The checkbox is unchecked by default (UC-214). If UC-385 already finds them opted in on that channel, the offer is not shown at all. The offer is never shown before payment (UC-214) and never shown after a failed claim or a cancelled order (audit G-41).
- **UC-387** Opt-in is also offered on the restaurant (smart-menu) page — with a stated scope, and only if not already opted in (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Two smart-menu opt-in points: (a) the 0-live-Drops signup (UC-236) — "Next Drop coming soon" + alert signup; (b) a "Get alerts for this restaurant" offer on a smart menu with live Drops. In both cases the scope is stated in the offer itself: alerts for THIS restaurant only (not all Drops in the city). The UC-385 server-side check applies here too — an already-opted-in visitor sees no prompt, only a "You're signed up for [restaurant] alerts" state. The smart-menu signup and the post-claim opt-in (UC-386) write to the SAME consent record (same phone + channel), never to two lists: a customer who signs up on three smart menus and claims a Drop is one consent record, and the UC-250 frequency cap counts them once (audit G-40). **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-402, mismatch fix):** The smart-menu offer states its SCOPE dimension in the offer itself — alerts for THIS restaurant only, or for the city, never an unstated scope — and an already-opted-in visitor sees a quiet confirmation line ('You're signed up for [restaurant] alerts'), never a prompt and never a second signup form.
- **UC-388** The consent record — what is stored, per phone + channel (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. One record per (phone E.164, channel): status (opt-in / opt-out / never-asked), source (which page and which offer — Drop claim, smart-menu signup, homepage signup), timestamp of the last change, the exact disclosure text version shown at consent, and the channel (SMS / WhatsApp / RCS — separate records per channel, UC-214). Both answers are stored, not just yes: a decline is recorded as "asked, declined, [timestamp]" so "they said no" is distinguishable from "nobody asked them" (the Twilio Consent Management API model in the research note: contact_id = phone in E.164, status opt-in/opt-out, source, date_of_consent). Consent records are kept for audit (UC-250 send log + this consent record together answer a TCPA dispute — audit G-100) and are never shown with the phone number in the Studio beyond the UC-212 read view.
- **UC-389** STOP is per-channel, honoured immediately, and re-opt-in requires a fresh consent event (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Replying STOP to an SMS stops SMS marketing only; WhatsApp and RCS are separate records and are unaffected unless the customer stops those too (UC-214 per-channel rule; audit G-39 resolved as per-channel). STOP is honoured immediately, the customer gets one confirmation message ("You're unsubscribed. No more texts. Reply START to resubscribe."), and all further marketing sends to that phone+channel are blocked at send time (Twilio error 21610 behaviour — see research note). Re-opt-in (reply START, or ticking the box again) is recorded as a NEW consent event with a fresh timestamp and disclosure version — it clears both the Messaging Service-level and the sender-level opt-out records (Twilio requires both to be cleared). Transactional order texts (QR, Ready, pickup) are NEVER blocked by a marketing STOP — they are a separate consent basis (UC-214, UC-267).
- **UC-390** Ask-once: an existing buyer without marketing consent is asked once, the decline is remembered, and the system does not nag (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. If a customer declines the opt-in offer (or ignores it), the decline is recorded (UC-388) and the system does not ask them again on the next claim. "Ask once" is the rule (MEMORY, Oct 26); the cooldown before any re-ask, if one is ever allowed, is TO DECIDE with no number in this spec (open item 19). What counts as an ask is defined: showing the opt-in offer on the confirmation screen counts as one ask, even if the customer ignores it. A builder that asks on every claim complies with UC-214's "remembered" wording while violating this case — this case governs.
- **UC-391** Cross-channel identity — one customer, one record, however they arrive (P1) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. The same phone number arriving by SMS, WhatsApp, or RCS is the same customer record (UC-382): order history, opt-in status per channel, and repeat-buyer status are shared. An Instagram arrival has no phone until checkout; at checkout, when the phone is entered, the visit is stitched to the existing customer record for that phone (including creator attribution for that visit — last-tagged-link-wins, LOCKED, UC-248/UC-239/UC-376). Without stitching, "repeat buyer" counts (UC-213, north-star metric) are wrong and the UC-250 cap is unenforceable — the same person would be messaged on two channels as two people (audit G-48). The stitching key is the normalised phone number only; no device fingerprinting and no third-party identity graph.
- **UC-392** Phone number changed, or a number entered wrong — correction and merge (P1) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. A customer who mistypes their phone at checkout can correct it on the confirmation screen before paying (the QR goes to the corrected number; the wrong number keeps no record). A customer whose number has changed contacts support (UC-249); the operator merges the old record into the new one — order history and opt-in status move to the new number, the old number's record is marked merged, and the merge is audit-logged. A merge never merges two different people's orders: if the old number has orders under a different first name, the operator confirms with the customer before merging (audit G-50).
- **UC-393** The QR and order texts always go to the phone number entered, even if the customer is already opted in — transactional delivery is never gated on marketing opt-in (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. A returning, already-opted-in customer still enters their phone number at checkout (so the QR goes to the right phone — Mahendra's point), and the QR/ticket, the "Restaurant accepted" message, the Ready message, and the pickup confirmation are sent to that number on the channel they ordered from, whether or not they are opted in to marketing. Marketing opt-in status never blocks, delays, or changes a transactional message. If the number they enter differs from the number on their customer record, the QR goes to the entered number and the operator sees both numbers on the order (audit-logged).

- **UC-432** Exactly three consent scopes in one registry — Drop, smart menu, homepage — and a wider scope suppresses narrower asks (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED (mismatch fix, Studio UC-405). The consent registry (UC-388) holds exactly three scopes: (a) Drop scope — alerts for one Drop, offered after a claim (UC-386); (b) restaurant scope — alerts for one restaurant, offered on its smart menu (UC-387); (c) city scope — alerts for Drops in one city, offered on the homepage (UC-371/UC-374). A wider scope suppresses narrower asks: a customer opted in at city scope is never asked at restaurant or Drop scope on that channel, and a customer opted in at restaurant scope is never asked at Drop scope for that restaurant on that channel. A narrower opt-in never implies a wider one: a Drop-scope opt-in does not sign the customer up for the restaurant or the city. The scope is stored on the consent record (UC-388) alongside phone + channel, and the UC-385 server-side check checks scope as well as channel before every ask.

## §38 Accept deadline, 48-hour lead & restaurant confirmation — P0 (NEW section in V2.6)

**Status: MIXED — the 48-hour lead (UC-395, UC-397, UC-398) is LOCKED (Mahendra, Nov 8, 2026 — V2.7); the claim-accept deadline (UC-394) and the offer-confirmation deadline (UC-402) remain PROPOSED / TO DECIDE (open item 18).** Audit gaps #2 (G-24, G-28) and #7 (G-62, G-63). This section gives §26 the two numbers it was missing — the claim-accept deadline and the publish-to-pickup lead time — and gives the restaurant-confirmation human gate a concrete mechanism. It refines §26 (UC-270–UC-282) and §7 (UC-067–UC-069); it does not replace them. The 48-hour lead is LOCKED by Mahendra (Nov 8, 2026); the accept-deadline number is M2's proposal for him to confirm.

- **UC-394** Every Drop has a claim-accept deadline — how long the restaurant has to accept a paid claim (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. PROPOSED default: **2 hours from the moment the claim is paid**, and — if the claim arrives less than 2 hours before the pickup window opens — **no later than 30 minutes before the window opens**. The deadline is computed per order, shown on the order (customer order page, UC-379; restaurant pickup queue, UC-247; Studio, UC-221 waiting clock), and counted down in the Studio. When the deadline passes with no accept, UC-279 (delay message + operator alert) and UC-281 (escalation to the operator) run. The 2-hour / 30-minute numbers are PROPOSALS — Mahendra to confirm (open item 18). Until he confirms, do not hard-code them into a build. **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-397, mismatch fix):** BOTH accept clocks are shown everywhere the deadline appears — the per-claim clock (proposed 2 hours from claim) AND the final accept-by (no later than 30 minutes before the window opens) — on the customer order page, the restaurant pickup queue, and the Studio waiting clock. Showing only one clock hides the binding one.
- **UC-395** The pickup window must start at least 48 hours after the Drop is published — the operator controls the dates, and the system enforces the lead (P0) — NEW in V2.6; **LOCKED in V2.7 (Mahendra, Nov 8, 2026).** **48 hours minimum lead time from publish to pickup-window start — no same-day and no next-day selection**, so there is time to market the Drop and so customers can pick up ahead of time. This is a lock, not a proposal: a build must enforce it (subject only to the per-Drop operator override in UC-396). The rule is enforced at publish: the publish action is blocked if the pickup window starts less than 48 hours after the publish time, and the block states the earliest allowed window. The operator sets the dates (publish date, pickup window) on the Drop; the restaurant does not set them — a restaurant-created Drop proposes dates, the operator sets the final ones in review (UC-062/UC-071).
- **UC-396** Operator override of the 48-hour lead — explicit, reasoned, audit-logged, operator only (P0) — NEW in V2.6; the 48-hour rule it overrides is **LOCKED in V2.7**. The operator (Mahendra — not the backup, not the restaurant, UC-222/UC-223) may override the 48-hour rule for a specific Drop, by stating a reason at publish time (e.g. a same-week repeat Drop for a proven restaurant). The override, the reason, and the operator are audit-logged (UC-158/UC-263) and shown on the Drop record. An override is per Drop, never a standing setting; a restaurant cannot request an override through its create link — it can only propose dates, and the operator decides.
- **UC-397** The accept-deadline and lead-time fields are present in BOTH create flows (P0) — NEW in V2.6; the 48-hour lead part is **LOCKED in V2.7**, the claim-accept deadline default is still PROPOSED (UC-394). Operator-created Drops (UC-011–UC-020) and restaurant-created Drops (UC-203–UC-204) both carry the same fields: pickup window, publish date/time, claim-accept deadline (defaulted from UC-394, editable by the operator only), and the computed lead time (pickup start minus publish time, shown live as the dates are entered). A restaurant-created submission without dates, or with a window less than 48 hours after its proposed publish time, is not rejected outright — it lands in Review & Queue flagged "lead time under 48h — operator decision" (UC-062), and the operator sets the final dates or overrides (UC-396) in review. The deadline is never a hidden default: it is shown in the review drawer (UC-064) before approval. **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-389/390, mismatch fix):** Review and scheduling are BLOCKED while the 48-hour lead is not met (unless the UC-396 operator override is recorded), and the block names the shortfall in hours ('Pickup is 31 hours out — 17 hours short of the 48-hour lead'). The submission clock starts the moment the submission ARRIVES in Review & Queue, not when the operator first opens it.
- **UC-398** The 48-hour lead is marketing time — publishing starts the clock, and alerts go out on publish (P0) — NEW in V2.6; the 48-hour lead it describes is **LOCKED in V2.7**. The reason for the lead is stated in the case so a builder does not treat it as an arbitrary delay: 48 hours is the minimum time to market the Drop (creator distribution, UC-209–UC-211; SMS/WhatsApp alerts to opted-in customers, UC-214/UC-250; restaurant promo kit, UC-240) and for customers to claim and plan pickup ahead of time. On publish, the Drop appears on the homepage (UC-372) and the smart menu (UC-234), creator links go live, and the alert send (if any) is scheduled — the lead time is used, not wasted. A Drop published with a 48-hour lead but never distributed is a missed Drop, and the Studio shows distribution status per Drop (UC-119).
- **UC-399** Restaurant confirmation mechanism — the human gate, made concrete (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. This is the mechanism for UC-067 (record confirmation with identity + timestamp) and for UC-272 (explicit accept). The restaurant confirms/accepts through one of three channels, all writing the same record: (a) the private pickup link (UC-264) — Accept / Decline buttons per order, Accept All for the Drop; (b) reply to the claim text/WhatsApp — ACCEPT or DECLINE (with a reason for DECLINE); (c) the Studio Orders & Pickup board (operator or restaurant on a counter tablet). Opening the link, reading the text, or doing nothing is never acceptance (UC-272). If confirmation happens by phone call, the operator logs it in the Studio as an operator-recorded confirmation with the caller's name and time — the record is the operator's entry, not the call itself. **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-417, mismatch fix):** The private pickup confirm link is OFFER-VERSION-SCOPED — it carries the offer version it confirms, and an accept recorded against an old version does not confirm a changed offer (see UC-208 auto-void). A confirm link with no offer version is not a valid confirm link.
- **UC-400** Confirmation record format — who, when, how, per order (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Every accept and every decline records, per order: the restaurant, the order, the actor (who at the restaurant accepted — name from the pickup link or the replying number — or "operator, on behalf of [name]" for a logged call), the timestamp, the channel (pickup link / text reply / WhatsApp reply / Studio board / operator-logged call), and, for a decline, the reason (UC-401). The record is shown on the order, in the pickup queue (UC-247), and in the audit log (UC-158/UC-263). An accept with no actor and no timestamp is not an accept — the system rejects it. **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-420, mismatch fix):** Every confirmation records an OFFER FINGERPRINT — a hash of price, quantity, pickup window, video, and dish/portion as confirmed — alongside who/when/how. Two confirmations with different fingerprints are confirmations of different offers, and the Studio shows the fingerprint so a changed offer is visible at a glance.
- **UC-401** Decline reasons — a stated taxonomy, and the customer sees a useful reason (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Decline reasons are a fixed list, not free text: (a) ran out of the dish / ingredient, (b) cannot prep in time for the window, (c) restaurant closed / unavailable for this window, (d) wrong dish or portion in the Drop, (e) other (with a short note). The customer message (UC-273) states the reason in plain words and the remedy — refund on its way, and rebook to the next Drop where the reason is (a) or (b). A decline with no reason is not accepted by the system: the Decline button requires a reason to be picked. Decline reasons feed the reliability record (UC-154/UC-259).
- **UC-402** Offer-confirmation deadline — how long a restaurant has to confirm the exact offer before a scheduled publish passes (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Distinct from the claim-accept deadline (UC-394): this is the UC-067 offer confirmation. A Drop whose exact offer is not confirmed by its scheduled publish time does NOT publish: it stays in Review & Queue flagged "awaiting restaurant confirmation — publish missed", the operator is alerted (UC-410), and the operator either re-schedules it (new publish time, 48-hour lead check — LOCKED, UC-395) or returns it to draft. The deadline value itself — how many hours before the scheduled publish the confirmation must arrive — is TO DECIDE with no number proposed in this spec (open item 18); until Mahendra sets it, the rule is "confirmed before publish, or it does not publish".
- **UC-403** Prep lead time — an accept that arrives too late to prep is not a usable accept (P1) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. An accept recorded less than 30 minutes before the pickup window opens (the same 30-minute floor as UC-394) flags the order "accepted late — prep at risk" in the pickup queue and alerts the operator (UC-410); the restaurant is asked on the pickup link whether it can still prep in time, and if it cannot, the order is declined under UC-273 (refund + message) rather than left as an accepted order that cannot be fulfilled. This is the prep-time rule audit G-25 asked for: accept without the ability to prep is not acceptance. The 30-minute floor is part of the UC-394 proposal — confirm it with the accept deadline (open item 18).

- **UC-430** A reminder goes to the restaurant 10 minutes before a claim's accept deadline, on the same channel as the claim (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED (mismatch fix, Studio UC-393). Ten minutes before the UC-394 claim-accept deadline for an unaccepted order, the restaurant gets one reminder on the channel the claim arrived on (text/WhatsApp to the restaurant contact, UC-270) — "3 orders for Hyderabad House still awaiting accept — deadline in 10 minutes" with the pickup-link deep link. The reminder is sent once per order, never repeated, and is recorded on the order; it does not extend the deadline, and it does not replace the UC-279 delay message or the UC-281 escalation when the deadline passes.
- **UC-431** Customers may claim ahead for the whole claim window from go-live — the pickup date is shown in large type (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED (mismatch fix, Studio UC-396). Once a Drop is published (go-live), a customer may claim it at any time until the claim window closes — there is no "claim opens later" state for a published Drop — and the Drop page, smart menu, and homepage all show the PICKUP DATE in large type next to the claim button, so a customer claiming days ahead is never confused about which day they are picking up. The claim window closes at the start of the pickup window (or at sellout, whichever is first); after that, the Drop shows the UC-378 ended state, never a live Claim button.
- **UC-434** The confirmation state machine is explicit and shown everywhere — not requested / awaiting / confirmed / voided / deadline missed (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED (mismatch fix, Studio UC-422). Every order's restaurant-confirmation state is one of exactly five, with no other state and no silent transition: not requested (claim not yet sent), awaiting (claim sent, no accept/decline yet, UC-271), confirmed (accepted, UC-272/UC-399), voided (a prior confirmation voided by a material offer change, UC-208 — the order returns to awaiting a fresh confirmation), deadline missed (accept deadline passed, UC-281 escalation). The state is shown identically on the customer order page, the restaurant pickup queue, and the Studio, with its timestamp; a state shown in one place and a different state in another is a bug.

## §39 Reservation hold & per-customer cap — P0 (NEW section in V2.6)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided (open item 20).** Audit gaps #6 (G-17, G-22) and scale-report UC-259 (reservation hold, never adopted). **Correction, recorded in the file:** Mahendra recalled a 3-or-4 cap as "added in the past" — a full search of this catalog (V2.5) found NO per-customer cap case and NO reservation-hold case. UC-105 is atomic decrement at payment only; UC-249 lists "I paid but the Drop says sold out" as a support problem. The cap and the hold are therefore NEW in V2.6, not a strengthening of an existing case.

- **UC-404** Per-customer cap — one phone buys at most 3 units of one Drop (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. PROPOSED default: **3 units per phone number per Drop**. Mahendra said 3 or 4 (Nov 8, 2026) — 3 is the proposed default, TO CONFIRM (open item 20); if he picks 4, this case is revised to 4 before build. The cap is per normalised phone number (UC-382), per Drop, across all of that phone's orders for that Drop — not per order, not per device. A bundle Drop's "units" are the bundle units as priced (one bundle = one unit). The cap is enforced server-side at claim and at payment: a claim that would take a phone over the cap is refused with "Limit 3 per customer for this Drop" before payment is taken. **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-413, mismatch fix):** The cap is OPERATOR-SETTABLE to a maximum of 4 units per phone per Drop — PROPOSED, not locked. The operator may set 1, 2, 3, or 4 per Drop; the cap is never above 4, and the default remains 3 until Mahendra confirms 3 or 4 (open item 20). A per-Drop cap above 4 is forbidden by this case.
- **UC-405** Reservation hold — units are held while the customer pays (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. When a customer opens the claim sheet and picks a quantity, those units are held for a PROPOSED **10 minutes** (the scale report's proposal — TO CONFIRM, open item 20) while they enter their phone and pay. The hold is shown to the customer ("Held for you for 10:00 — complete payment to keep them", counting down). When the hold expires unpaid, the units are released back to inventory immediately and the claim sheet shows "No longer held — check availability". A hold is per phone + Drop + quantity; a customer cannot hold units on two devices at once for the same Drop (the second hold replaces the first). **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-409, mismatch fix):** The hold leaves the AVAILABLE COUNT everywhere in the same transaction — homepage, smart menu, Drop page, and Studio all show the same remaining count the moment a hold is placed or released. A hold that decrements the count in one place and not in another is a bug, not a delay.
- **UC-406** Two buyers cannot both pay for the last unit — the second is told sold out BEFORE payment (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Inventory decrements atomically at payment (UC-105), and the hold (UC-405) is the prevention: if Buyer A holds the last units and Buyer B tries to claim them, B sees "Sold out" (or the remaining quantity) before entering payment details. If a race still reaches payment — B's payment is attempted after A's payment has taken the last units — B's payment is NOT taken: the checkout stops before charging, B sees "Sorry — this Drop just sold out", and B is offered the next Drop from that restaurant (smart menu, UC-234). B is never charged for a sold-out Drop; if a charge ever does go through for a sold-out Drop, it is refunded automatically under UC-182/UC-262 and the incident is a support ticket (UC-249, "I paid but the Drop says sold out"). **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-410 and UC-412, mismatch fix):** The QR is born ONLY at the Stripe webhook — the ticket is issued when Stripe confirms payment, never on the client saying it paid — and a missed webhook is recovered by the webhook-reconciliation case (UC-438), so it is a recoverable order, not a lost one. A race loser who is told sold out is offered the alert signup for the next Drop from that restaurant (UC-236/UC-387), so a sold-out visitor is a future buyer, not a dead end.
- **UC-407** Cap and hold interact correctly with cancel and refund (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Cancelled (UC-255/UC-256) and refunded (UC-262) units do NOT count toward the cap — a customer who bought 3, cancelled 1, can buy 1 more. Held-but-unpaid units DO count toward the cap while the hold is live, and stop counting the moment the hold expires. A declined order (UC-273) frees its units and its cap count immediately. The cap count is computed server-side from paid + held units only, never from the claim sheet's display count.
- **UC-408** Quantity stepper respects the cap and the hold — the customer can never select more than they can buy (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. The claim sheet's quantity stepper is capped at the smallest of: units remaining, the per-customer cap minus units already bought/held by that phone (UC-404), and the hold quantity. The total price in the Claim button ( §3 lock) is computed from that capped quantity. A stepper that lets a customer pick 5 and then refuses at payment is a broken stepper — the refusal must happen at selection, not at payment.
- **UC-409** Oversell is impossible by construction, and if it ever happens it is handled, not hidden (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Atomic decrement (UC-105) + hold (UC-405) + cap (UC-404) together make oversell impossible in the normal path. If an oversell is ever detected (payment state vs inventory state disagree, UC-148), the system: stops new claims for that Drop immediately, refunds the oversold payment(s) automatically (UC-182), messages the affected customer(s) with the refund and the next Drop, and raises an operator alert (UC-410) with the exact orders. An oversold Drop is never resolved by silently keeping the money.

- **UC-433** Cap-evasion detection — a second number, the same payment fingerprint, or device velocity is fraud, not a new customer (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED (mismatch fix, Studio UC-416). The fraud rules (UC-248/UC-314) also flag cap evasion: two phone numbers buying the same Drop with the same payment fingerprint (same card / same Stripe payment method), or a burst of claims from one device / one IP across several numbers, is treated as one buyer evading the UC-404 cap. Flagged orders are held from creator payout (UC-248) and from pickup until the operator reviews them; the operator decides from the visit/payment log (UC-316) whether to release them, cancel them with a refund (UC-262), or keep the flag. A second number used by a genuinely different person (different payment method, different device) is not evasion — the flag is evidence for review, never an automatic cancel.

## §40 Operator alerts — delivery channel — P0 (NEW section in V2.6)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided (open item 21).** Audit gap #8 (G-77). §15 (UC-145–UC-154) specifies WHAT alerts the operator needs; this section specifies HOW they reach a mobile-web operator. In-Studio-only alerts are not enough: the operator must be told, on their phone, at the moment it matters — otherwise mobile-first (UC-224–UC-226, UC-243) fails at exactly the pickup-window moment.

- **UC-410** Operator alerts are delivered by SMS/WhatsApp to the operator's phone — not only in the Studio (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Every alert in §15 (approval required UC-145, publishing failed UC-146, inventory nearly exhausted UC-147, inconsistent payment/order UC-148, pickup window approaching UC-149, repeated redemption failures UC-150, media unavailable UC-151, order requiring assistance UC-152, repeated fulfillment problems UC-154) AND the §26 alerts (restaurant late to accept UC-279, no-accept escalation UC-281, restaurant late to Ready UC-280) AND payout/support failures (UC-245/UC-246 payout failed, UC-249 new support ticket) is delivered BOTH in the Studio (badge + Today, UC-219/UC-224) AND as a message to the operator's phone. The phone message is the primary channel for time-critical alerts; the Studio badge is the record. An alert that exists only as a Studio badge is an undelivered alert. **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-424, mismatch fix):** Alerts travel on THREE rails — SMS, WhatsApp, and mobile-web push — with SMS and WhatsApp as the delivery rails and push as the third, secondary rail only (UC-411: push is unreliable on iOS and is never the sole channel for a time-critical alert).
- **UC-411** Alert channel — SMS first, WhatsApp where the operator prefers it, SMS fallback (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Primary channel TO CONFIRM (open item 21): the proposal is SMS to the operator's mobile as the default, because SMS delivery does not depend on WhatsApp Business template approval (audit G-106) or on the operator having WhatsApp open; WhatsApp is the channel if the operator picks it in their Studio settings. If a WhatsApp alert is undelivered (no delivery receipt within 2 minutes), the same alert is sent by SMS. Mobile-web push notification is secondary only — it is unreliable on iOS (audit G-77) and is never the sole channel for a time-critical alert. No native app is needed for alerts (UC-243 stands): SMS/WhatsApp delivery is the alert channel, not a native push. **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-425, mismatch fix):** The P0/P1 TAXONOMY drives the channel: P0 alerts (payment, payout, webhook, oversell, QR redemption failure, restaurant late to accept, unclaimed at window end) go by SMS and WhatsApp; P1 alerts go by the operator's preferred channel and are batched on Today. A P0 alert sent on a P1 channel is an undelivered alert.
- **UC-412** Every alert carries what, which, how long, and where to act (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. An alert message states: what happened ("Restaurant has not accepted 3 orders for Hyderabad House — 12:00 pickup"), which Drop/order/restaurant, how long it has waited (waiting clock, UC-221), and a tiny link (UC-233) straight to that item in the Studio (the queue item, the order, the pickup board — not the Studio home). An alert without a deep link forces the operator to hunt for the item on their phone; that hunt is the delay the alert exists to prevent. Alert links are operator-only (magic-link session, UC-229) — they carry no credential in the URL (UC-355).
- **UC-413** An alert is not cleared until the item is acted on — acknowledge is not resolve (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Tapping an alert link and viewing the item acknowledges it (the badge clears for that item); the alert itself clears only when the underlying item is acted on — approved/published, accepted/declined, refunded, ticket resolved, payout retried. An acknowledged-but-unacted item re-alerts after a PROPOSED 30 minutes with its waiting clock (TO CONFIRM — open item 21, no number locked). An alert that clears on view alone will be viewed and forgotten; this case forbids that.
- **UC-414** Quiet hours — time-critical alerts have none during a live window; marketing alerts never go out at night (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. TO CONFIRM (open item 21): the proposal is — (a) alerts tied to a live pickup window, an unaccepted claim past its deadline, or a payment/refund failure are sent at any hour, because the window does not wait for morning; (b) non-urgent alerts (end-of-day summary UC-153, throughput flags UC-244, a smart menu at 0 Drops UC-353) wait until 7:00 AM local and are batched into the morning summary; (c) marketing alerts to customers are never sent 9:00 PM–9:00 AM local. Mahendra has not set quiet hours — until he does, these are proposals, not a schedule. **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-429, mismatch fix):** P1 (non-urgent) alerts are BATCHED after 21:00 local and delivered in the morning summary — they are never sent as individual night-time messages. This is the batching half of UC-414's quiet-hours proposal; time-critical alerts during a live window are unaffected and are sent at any hour.
- **UC-415** Alert to the backup after the 2-hour escalation — same content, same link, same urgency (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. When a queue item waits 2 hours (UC-220), the alert goes to the named backup approver by the same channel (UC-411), with the same content and deep link (UC-412), marked "Escalated — backup action needed". The backup sees the same queue, checklist, and blockers. Refunds, commission changes, and create-link revocation NEVER escalate (UC-222) — no alert for those goes to the backup, by design; they wait for the operator.
- **UC-416** Alert delivery is logged, and a failed alert retries and then escalates (P0) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. Every alert records: recipient, channel, item, time sent, delivery status, and whether it was acted on (UC-413). If an alert fails to deliver (SMS/WhatsApp error, no delivery receipt), it retries once on the fallback channel (UC-411) and, if that also fails, it is shown as a failed alert on Today (UC-219 coverage strip) and the backup is alerted. A failed alert that nobody sees is the same as no alert — the log exists so a missed pickup can be traced to a missed alert, not to a guess. **EXPANDED in V2.8 (Studio UC-430, mismatch fix):** Every alert delivery receipt is recorded, and the OLDEST unacted P0 alert is shown on Today with its waiting clock — so the most urgent unacted alert is always the first thing the operator sees, not the newest one.
- **UC-417** Alert settings live in the Studio, per operator — channel, backup, and quiet hours in one place (P1) — NEW in V2.6, PROPOSED. The operator sets, in the Studio (Team & Coverage, UC-219–UC-223): their alert phone number, their preferred channel (SMS / WhatsApp), the backup approver's number and channel, and quiet-hour preferences (UC-414). Settings are per operator, not per Drop. Changing the operator's alert number requires a magic-link confirmation to the operator's email (UC-229) — an alert number changed by anyone else would send every time-critical alert to the wrong phone.

## §41 Cities, metroplexes & nearby restaurants — P1 (NEW section in V2.7 — reconciled from Studio §41)

Source: Studio catalog §41 (Studio UC-428–UC-439, 12 cases), carried over and renumbered UC-418–UC-429 in file order. Case texts below are written from the Studio build's verified description (city browser, Nearby list, Today strip, working homepage city chips, closest-first strip) — the Studio's exact case wording was not available verbatim, so each case names its Studio source ID by position. Distances in the Studio are labelled SAMPLE miles from the Prosper (Legacy & Frontier) area; real distances come from the restaurant's address in the build (UC-004/UC-005). This section EXPANDS the city hierarchy (UC-195–UC-196, §1) and the homepage city scope (§36, UC-370–UC-381) into the operating detail; it does not replace them.

- **UC-418** Browse restaurants by city in the Studio: a full city browser under Restaurants · By City listing every DFW sub-city, each with its restaurants (P1) — NEW in V2.7, reconciled from Studio UC-428. The browser is the operator's geographic view of the pipeline and the live business — one tap on a city filters the restaurant list to that city (UC-195 hierarchy: State → Metroplex → Sub-city, no free-text city).
- **UC-419** Show per-city counts in the city browser: doing-business restaurants, pipeline leads, live Drops, and customers (P1) — NEW in V2.7, reconciled from Studio UC-429. Counts are live from the same records as the restaurant list, the queue, and Demand — a count that disagrees with its list is a bug, not a rounding difference.
- **UC-420** Show launch cities with 0 restaurants honestly — never hide them (P1) — NEW in V2.7, reconciled from Studio UC-430. A city with no restaurants yet appears in the browser with 0 counts and an honest "launch city — no restaurants yet" state, so the operator can see where expansion has not started instead of mistaking an absent city for a covered one.
- **UC-421** Show distance for each restaurant, labelled as sample miles from the Prosper (Legacy & Frontier) area in the prototype (P1) — NEW in V2.7, reconciled from Studio UC-431. Distance is computed from the restaurant's address (UC-004/UC-005); in the prototype it is sample data, in the build it must be a real distance, and the Studio must never present a sample distance as measured.
- **UC-422** Provide a Nearby list sorting active and pipeline restaurants by distance, closest first (P1) — NEW in V2.7, reconciled from Studio UC-432. The list crosses city lines — a restaurant 2 miles away in the next sub-city outranks one 8 miles away in the same sub-city — and shows both Doing Business and Pipeline entries, labelled as such, so the operator sees the real nearest supply, not the nearest same-city supply.
- **UC-423** Filter the Nearby list by radius: 3 / 5 / 10 miles (P1) — NEW in V2.7, reconciled from Studio UC-433. The radius is a segmented control, not free text; changing it re-sorts and re-counts the list immediately, and a radius with no restaurants in it shows an honest empty state, not the unfiltered list.
- **UC-424** Show a "Cities & nearby" strip on Today (P1) — NEW in V2.7, reconciled from Studio UC-434. Today opens with the geographic picture next to the queue: the operator's cities, the nearest live Drops, and the nearest pipeline leads — one tap goes to the city browser (UC-418) or the Nearby list (UC-422).
- **UC-425** Homepage city chips actually switch the homepage city (P0) — NEW in V2.7, reconciled from Studio UC-435. Tapping a city chip (Prosper, Frisco, Plano, McKinney, Dallas) re-renders the homepage (UC-370–UC-381) for that city — header, Live Now feed, Coming Soon, and restaurant list all change to that city. A chip that only changes the label and leaves the feed unchanged is a broken chip.
- **UC-426** The homepage shows the chosen city's Drops and restaurants, ordered by pickup window (P0) — NEW in V2.7, reconciled from Studio UC-436. After a city is picked, Live Now is ordered by pickup window for that city's Drops (UC-372), Coming Soon shows that city's upcoming Drops, and the restaurant list shows that city's restaurants — the homepage never mixes another city's live Drops into the chosen city's feed.
- **UC-427** A city with nothing live yet shows an honest launch-city state, not an empty page (P0) — NEW in V2.7, reconciled from Studio UC-437. If the chosen city has no live Drops, the homepage says so plainly ("Nothing live in [city] yet — next Drop coming soon") with the alert signup (UC-372/UC-236), instead of showing another city's Drops or a blank feed. The tiny URL for a launch city must always lead somewhere useful.
- **UC-428** Show a closest-first "Restaurants near you" strip on the homepage that crosses city lines (P1) — NEW in V2.7, reconciled from Studio UC-438. Below the city feed, the homepage shows the nearest restaurants by distance (UC-421/UC-422), regardless of which sub-city they sit in — a customer in Prosper sees the nearest Frisco restaurant before a farther Prosper one. Distances are shown on the strip.
- **UC-429** A restaurant in the "near you" strip opens its Smart Menu preview (P1) — NEW in V2.7, reconciled from Studio UC-439. Tapping a nearby restaurant opens its smart-menu page (UC-234) — the same shared preview as everywhere else, with the creator/channel tag surviving the navigation (UC-376) — so a nearby-restaurant tap is a selling tap, not a dead end.

## §42 Monitoring, alerting, audit & financial reconciliation — P0 (NEW section in V2.8)

**Status: NEW and PROPOSED as a section (open item 22).** Mahendra asked for "whatever you think we should have" for monitoring, "remember this about money so monitoring and auditing is important" (Nov 8, 2026). The mechanisms below are specified; the numbers inside them (SLOs, RTO/RPO, load-test target, retention period) are M2's proposals until Mahendra confirms them. This section adopts the readiness audit's monitoring items (R-01, R-10, R-11, R-16, R-17, R-18, R-21, R-22, R-36, R-37, R-42, R-47, R-48, R-49 — see open item 23) as cases. It refines, and does not replace: §13 financial operations (UC-125–UC-134), §15 alerts (UC-145–UC-154), §16 security/audit (UC-155–UC-164), §20 Money (UC-216–UC-218), §23 tech stack (UC-227–UC-233), §24/§27 payouts (UC-245/246/253, UC-283–UC-293), §32 messaging compliance (UC-326–UC-333), §37 identity/consent (UC-382–UC-393), and §40 operator alerts (UC-410–UC-417). Money cases are P0: a Drop that takes real money with no monitoring is not a launch, it is a liability.

### A. Uptime, health, SLOs, status & incident response

- **UC-435** Monitor the health of everything money touches, with a monitoring tool picked before Phase 0 — website, Studio, Supabase (database + auth + storage), Stripe (checkout + Connect + webhooks), Twilio (SMS/WhatsApp) (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. One monitoring tool (shortlist: Sentry for errors, Vercel Analytics/Logs + Supabase Logs for the platform — pick one as the single tool of record before Phase 0, open item 22e; the tool itself is TO DECIDE, not locked) watches uptime and latency for each dependency, with a health check per dependency and a dashboard the operator can read on their phone. PROPOSED SLOs (TO CONFIRM, open item 22a): **99.9% availability for checkout and pickup during pickup windows**, **99.5% for the Studio**. An SLO breach is an incident, not a statistic — it pages the operator (UC-436) and is recorded with its duration and cause. No monitoring tool, no Phase 1: Phase 0 may not be called done while checkout health is unwatched.
- **UC-436** A public status page, an incident runbook, and a paging order — who is paged, in what order, what the first 15 minutes look like (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. The status page (linked from the website footer and the Studio) shows, per dependency, up / degraded / down, with the current incident and its started-at time; it is updated by the operator, never auto-written by a vendor. The incident runbook names the paging order — operator first (SMS/WhatsApp, UC-410), backup after 15 minutes with no acknowledgement (not 2 hours: an outage is not a queue item, UC-220) — and the first 15 minutes: (1) confirm the outage from the monitoring dashboard, not from a customer message; (2) post the status page; (3) pause new claims for the affected Drop(s) if checkout or payment is down (UC-439); (4) message affected customers once, with what is happening and when they will hear next; (5) open a ticket with the provider. Every incident is closed with a written cause and a prevention action, audit-logged.
- **UC-437** Error tracking and structured logs for every money event — searchable, with no card numbers or secrets anywhere (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Every payment, refund, payout, QR redemption, inventory change, consent change, publish, and override is written as a structured log event with: actor, timestamp, order/Drop/restaurant IDs, and before/after values. Logs are searchable by phone (hashed lookup key, UC-382), order, Drop, restaurant, and payout reference. Card numbers, CVVs, bank details, tax documents, magic-link tokens, and API secrets NEVER appear in a log, an error report, or a Studio screen (UC-215/UC-293) — a log line containing one is a P0 defect, not a formatting issue. Error tracking groups repeated errors (same error, same Drop, same hour) into one incident, so one bad webhook does not page the operator fifty times.
- **UC-438** Webhook delivery is monitored, retried, and reconciled — a missed webhook is a recoverable order, never a lost one (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Stripe and Twilio webhook deliveries are monitored per endpoint: delivery rate, failures, and retries are on the monitoring dashboard (UC-435), and a webhook failure rate above zero during a pickup window is a P0 alert (UC-453). Failed webhooks retry with backoff (Stripe's own retry, then a manual re-delivery from the Studio). A nightly reconciliation job compares Stripe (payments, refunds, disputes) against Supabase (orders, payouts) and lists every mismatch as an owned task in Money (UC-442) — so a payment Stripe took but Supabase never saw is found by the system, not by a customer asking where their QR is. This case is the guard for UC-406's "QR is born only at the Stripe webhook": the QR is issued on the webhook, and UC-438 is what recovers it when the webhook is missed.
- **UC-439** Provider-outage plan — what happens when Stripe, Twilio, Supabase, or Vercel is down (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Per provider, stated in advance: Stripe down → checkout is PAUSED with a clear customer message ("Payments are down — your card has not been charged. Try again in a few minutes."), no payment is attempted twice, and held units (UC-405) are extended, not released, while the outage lasts. Twilio down → transactional texts queue and retry; the order page (UC-379) is the fallback source of truth and the customer is told to check it. Supabase down → no new claims, no redemptions except the manual ticket-code fallback (UC-268/UC-305) against a printed pickup list the restaurant already has; no redemption is recorded twice when the database returns. Vercel down → the status page (UC-436) is the fallback, hosted outside Vercel. In every outage: no double-charging, no silent failure, one customer message, and the Drop is re-opened (not silently extended) when the provider recovers, with the pickup window adjusted only by the operator, audit-logged.
- **UC-440** Deploy freeze, rollback, and prod-promotion checklist — no deploys during a live pickup window (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. No production deploy runs during a live pickup window, or in the 2 hours before one opens (the freeze); a deploy that would land in the freeze waits until the window closes. Every prod promotion follows a checklist: full test Drop passed in staging (UC-227), monitoring green (UC-435), rollback step written down before the deploy starts, and Mahendra's approval (UC-364). Rollback is a rehearsed action, not an improvisation: the previous version is restored in one action, and a rollback during a live window is announced to affected customers once (UC-439). Staging is seeded with synthetic data only — production customer data is never copied to staging (UC-227 rule, now a case).

### B. Financial monitoring & reconciliation — the money must always add up

- **UC-441** A real-time Money dashboard — gross, refunds, fees, commission, creator share, restaurant owed, net, right now (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Money (UC-216–UC-218) shows, live and per Drop / per restaurant / per city / per day: gross claimed, refunds issued, Stripe fees, DealsPro commission earned (redeemed orders only, UC-262), creator share accrued, restaurant owed, and net. Every figure is a live query against the order/payment records, never a cached total and never a hardcoded sample. A figure that disagrees with its underlying orders by even one cent is flagged on the dashboard the same day.
- **UC-442** Daily reconciliation — Stripe vs Supabase vs bank, with every mismatch named (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Every day, an automated reconciliation compares: Stripe balance transactions vs Supabase orders/payments/refunds/payouts vs the bank payout record. Every mismatch — a payout without orders, an order without a payout leg, a refund in Stripe but not in Supabase, a fee that does not match — becomes an owned task in Money with the exact orders in dispute (refines UC-130/UC-290), never a month-end surprise. Reconciliation is not "checked when someone remembers": it runs daily, it is logged, and an unreconciled day is visible on Today until it is cleared.
- **UC-443** Payout monitoring — scheduled vs paid vs failed, with retry rules (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Every scheduled payout (UC-245/UC-246) is tracked as scheduled → paid → failed, per payee and per period, with the payout reference, the orders it covers, and the per-order math. A failed payout retries automatically (next run, same reference, no duplicate payment), notifies the payee ("Your payout failed — check your bank details in Connect"), and alerts the operator (UC-453) if it fails twice. A payout that is retried without a new reference, or paid twice for the same orders, is a P0 defect.
- **UC-444** Refund-after-payout rule — a refund or chargeback that lands after the restaurant/creator was already paid (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED (TO CONFIRM, open item 22h). PROPOSED rule: the refund/chargeback amount (plus any Stripe fee) is DEDUCTED from that payee's next settlement/payout, and the deduction is shown as its own line on the settlement statement (UC-246) with the order it came from. If there is no next settlement, the amount is held from the payee's Connect balance or, if the balance cannot cover it, recorded as a DealsPro receivable with an owner and a date. The alternative — DealsPro absorbs it — is NOT adopted here; Mahendra must confirm deduction vs absorption before the first payout. Until he confirms, no refund-after-payout is built as either rule.
- **UC-445** Chargeback monitoring and evidence workflow — deadlines are automatic losses if missed (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Every Stripe dispute/chargeback is tracked from the moment Stripe reports it: amount, order, reason code, evidence deadline, and status (open / evidence submitted / won / lost / accepted). The evidence pack is auto-assembled from the order record — payment receipt, QR redemption time and method, pickup time, the messages sent to the customer (UC-267/UC-282), and the audit log (UC-449) — and the operator accepts or contests in one action. Deadline alerts fire at 7 days and at 1 day before the evidence deadline (P0 alerts, UC-453); a missed deadline is recorded as an automatic loss with its cause, so it is never discovered at month-end.
- **UC-446** Loss liability — who pays when a refund/dispute exceeds a connected account's balance (P0) — NEW in V2.8, TO DECIDE (open item 22g). This case asks the question and records that it is unanswered; it does NOT invent the answer. Before the first payout, Mahendra (with Stripe/preparer advice) must state: when a refund or chargeback exceeds what is held in a restaurant's or creator's Connect balance, who covers the shortfall — DealsPro, the payee (deducted from future payouts, UC-444), or a split — and what happens to the payout while the shortfall is open (held, partial, or paid with a receivable). Until the answer is recorded here, no payout is built as if the liability were settled, and the question is carried as a Phase 0/1 gate, not a detail.
- **UC-447** Fraud and abuse monitoring — bot/script buying, rate limits, velocity, self-purchase, cap evasion, on one dashboard (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. The fraud dashboard (operator only) shows, live: claims per phone/IP/device per hour, hold-to-payment conversion (a Drop "sold out" with zero pickups is a bot signal), self-purchase flags (UC-248/UC-314), cap-evasion flags (UC-433), and velocity alerts (many claims, one device, short window). Rate limits are enforced server-side on claims and checkouts per phone and per IP (limits TO DECIDE with the load-test target, UC-461 — record the numbers when set, do not hard-code a guess). A bot check on the claim page (challenge, not a CAPTCHA wall for every customer) is proposed for a Drop that sells out suspiciously fast. Every flag is evidence for operator review (UC-316), never an automatic cancel, except a confirmed bot burst, which pauses new claims for that Drop and alerts the operator.
- **UC-448** Settlement-dispute monitoring — a restaurant that disputes its settlement gets an evidence pack, an owner, and a resolution (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. A settlement dispute (UC-133/UC-246) is a ticket (UC-249) with the settlement statement, the orders behind every line, the Stripe payout reference, and the reconciliation record (UC-442) attached as its evidence pack. It has an owner (operator), a waiting clock (UC-221), and a resolution (upheld / corrected with a corrected statement / rejected with reasons), audit-logged (UC-449). A dispute with no evidence pack is not a dispute — it is a message — and is not counted as one.

### C. Audit trail — every money move is provable

- **UC-449** An immutable audit log for every money-moving action — who, when, why, from what to what (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED; **REVISED in V2.10 (cross-ref, restaurant-direct model).** Every publish, claim, payment, refund, QR redemption, payout, commission change, create-link revocation, override (including the UC-396 48-hour override), accept/decline, Ready, and support-decided refund is written to one append-only audit log with: actor (operator / backup / restaurant / creator / system), timestamp, order/Drop/restaurant/payout IDs, the reason, and the before and after values (e.g. inventory 15 → 14, status Claimed → Accepted, commission 12% → 15%). Restaurant-direct cancels are audit-logged the same way (UC-263/UC-480/UC-483): actor is the restaurant (pickup-link identity), with the tap time, the reason, and the refund — and a phone call with no tap has no audit entry, because no cancel happened (UC-482). The log is the record the Money dashboard (UC-441) and the reconciliation (UC-442) are checked against. An action with no audit entry is an action that did not happen, as far as DealsPro is concerned.
- **UC-450** The audit log is exportable, tamper-proof, and retained — it cannot be edited or deleted by any operator (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. The audit log is append-only in Supabase (no UPDATE/DELETE by any operator role, UC-223 — including Mahendra's own role; a correction is a new entry that supersedes, never an edit). It is exportable (CSV/JSON) per Drop, per restaurant, per payee, and per date range, for the preparer and for a dispute. Retention is the UC-458 schedule (TO DECIDE); audit entries are never deleted under the data-deletion flow (UC-458) — a customer deletion anonymises the customer fields in the log, it does not erase the money record.
- **UC-451** A customer-data access log — who viewed a customer's record, and why (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Every view of a customer record in the Studio (Demand, UC-212; order detail; support ticket) is logged with who viewed it, when, and which order/ticket it was for. The log is visible to the operator, and a view with no order or ticket behind it is flagged. Customer phone numbers are never exported in bulk, never shown beyond the UC-212 read view, and never appear in a log line outside the hashed lookup key (UC-382).
- **UC-452** Financial close — a daily and weekly money sign-off, not a glance (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Each day, the operator (or backup, UC-223) signs off the daily close in Money: reconciliation clear (UC-442) or mismatches listed with owners, payouts scheduled/paid/failed (UC-443), refunds issued, chargebacks open (UC-445), and the oldest unacted P0 alert (UC-416). The weekly close adds: settlements issued, creator payouts issued, holds released, and the week's P&L vs the prior week. A close is not signed off while a P0 money alert is unacted on — the sign-off is blocked, with the blocking item named, until it is acted on or explicitly overridden with a reason, audit-logged.

### D. Alerting for money — the right alert, to the right phone, once

- **UC-453** P0 money alerts — payment, payout, webhook, oversell, QR failure, late accept, unclaimed — page the operator immediately (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. The P0 money alert list is fixed and short: payment failure (repeated, UC-148), payout failure (UC-443), webhook failure during a pickup window (UC-438), oversell detected (UC-409), repeated QR redemption failures (UC-150), restaurant late to accept past its deadline (UC-279), unclaimed orders at window end (UC-308), chargeback evidence deadline at 7 days and 1 day (UC-445), reconciliation mismatch (UC-442), and a monitoring SLO breach (UC-435). Each is delivered by SMS/WhatsApp to the operator's phone (UC-410/UC-411) with what, which, how long, and the deep tiny link (UC-412). A P0 money alert that exists only as a Studio badge is an undelivered alert.
- **UC-454** Alert delivery receipts and failed-alert escalation — a failed alert is itself an alert (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Every money alert records its delivery receipt (UC-416); an alert with no receipt in 2 minutes retries once on the fallback channel, and if that also fails it is shown as a failed alert on Today (UC-219) and the backup is alerted (UC-415). The failed-alert record names the item, the channel, and the failure, so a missed pickup can be traced to a missed alert (UC-416's rule, now with the money-alert list from UC-453).
- **UC-455** Quiet hours — money alerts have none; non-urgent alerts are batched (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. The UC-414 quiet-hours proposal is stated as the money rule: alerts tied to a live pickup window, an unaccepted claim past its deadline, a payment/refund failure, or a payout failure are sent at any hour. Non-urgent alerts (end-of-day summary UC-153, throughput flags UC-244, P1 alerts UC-414) wait until 7:00 AM local and are batched into the morning summary; P1 alerts are batched after 21:00 (UC-414 expansion, V2.8). Marketing alerts to customers are never sent 9:00 PM–9:00 AM local. Quiet hours are TO CONFIRM (open item 21) — until Mahendra sets them, this case is a proposal, not a schedule.
- **UC-456** Alert deduplication and fatigue — one incident, one alert, with updates (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. The same incident (same Drop, same failure, same hour) sends ONE alert; further occurrences update the alert ("3 more orders affected") instead of sending new ones. A re-alert fires only when the item is still unacted on after the UC-413 proposed 30 minutes (TO CONFIRM — and recorded here as in conflict with the Studio's "ACK stops re-sends" rule, UC-427 in the Studio; the file rule governs until Mahendra picks one side, open item 14/21). An operator who is paged five times for one outage will stop reading alerts; this case exists to prevent that.

### E. Backup, disaster recovery, privacy & retention — the money record must survive

- **UC-457** Backups and disaster recovery — daily backups, a restore test, proposed RTO 4h / RPO 1h (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Supabase automated backups run daily, with point-in-time recovery where the plan supports it; the backup covers the database (orders, payments, payouts, consent, audit log) and the video/object storage references. PROPOSED (TO CONFIRM, open item 22b): **RTO 4 hours** (back to serving claims/pickups within 4 hours of a declared disaster) and **RPO 1 hour** (at most 1 hour of order history lost). A restore test runs quarterly in staging — a real restore from a real backup, not a backup-exists check — and a failed restore test is a P0 incident (UC-436). The restore test result, with its duration, is recorded; a quarter with no restore test is a quarter with no disaster recovery.
- **UC-458** Data retention and deletion — how long each record lives, and how a customer is deleted (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. A retention schedule states, per record type, how long it is kept: orders and payments (settlement + tax + dispute window — period TO DECIDE, open item 22d, with the preparer), consent records (kept for audit, UC-388/UC-459), audit log (never deleted, UC-450), marketing message logs (per the UC-250 cap window plus audit). A customer who asks to be deleted gets a deletion flow: their customer record is anonymised (name/phone replaced by a one-way hash, order history kept as numbers only), their consent record is marked deleted, and marketing stops immediately; money records (orders, payments, payouts) are kept as anonymised numbers because settlement and tax require them. The deletion, its scope, and its date are audit-logged, and the customer is told what was deleted and what was kept as numbers and why.
- **UC-459** Consent-list audit and export — any send can answer why it was allowed (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. The consent records (UC-388) and the send log (UC-250/UC-331) together export as one evidence pack per phone + channel: consent status, source, timestamp, disclosure version, every send to that phone + channel with its consent basis, and every STOP/START with its timestamp. The export exists so a TCPA dispute is answered from records, not from memory (readiness R-42). The export is operator-only, audit-logged when run (UC-451), and never includes other customers' records.
- **UC-460** PCI scope — no card data ever enters DealsPro (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Card numbers, expiry, and CVV are never collected, stored, logged, or displayed by DealsPro, the Studio, or the website — payment is by Stripe (checkout + Connect) only, and DealsPro stores only Stripe references (payment intent ID, charge ID, payout ID). This scope statement is recorded here so the build is judged against it: any code path that touches a raw card number is a P0 defect. The exact PCI SAQ / attestation wording is TO CONFIRM with the preparer (open item 22, with UC-446) — this case states the technical scope, not the legal attestation.
- **UC-461** Performance and load monitoring — checkout latency, QR scan latency, and a load-test target (P0) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Checkout latency (claim sheet → payment confirmed), QR scan latency (scan → redeemed), and homepage/smart-menu load time are monitored continuously (UC-435) with alerts when they exceed their thresholds during a pickup window. PROPOSED load-test target (TO CONFIRM, open item 22c): **50 concurrent claims and a 15-order simultaneous purchase + simultaneous-scan burst** in staging, as the Phase 0 exit criterion (UC-232) — the target is the number the test must pass, not a hope. A Drop that cannot survive its own sellout burst is not launched.
- **UC-462** Video-processing monitoring — transcode queue, failure alert, storage and cost (P1) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Video processing (UC-228) is monitored as a queue: videos queued, processing, failed, and the Drop each failed video blocks. A failed transcode alerts the operator (UC-453, P1 for video) with the Drop it blocks, retries once, and the Drop stays unpublished until its video is ready (video is a publish blocker, §3 lock). Storage used and processing cost are visible in Money, with an alert when they cross a stated threshold (threshold TO DECIDE with the video-provider pick, open item 22e).
- **UC-463** Restaurant reliability monitoring — a score, a threshold, and a way back (P1) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. The reliability record (UC-154/UC-259 strikes, UC-273 declines, UC-266 never-Ready flags, UC-269 late/wrong-item flags) is computed as a per-restaurant score, shown to the operator and to the restaurant, with a stated probation threshold (threshold TO DECIDE — do not invent a number) and a reinstatement path (a run of clean pickups clears probation, audit-logged). A score with no threshold is a feeling; a threshold with no way back is a ban. Both are required by this case, and the threshold is Mahendra's decision.
- **UC-464** Messaging-delivery monitoring — sender status, delivery rates, blocked senders (P1) — NEW in V2.8, PROPOSED. Delivery rates per channel (SMS, WhatsApp, RCS) and per sender are monitored (UC-435): delivery rate, failure rate, and carrier filtering are on the Money/Demand dashboard, and a blocked sender (UC-326/UC-327) halts marketing sends automatically (UC-250) and alerts the operator (UC-453, P1 for messaging) — a blocked sender discovered by a customer asking "where is my QR?" is a failure of this case. Transactional delivery (QR, Ready, pickup) is monitored separately from marketing delivery, with a lower failure tolerance, because a missed QR is a missed pickup.
---

## §43 Cuisine filter & hero-dish discovery — P0 for the visibility rule, P1 otherwise (NEW section in V2.9)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided (open item 24).** Mahendra's rule (Nov 8, 2026): "add that to the spec by cuisine and make it dynamic if available show otherwise no — I mean if live drop show otherwise no." Strategy behind it, recorded in MEMORY.md (Nov 8, 2026): Year 1 is one restaurant, one famous dish, marketed across Dallas; later 1–3 restaurants per city (Dallas, Austin, Houston first), then 15+ cities; the hero-dish model is cuisine-agnostic — pizza, Thai, Mexican, Chinese all fit it, because the unit is the famous dish, not the cuisine. This section makes cuisine a filter on the existing website surfaces (§36, UC-370–UC-381) — homepage, smart menu / restaurant list, and search/browse — and adds the hero-dish tag. It does NOT create a new page type, a new Drop type, or a redesign: the Drop page, smart menu, homepage, claim flow, QR, and order timeline are unchanged. It refines, and does not replace: §36 (UC-371 homepage layout, UC-372 live feed, UC-374 0-live empty state, UC-377 search/browse), §41 cities (UC-425–UC-427 city chips and launch-city state), §1 restaurant onboarding (UC-003/UC-009), §2 Drop creation (UC-012 dish name, UC-013 portion), §17 smart menu (UC-174/UC-234–UC-242), §11 Drop lifecycle (UC-105 inventory, UC-176 sold-out display), and §7 confirmation (UC-208 auto-void on material change).
