- **UC-465** A cuisine chip is shown ONLY when at least one LIVE Drop in that cuisine exists in the selected city right now — otherwise the chip is hidden entirely (P0) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. This is the dynamic visibility rule, and it is the whole point of the section. A cuisine appears as a filter chip on the homepage (UC-467) only if, in the city currently selected (UC-371 city picker, UC-425 city chips), there is at least one Drop in that cuisine that is live by the UC-476 definition. If there is no live Drop in that cuisine, the chip is NOT shown — not greyed out, not disabled, not shown with a "0" count, not shown as "coming soon". A customer must never see a cuisine they cannot buy from right now. The rule is computed server-side from live Drop state, never from a cached list of cuisines and never from the restaurant list alone (a city can have three Mexican restaurants and still show no Mexican chip if none of them has a live Drop). When the last live Drop in a cuisine sells out or its window ends, the chip disappears; when a new Drop in that cuisine goes live, the chip appears — with no operator action and no deploy in between.
- **UC-466** Cuisine is a controlled property of a restaurant and of a dish — a fixed list, never free text (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. The cuisine list is: Indian, Pizza, Thai, Mexican, Chinese, Other. Cuisine is set at restaurant onboarding (UC-003, alongside city and contact details) as the restaurant's primary cuisine, and per dish/video (UC-012/UC-014, alongside dish name and portion) as the dish's cuisine. The list is controlled: a restaurant cannot type a new cuisine in its create form (UC-203) or in onboarding — a new cuisine is added only by the operator in the Studio, audit-logged, and the new value appears as a filter chip only under the UC-465 rule. "Other" is a real cuisine value for a dish that fits none of the five named cuisines, not a bucket for uncategorised dishes: a dish filed as Other with no cuisine note is flagged in review (UC-062) for the operator to set correctly. Cuisine values are stored as codes (e.g. `indian`, `pizza`), never as display strings, so a display-name change never breaks a filter.
- **UC-467** Homepage cuisine filter chips — order, toggle, clear, and what they filter (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. The chips sit above the Live Now feed (UC-371/UC-372), in a single horizontal scroll row on mobile. Chips are ordered by live Drop count in the selected city (most live Drops first), then alphabetically for ties. Tapping a chip filters BOTH Live Now and Coming Soon (UC-371) to that cuisine; tapping the active chip again clears the filter and restores the full feed. Only one cuisine may be active at a time — there is no multi-select. The active chip is visibly distinct (filled red, per the red-accent design lock) and shows its live count ("Mexican · 2"). Clearing is always one tap away: the active chip itself, or a "Clear" control next to the row. The filter never hides a live Drop in the chosen cuisine, and it never shows a live Drop outside it while active.
- **UC-468** Changing the city re-computes which cuisines have live Drops — the chip row is rebuilt, not carried over (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. When the visitor changes city (UC-371 city picker, UC-425 city chips), the cuisine chip row is re-computed from that city's live Drops (UC-465) before the feed renders: a cuisine live in Frisco and not live in Plano appears in Frisco and disappears in Plano. If the visitor had a cuisine filter active and that cuisine has no live Drop in the new city, the filter is cleared automatically and the feed shows the new city's full Live Now feed with a plain note ("No live Mexican Drops in Plano yet") — the filter is never left active on a city where it matches nothing. If the cuisine IS live in the new city, the filter stays active and the feed re-renders for the new city. City and cuisine combine as AND, never as OR: the feed is always (selected city) AND (selected cuisine, if any).
- **UC-469** Hero dish — one famous dish per restaurant, operator-set, shown as a small tag on a normal Drop (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. **REVISED in V2.12 (placement, Mahendra Nov 10, 2026):** the tag is an overlay on top of the hero picture/video, immediately next to the red DROP badge — see the placement rule in this case. *Superseded placement (V2.9): "shown as a small \"Hero Dish\" tag on that dish's Drop page and on its smart-menu card (UC-234), next to the Bundle/Serves pill" — the tag is no longer next to the Bundle/Serves pill; that pill stays below the picture.* Each restaurant has at most ONE hero dish — its famous dish (the Year-1 strategy: one restaurant, one famous dish marketed across Dallas). The hero dish is set by the operator in the restaurant workspace (UC-009), changeable at any time, and shown as a small "Hero Dish" tag (wording PROPOSED — "Hero Dish", TO CONFIRM whether "★ Hero Dish"; open item 24e) as an OVERLAY ON TOP OF THE HERO PICTURE/VIDEO, immediately NEXT TO the red DROP badge, in the same overlay row in the same top corner — never as a large banner and never as a separate page section. The tag appears in that overlay position on EVERY surface where a hero-dish Drop appears: its Drop page, its smart-menu card (UC-234), the homepage Live Now card (UC-371), the Top 5 card (UC-519), the "Because you picked X" card (UC-526), and the matcher answer card (UC-523). The tag never covers the red DROP badge, never replaces it, and never covers the dish itself beyond the overlay row; it stays legible over video (contrast/scrim as needed). The Bundle/Serves pill stays below the picture, as before. A hero dish is NOT a separate Drop type: it is a label on a normal Drop, so it follows every existing Drop rule — 48-hour lead (UC-395, LOCKED), video required (UC-021), claim/QR/pickup flow (§26), cap and hold (§39), and commission (UC-207). A restaurant may not set its own hero dish through its private create link (UC-203); it may suggest one in the submission, and the operator sets it in review. Changing a restaurant's hero dish does not change any live Drop's price, quantity, or window, and is audit-logged.
- **UC-470** Restaurant list / smart menu — cuisine shown as a small label, and filtering by cuisine hides restaurants with no live Drop in it (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. Every restaurant card (homepage restaurant list, UC-371; city browser, UC-418; Nearby list, UC-422) shows the restaurant's primary cuisine as a small label (e.g. "Mexican · Frisco"), and a smart-menu page (UC-234) shows the cuisine of each Drop on its card. When a cuisine filter is active on a restaurant list, the list shows ONLY restaurants with a live Drop in that cuisine in the selected city — a restaurant with no live Drop in that cuisine is hidden from the filtered view, even if it has a Coming Soon Drop in that cuisine (Coming Soon does not count, UC-473) and even if it is the visitor's nearest restaurant (UC-428). Clearing the filter restores the full list. The unfiltered list is never filtered by cuisine by default.
- **UC-471** Search/browse — cuisine as a facet alongside price, Serves, and pickup day; no live results shows the 0-live empty state, never an empty list (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. Cuisine joins the existing §36 search facets (UC-377: price band, Serves 1/2/3/4/5/6+, pickup day) as a facet on the homepage search/browse and on the smart-menu list: it combines with the other facets as AND (e.g. Mexican + Serves 3 + this week). A search or facet combination with no matching LIVE Drop shows the UC-374 empty state for that filter — "No live [cuisine] Drops [in city / on that day] yet — next Drop coming soon" with the next scheduled Drop in that cuisine (if any) and the alert signup (SMS/WhatsApp, unchecked by default, per-channel, UC-214) scoped to that cuisine in that city — never a blank results page and never a "0 results" list. The alert signup records the cuisine + city so the customer is alerted only when a Drop in that cuisine goes live in that city (UC-387 scope rule, extended to cuisine).
- **UC-472** A Drop whose cuisine is changed after publish requires re-confirmation, like a price change (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. Cuisine is part of the offer the restaurant confirmed (UC-067/UC-399/UC-400 offer fingerprint: price, quantity, pickup window, video, dish/portion — cuisine is added to the fingerprint). Changing a live or confirmed Drop's cuisine is a material change: it auto-voids the prior restaurant confirmation under the UC-208 auto-void rule, returns the Drop to awaiting confirmation (UC-434 state machine), and the Drop does not publish — or, if already live, is paused from new claims — until the restaurant confirms the new cuisine. The cuisine change, the void, and the re-confirmation are audit-logged (UC-449). A cuisine change made in draft, before confirmation, needs no re-confirmation.
- **UC-473** Coming Soon Drops do not count as live for the cuisine filter — a cuisine with only Coming Soon Drops has no chip (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. A cuisine whose Drops in the selected city are all Coming Soon (published but not yet claimable, UC-371) or scheduled (not yet published) shows NO chip under UC-465 — the chip appears only when a Drop in that cuisine becomes claimable. The Coming Soon section itself (UC-371) still shows those Drops, with their cuisine label on each card (UC-470), so a visitor browsing Coming Soon can see a Mexican Drop is coming without a Mexican chip promising it is buyable now. The alert signup on a Coming Soon Drop (UC-471) is the way a visitor asks to be told when that cuisine goes live.
- **UC-474** A city with no live Drops at all shows no cuisine chips — the homepage 0-live empty state governs (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. If the selected city has no live Drops in any cuisine, the chip row is empty — no chips are shown, not even the cuisines of that city's restaurants or its Coming Soon Drops — and the homepage shows the UC-374 / UC-427 0-live empty state ("Next Drop coming soon in [City]" with the next scheduled Drop, the restaurants in the city, and the alert signup). The launch-city state (UC-427) and the cuisine rule agree: a city with nothing to buy shows no filter promising something to buy. The chip row reappears, with the correct chips, the moment the city's first Drop goes live.
- **UC-475** A restaurant with two cuisines — dish-level cuisine wins over restaurant-level cuisine (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. A restaurant whose menu spans two cuisines (e.g. an Indian restaurant whose famous dish is a pizza, or a restaurant with both Thai and Chinese dishes) is filtered by its DISHES, not by its primary cuisine: each Drop carries its dish's cuisine (UC-466), the cuisine chip counts that Drop under its dish's cuisine, and the restaurant appears in a cuisine-filtered list if ANY of its live Drops is in that cuisine. The restaurant card's small label (UC-470) shows its primary cuisine, and a restaurant with live Drops in two cuisines shows both labels ("Thai · Chinese"). No restaurant is forced into one cuisine, and no dish is misfiled under the restaurant's primary cuisine to make the filter work.
- **UC-476** "Live" for the cuisine filter — published, claimable, units remaining, window not ended — and the chip disappears the moment the last one goes (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. A Drop counts toward its cuisine's chip only while it is live by this definition: published (UC-395 48-hour lead met, operator-approved, UC-062), claimable (claim window open, UC-431 — claimable from go-live until the claim window closes), with at least one unit remaining (not sold out, UC-372/UC-405 — a sold-out Drop stops counting the moment the last unit is claimed or held, not when its window ends), and its pickup window not yet ended (UC-375). A paused Drop (operator-paused, UC-059/UC-186), a cancelled Drop (UC-254), or a Drop whose offer confirmation was voided (UC-472/UC-208) counts as not live and stops counting immediately. The chip row is re-computed on every feed load and on every inventory change (claim, hold expiry, sellout, cancel) — a chip that lingers after its last Drop sold out is a bug, and a chip that appears before its Drop is claimable is a bug.
- **UC-477** The cuisine filter survives navigation to a Drop page and back, and combines with the other filters (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. Tapping a Drop card while a cuisine filter is active opens the Drop page (UC-376); going back returns to the filtered feed with the same cuisine still active and the same scroll position — the filter is not lost on navigation. The cuisine filter combines with the other §36 facets (UC-377: price band, Serves, pickup day) as AND, and with the city (UC-468) as AND; clearing any one facet leaves the others active. The active cuisine is part of the page state (not a URL parameter that breaks the tiny-URL rule, UC-233 — the filter is session/device state, and a shared link to a Drop page never carries a filter). The creator/channel tag survives cuisine filtering exactly as it survives homepage navigation (UC-376, last-tagged-link-wins, LOCKED, UC-248): filtering never strips a tag, and buying after filtering is still the tagged creator's sale.
- **UC-478** The operator sees, per city, which cuisines are live, which are Coming Soon only, and which are absent — in the Studio (P1) — NEW in V2.9, PROPOSED. The Studio (Restaurants · By City, UC-418, and Today, UC-424) shows, per city, a cuisine row: cuisines with live Drops (with their live Drop count), cuisines with Coming Soon Drops only (labelled "coming soon — no chip on the website", UC-473), and cuisines with no Drops at all in that city. The operator can preview the website's chip row for a city from the Studio — the preview shows exactly the chips a customer in that city sees (UC-465), so a missing chip is diagnosable ("no live Mexican Drop in Plano" is a supply fact, not a website bug). The cuisine row is read-only in this case: changing a Drop's cuisine is UC-472, adding a cuisine is UC-466, and neither happens from the preview.


## §44 Pickup verification & reminders — P0 for pickup state and unclaimed, P1 for reminder timing (NEW section in V2.10)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided (open item 26).** Mahendra's instruction (Nov 8, 2026): "we need to check if the customer picked up or not and send text messages reminders before the window and within the window add these use cases as well." This section makes pickup a provable fact, not an assumption: every order has a pickup state that is recorded at redemption, the customer is reminded before and during the window, and an order that is not picked up by window end is detected and closed by the system, not discovered later. It refines, and does not replace: §25 (UC-254–UC-269 cancel/refund, UC-261 no-show, UC-267 transactional messages, UC-268 QR fallback; plus the V2.10 restaurant-direct extension UC-479–UC-488 — the goodwill refund in UC-497 uses UC-483, the restaurant number in UC-492–UC-496 is UC-479/UC-485, the tap-is-the-record rule is UC-482), §26 (UC-270–UC-282 claim → confirm → Ready, UC-277 timeline, UC-278 countdown, UC-282 status-change messages), §29 (UC-302–UC-309 pickup board, QR fallback, unclaimed), §32 (UC-326–UC-333 messaging compliance, transactional vs marketing), §37 (UC-382–UC-393 identity/consent), §40 (UC-410–UC-417 operator alerts), and §42 (UC-437 logs, UC-449 audit log, UC-453 P0 money alerts, UC-454 failed-alert escalation). **CONFLICT, recorded not resolved:** UC-497's window-end unclaimed marking conflicts with the 30-minute post-window grace + operator-decision rule (wording retained in open items 26 and 29d; formerly UC-307/UC-308 — UC-307 was DELETED in V2.17, delete pass, Nov 22, 2026), and its restaurant goodwill-refund step conflicts with UC-261's operator goodwill override (now moved to the restaurant by UC-483 in the V2.10 restaurant-direct model). Mahendra's Nov 8 rule is recorded here as the V2.10 proposal; UC-308/UC-261 are NOT silently rewritten. Open item 26 asks him to pick: adopt the V2.10 window-end rule (and revise UC-308/UC-261 accordingly) or keep the grace + operator-decision rule. Until he picks, do not build both. **Numbering note:** §44 was written in parallel with the restaurant-direct cancel work, which took UC-479–UC-488 first; §44 therefore starts at UC-489, not UC-479 — the two V2.10 parts share one version, with no ID reused. A third parallel write also framed a §45 "Restaurant order management" on the same UC-489–UC-502; that framing is recorded as a conflict in "Biggest changes in V2.10" and corrected in the header/summary/audit table — the §44 cases are the content at those IDs.

- **UC-489** Every order has one pickup state, and the state is the truth about pickup — Claimed → Accepted → Ready → Picked up / Unclaimed (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. The pickup state is a single server-side field on the order, shown identically in the Studio, on the restaurant pickup board (UC-302), and on the customer's order page (UC-277 timeline): Claimed (paid, awaiting restaurant confirmation, UC-271) → Accepted (restaurant accepted, UC-272) → Ready (restaurant marked Ready, UC-264) → Picked up (redeemed, UC-490/UC-491) — or Unclaimed (window ended with no redemption, UC-497). Cancelled/Refunded and Declined (UC-273) are separate terminal states, not pickup states. The state never jumps a step (UC-277/UC-282 rule, restated here for pickup): an order cannot show Picked up before Ready, and cannot show Ready before Accepted. The state, its timestamp, and the actor are written to the audit log (UC-449) on every transition.
- **UC-490** Pickup is recorded at the QR scan — timestamp, order, who redeemed it (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. When the restaurant scans the customer's QR at pickup, the redemption is atomic (UC-038/UC-105: scan once, mark redeemed, cannot be reused) and the record states: order ID, ticket code, redemption timestamp (restaurant timezone, UC-276), and who redeemed it — the restaurant staff member / counter device on the pickup board (UC-302/UC-303) or the pickup link (UC-264). The customer immediately gets the "picked up, thank you" message (UC-267) and the order-page timeline (UC-277) shows Picked up with its timestamp. A scan of an already-redeemed, cancelled, or refunded QR is rejected with its original redemption/cancel time shown to the restaurant (same rule as UC-305), never silently accepted and never re-recorded as a second pickup.
- **UC-491** Pickup by manual ticket-code fallback is recorded exactly like a QR scan (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. When the QR will not scan, the restaurant enters the ticket short code or order number (UC-268/UC-305) and the redemption runs the identical one-time atomic rule and writes the identical record as UC-490 — order, code, timestamp, redeemer — with a flag that the redemption was by manual code, not by scan. The fallback is not a second, looser path: it rejects an already-redeemed, cancelled, or refunded code (UC-305/UC-306), and a manual-code redemption of an order whose QR was already scanned is rejected as a duplicate, not recorded as a second pickup.
- **UC-492** Reminder 24 hours before the pickup window — transactional, on the channel the customer ordered from (P1) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. 24 hours before the window opens, the customer gets one reminder text on the same channel they ordered from (SMS/WhatsApp, UC-267 — never email, UC-332): restaurant name, dish, quantity, pickup window (date + start/end time, restaurant timezone, big type per UC-276), pickup address, order code, and the restaurant's published/order phone number for changes (UC-479/UC-485 — the number is shown so the customer can call the restaurant, per Mahendra Nov 8, 2026). The reminder is skipped if the order was already cancelled/refunded or declined (UC-498). It is a transactional order text, not marketing (UC-214): it is never capped, never needs a marketing opt-in, and never carries a marketing message.
- **UC-493** Reminder 2 hours before the pickup window (P1) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. Same channel, same contents as UC-492 — restaurant name, dish, quantity, window (date + start/end, restaurant timezone), address, order code, restaurant phone number for changes — sent 2 hours before the window opens. If the order was cancelled/refunded/declined or already picked up (not possible before the window, but the check is the same), it is skipped (UC-498). If the 24-hour reminder (UC-492) failed to deliver (UC-499), the 2-hour reminder is still sent — one failed reminder never cancels the next one.
- **UC-494** Reminder at window start — "Your pickup window is open now" (P1) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. At the moment the pickup window opens, the customer gets one reminder on their channel: the window is open now, it ends at [end time], the restaurant name and address, the order code / what to show (QR or ticket code, UC-274), and the restaurant phone number. This is the reminder that turns a paid claim into a pickup: a customer who forgot the day is told at the exact moment they can go. Skipped if cancelled/refunded/declined or already picked up (UC-498).
- **UC-495** Midpoint reminder, only if not yet picked up (P1) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. At the midpoint of the pickup window (halfway between window start and window end, computed from the window, not from a fixed clock time), the customer gets one reminder — but ONLY if the order's pickup state is not Picked up (UC-489): "Your order is waiting — pickup window ends at [end time], [time remaining] left." It states the time remaining (UC-278 countdown), the restaurant name and address, the order code, and the restaurant phone number. A customer who has already picked up never gets this reminder (UC-498) — a "come and pick up" text after pickup is a defect, not a reminder.
- **UC-496** Final reminder 30 minutes before the window ends, only if not yet picked up (P1) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. 30 minutes before the window ends, the customer gets one final reminder on their channel — but ONLY if the order is not yet Picked up: "Last 30 minutes — your order is waiting at [restaurant], window ends [end time]." It states the time remaining, the restaurant phone number, and that after the window ends the order is marked unclaimed with no refund by default (UC-497/UC-261) — the consequence is stated plainly in the reminder itself, so the missed-pickup text (UC-497) is never the first time the customer hears it. No reminder is sent after this one inside the window.
- **UC-497** At window end, an order not picked up is automatically marked Unclaimed — one missed-pickup text, restaurant summary, operator P0 alert (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. **CONFLICT with the 30-minute grace + operator-decision rule — see the §44 status note and open item 26 (grace wording retained there; UC-307 deleted in V2.17).** Under this V2.10 rule: the moment the pickup window ends, every order whose pickup state is not Picked up is automatically marked Unclaimed (UC-489) — no grace period, no waiting for the operator. Then, automatically and at once: (a) the customer gets ONE "You missed your pickup window" text on their channel, stating no refund by default and the restaurant phone number if they want to ask about it; (b) the restaurant gets a summary of its unclaimed orders for that Drop (order codes, customer first names, quantities); (c) the operator gets a P0 alert if any unclaimed orders exist at window end (UC-453 list, UC-308's alert — the alert fires at window end, not after a grace). The restaurant may then do a one-time goodwill refund via the restaurant-direct flow (UC-483 — restaurant taps "Refund as goodwill" with a reason, audit-logged, UC-449); otherwise the order closes as unclaimed and the food is the restaurant's to dispose of. If Mahendra instead keeps the 30-minute grace + operator-decision rule (wording in open item 29d), this case is revised to that rule before build — do not build both.
- **UC-498** Reminder suppression — no reminder after pickup, after cancel/refund, or after the window ends, except the single missed-pickup text (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. Before every reminder (UC-492–UC-496) the system checks the order's pickup state (UC-489) and its cancel/refund state: a reminder is sent ONLY while the order is live (Claimed, Accepted, or Ready) and inside its reminder schedule. No reminder is sent after Picked up, after Cancelled/Refunded, after Declined, or after the window ends — the only post-window customer text is the single missed-pickup text (UC-497). A customer who picked up at 5:10 PM never gets the 6:30 PM midpoint or final reminder for a 5:00–7:00 PM window. Reminders respect STOP / opt-out for MARKETING (UC-330/UC-385) but transactional pickup reminders are always sent: they are order texts, not marketing, per the consent separation (UC-214) — a customer who opted out of Drop alerts still gets their pickup reminders, because without them they cannot collect what they paid for.
- **UC-499** Every reminder's delivery is logged, and a failed reminder escalates — never silently dropped (P1) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. Each reminder (UC-492–UC-496, UC-497 missed-pickup text) is written to the message log (UC-250/UC-331/UC-437) with: order, reminder type, scheduled time, sent time, channel, and delivery status (delivered / failed / undelivered, per the Twilio delivery receipt, UC-454). A failed reminder retries once on the fallback channel (UC-411 SMS fallback) and, if that also fails, is shown as a failed reminder on the operator alert feed (UC-454 rule, extended to customer reminders) with the order named — so a missed pickup can be traced to a missed reminder. A reminder with no log entry is a reminder that was never sent, as far as DealsPro is concerned.
- **UC-500** Pickup identity check — a forwarded QR screenshot can still collect the order; no solution is specified here (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED as an open gap, not a design. This case records the gap, it does not invent the fix. The readiness audit (R-05, open item 23) named it and it is still missing as a case anywhere in the catalog: nothing in §§25/26/29/44 checks that the person presenting a QR is the person who paid for the order — a forwarded screenshot, a shared order link, or a read-out ticket code collects the food. Until Mahendra picks a check (e.g. name match at the counter, last-4-digits of the phone, OTP at pickup — each with its own friction and failure mode, none chosen here), pickup verification (UC-489–UC-491) records WHAT was redeemed and WHEN, not WHO collected it. Do not present UC-490/UC-491 as an identity check: they are a redemption record. The fix, when chosen, is a new case here, not a silent edit to UC-490.
- **UC-501** Operator view — Orders & Pickup board shows pickup state, reminder history, and time remaining, with a "not yet picked up" filter (P1) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. The Orders & Pickup board (UC-247/UC-302) shows, per order: pickup state (UC-489) with its timestamp, reminder history (which of UC-492–UC-497 were sent, when, and delivered/failed per UC-499), and time remaining in the window (UC-278 countdown, live). The board has a filter for "not yet picked up" — orders in Claimed/Accepted/Ready whose window is open or about to open, oldest first — so the operator can see, at a glance during a window, exactly which customers still need to come. The filter is the operator's view of UC-495/UC-496: the orders that will get the midpoint and final reminders are the orders on this filter.
- **UC-502** A picked-up order is terminal — it can never be re-redeemed, cancelled, or refunded as unclaimed (P0) — NEW in V2.10, PROPOSED. Once an order's pickup state is Picked up (UC-490/UC-491), it is closed as a pickup: it cannot be re-redeemed (UC-490 duplicate rejection), it cannot be cancelled (UC-257/UC-258 — after pickup there is no cancel, only a support case for a real issue: wrong item, bad food, missing item), and it cannot be refunded as unclaimed (UC-497 does not apply to it, and the missed-pickup text is never sent for it). Commission, creator share, and restaurant payout count only on picked-up orders (UC-262/UC-286/UC-441) — a picked-up order is the only order that earns anyone money, which is why its state must be provable (UC-489) and its record must be complete (UC-490/UC-491).


## §46 Top 5 in your area this week + interactive matcher (NEW, PROPOSED)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided.** This is the customer-facing homepage shelf and its matcher. It refines, and does not replace: §36 (UC-370–UC-381 website & homepage, UC-371 homepage layout, UC-372 Live Now feed, UC-374 0-live empty state, UC-377 search/browse), §43 (UC-465–UC-478 dynamic cuisine filter & hero-dish discovery, UC-476 "live" definition), §41 (UC-418–UC-429 cities, UC-425 city chips), §37 (UC-382–UC-393 identity & opt-in recognition, phone is identity), §17 smart menu (UC-174/UC-234–UC-242), §11 Drop lifecycle (UC-105 inventory, UC-176 sold-out display, UC-372/UC-405), §26 (UC-277 timeline, UC-278 countdown), §39 (UC-404–UC-409 cap & hold), §42 (UC-449 audit log, UC-437 logs), and §25/§44 (UC-489–UC-502 pickup state). The Top 5 shelf shows live Drops only; it never creates a Drop, never changes a price, quantity, or window, and never bypasses the two human gates (restaurant confirms the exact offer, Mahendra authorizes every publish).

- **UC-516** Top 5 shelf — "Top 5 in [city] this week", max 5 live Drops, city-scoped, 3 visible on mobile first (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The customer homepage (§36, UC-371) opens with one shelf titled "Top 5 in [city] this week" (city = the selected city, UC-371/UC-425), showing at most 5 live Drops in that city, ordered by the UC-517 ranking. On mobile, the first 3 cards are visible without scrolling; cards 4–5 are one short scroll away — never a long list, never an endless feed. On desktop the same 5 cards show in one row/grid. The shelf is the first thing on the homepage, above the full Live Now feed (UC-372) and above the cuisine chips (UC-467): a customer who wants a Drop picks from the Top 5; a customer who wants to browse can scroll past it. The shelf title always names the city ("Top 5 in Frisco this week", "Top 5 in Prosper this week") — never a generic "Top Drops". Changing the city re-computes the shelf for the new city (UC-468 rule, applied to the shelf): a Drop live in Frisco and not live in Plano appears in Frisco's Top 5 and not in Plano's.
- **UC-517** Ranking signals — earned signals only, NO paid ranking, NO bidding (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The Top 5 order is computed server-side from real order/pickup data only, from these four signals — **PROPOSED weights, TO CONFIRM (open item 27a)**: (a) sellout velocity — how fast units are claimed relative to quantity and time live (PROPOSED weight 40%); (b) pickup reliability — on-time pickup rate for that Drop's orders so far, from the UC-489 pickup state (PROPOSED weight 30%); (c) repeat-buyer rate — share of that Drop's buyers who have bought a previous Drop (PROPOSED weight 20%); (d) recency — a new hero dish this week gets a small boost so a new Drop can enter the shelf (PROPOSED weight 10%). No signal is paid placement: a restaurant cannot pay, bid, or buy a higher rank; there is no sponsored slot in the Top 5, no "boost" purchase, and no operator manual override of the order except pausing/cancelling a Drop under the existing rules (UC-059/UC-186), which removes it from the shelf by UC-520. The ranking formula, its weights, and its inputs are visible to the operator in the Studio (UC-527) and are never shown as a score to the customer — the customer sees the order and, where a badge applies, the real fact behind it (UC-528).
- **UC-518** Real-time recompute — the Top 5 reorders the moment the underlying signals change (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The ranking is re-computed on every event that changes a signal, with no operator action and no deploy in between: a claim or hold (UC-405/UC-406) changes sellout velocity; a pickup (UC-490/UC-491) or a missed pickup (UC-497) changes pickup reliability; a repeat purchase changes repeat-buyer rate; a new Drop going live, a Drop being paused/cancelled, or a pickup window ending changes the eligible set. The recompute is server-side and the homepage shelf, the matcher result (UC-523), and the Studio operator view (UC-527) all read the same ranked list — a Drop that moves from #2 to #1 moves everywhere at once, never on one surface only. Recompute latency is PROPOSED as under 60 seconds from the triggering event to the new order being served (TO CONFIRM, open item 27b); a stale shelf that still shows yesterday's order after a sellout is a bug. Ranking changes are logged (UC-528) with the triggering event, so an operator can answer "why did this Drop move?" from the log, not from a guess.
- **UC-519** Top 5 card fields — video hero, dish, restaurant, Serves, total price, pickup window, units left (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Every Top 5 card shows, in this order: the video hero for the dish (UC-021, video is a must — no Drop without a real video reaches the shelf); dish name; restaurant name; Serves size (Bundle/Non-bundle rule, UC-205/UC-206 — Serves 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, or 6+, always shown for a Bundle); the TOTAL price the customer pays — one number, no fee surprise at checkout (the price is the price; Stripe/creator/restaurant splits are never shown to the customer); the pickup window (date + start/end time, restaurant timezone, UC-276, big type); and units left ("23 left", "Only 4 left", or "Sold out" per UC-520), computed live from atomic inventory (UC-105/UC-405). If the Drop is the restaurant's hero dish, the card also shows the UC-469 Hero Dish tag as an overlay on top of the video hero, immediately next to the red DROP badge (REVISED in V2.12 — same overlay placement as UC-469; the tag never covers the DROP badge). Tapping a card opens that Drop's Drop page (UC-376) with the creator/channel tag surviving (last-tagged-link-wins, LOCKED, UC-248). A card never shows a compare-at price, a countdown, or a viewer count that is not real (UC-528 — never faked, per the standing design rule).
- **UC-520** Sold-out and ended Drops — sold out stays visible until its window ends, then drops off (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. A Drop in the Top 5 that sells out stays on the shelf, visibly marked "Sold out", until its pickup window ends — it is not hidden the moment it sells out, because customers who claimed it still need to find it and because "sold out" is itself the scarcity proof (UC-176 sold-out display rule, applied to the shelf). While sold out it cannot be claimed (UC-105/UC-405) and it stops counting toward the cuisine chip (UC-476). The moment its pickup window ends, it drops off the shelf and the next-ranked live Drop moves up into the Top 5 (UC-518 recompute) — an ended Drop never occupies a Top 5 slot. A paused Drop (UC-059/UC-186), a cancelled Drop (UC-254), or a Drop whose confirmation was voided (UC-208/UC-472) leaves the shelf immediately, not at window end. A sold-out Drop's card still links to its Drop page, which shows the sold-out state and the next Drop / alert signup (UC-374), never a claim button that cannot work.
- **UC-521** Fewer than 3 live Drops — the shelf is not a real shelf; show what exists, invent nothing (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The Top 5 shelf is a real shelf only when at least 3 live Drops exist in the selected city. With 3–4 live Drops, the shelf shows those 3–4, in ranked order, with no filler and no empty slots — the title stays "Top 5 in [city] this week" and the customer sees what exists. With 1–2 live Drops, the shelf shows those 1–2 Drops with the same card (UC-519) and a plain note ("More Drops coming this week in [city]") — it does NOT invent filler Drops, does NOT repeat a Drop to fill 5 slots, and does NOT present itself as a full Top 5. With 0 live Drops, there is no Top 5 shelf at all — the homepage shows the UC-374 0-live empty state ("Next Drop coming soon in [city]" + alert signup), never an empty shelf. The matcher (UC-522) is likewise honest at low inventory: with only 1 live Drop, it must not present the matcher as finding a perfect match — it shows that Drop as the only live Drop, or says no live Drop fits (UC-525).
- **UC-522** "Not seeing what you're craving?" — the 2-question matcher, under the Top 5, two taps only (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Under the Top 5 shelf, in small type, is one line: "Not seeing what you're craving? Tell us in 2 taps and we'll find your best match." Tapping it opens the matcher — in the same thread on WhatsApp/SMS/Instagram, or as a small sheet on the website — and asks exactly TWO questions, each as tap chips, never as free text and never as a survey: **Q1 — What are you in the mood for?** [Indian] [Pizza] [Thai] [Mexican] [Chinese] [Surprise me] (the UC-466 cuisine list); **Q2 — Who's eating, and when?** Serves: [Just me] [2 people] [Family 4+] and pickup day: [Tonight] [Friday] — Q2 is one step with two chip rows, not two separate questions. Two taps total (one per question) and the matcher answers. There is no third question, no price question, no location question (the city is already the selected city, UC-516), and no account or login step — phone is the identity (UC-382). "Surprise me" skips Q1's cuisine filter and matches on Serves + pickup day + the UC-517 ranking alone.
- **UC-523** One best answer — ONE live Drop, with a plain-language reason why (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. After the two taps, the matcher shows exactly ONE best live Drop in the selected city — never a list of 10, never a ranked list of 5 — with its UC-519 card and one plain-language reason line: "Best for you in Frisco — Hyderabad House Chicken Biryani, Serves 4, pickup Friday 5–7pm. You picked Indian, family size, and it's the only live Drop that fits both." The reason names the customer's answers (cuisine, Serves, pickup day) and the fact that made this Drop the match (only live Drop that fits, or highest-ranked live Drop that fits, per UC-517). The only big button is Claim on that Drop; underneath, in small type, is "See all live Drops" (the UC-372 feed) for the minority who want to browse. If two Drops fit equally, the tie-break is the UC-517 ranking (UC-529) — the matcher never asks a third question to break a tie. The creator/channel tag survives the matcher exactly as it survives homepage navigation (UC-477): matching never strips a tag, and buying the matched Drop is still the tagged creator's sale (last-tagged-link-wins, LOCKED, UC-248).
- **UC-524** Preference memory — remembered by phone, never re-asked unless the customer taps Change (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. The customer's matcher answers (cuisine, Serves size, pickup day) are remembered against their phone number (UC-382, phone is identity, E.164-normalised) after the first match, in the same consent-aware store as §37 — the preference is a taste preference, not a marketing consent, and remembering it never implies a marketing opt-in (UC-385/UC-388 separation). On a return visit, the customer is never re-asked: the homepage shows "Your usual — Indian, family of 4, Friday pickup? [Yes, show me] [Change]" above the Top 5, and tapping "Yes, show me" runs the matcher with the remembered answers. Tapping "Change" re-opens the two questions (UC-522) and overwrites the stored preference. The preference is per phone, not per device: a customer who matches on WhatsApp and later opens the website with the same phone sees the same remembered preference (UC-384 returning recognition). A customer who never uses the matcher has no preference stored and sees the plain Top 5 (UC-516).
- **UC-525** No live Drop fits — say so honestly, show the closest match if any, offer a cuisine alert (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. If no live Drop in the selected city fits the customer's two answers, the matcher says so in plain words — never a forced sale, never a Drop presented as a fit when it is not: "No live Indian Drop in Frisco right now." If a closest match exists (same Serves + pickup day, different cuisine; or same cuisine, different Serves/day), it may be shown as exactly that — labelled "Closest match — Thai curry, Serves 2, pickup tonight. Not Indian, but it fits your family size and day." — with its UC-519 card and a Claim button, and the mismatch stated on the card. If no closest match exists, no Drop card is shown. In both cases the matcher offers one opt-in: "Text me when the next [cuisine] Drop goes live in [city]" — an alert signup (SMS/WhatsApp, unchecked by default, per-channel, UC-214/UC-387) scoped to that cuisine + city, so the customer is alerted only when a Drop in that cuisine goes live in that city (UC-471 scope rule, extended to the matcher). The alert signup is never pre-checked and never bundled with the claim.
- **UC-526** "Because you picked X" — Top 3 for that customer after a purchase (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. After a customer has claimed/picked up a Drop, the homepage shows a second shelf for that customer: "Because you picked [dish/cuisine] — Top 3 for you this week". It shows at most 3 live Drops in the selected city, each card following UC-519 (including the UC-469 Hero Dish tag overlaid on top of the picture, immediately next to the red DROP badge, on a hero-dish Drop — REVISED in V2.12), ranked by the UC-517 ranking, filtered to the customer's remembered preference (UC-524: their cuisine, Serves size, pickup day) — the same cuisine first, then the same Serves size, then the same pickup day, in that order. The row is shown only to a recognised returning customer (UC-384); a new customer never sees it. It never shows a Drop the customer already claimed for the same window, and it never shows a sold-out Drop as claimable (UC-520). The row's label always names what it is based on — "Because you picked Chicken Biryani last Friday" — so the personalisation is explainable, not creepy. If fewer than 1 live Drop fits the preference, the row is hidden entirely (UC-521 honesty rule, applied to the row) — it is never filled with unrelated Drops.
- **UC-527** Studio operator view — the live ranking, and why each Drop ranks where it does (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The Studio (Today, UC-424, and a "Top 5 this week" view under the website/homepage area, §36) shows, per city, the live Top 5 in ranked order, with each Drop's four UC-517 signals and their current values (sellout velocity, pickup reliability, repeat-buyer rate, recency boost) and the resulting rank — so an operator can see WHY a Drop is #1 and why another is #4, and can answer a restaurant's "why am I not in the Top 5?" from the signals, not from a guess. The view also shows the Drops just outside the Top 5 (#6–#8, labelled "next up") and the Drops that cannot rank (sold out, ended, paused, cancelled — UC-520, with the reason each is out). The operator can preview the customer homepage's Top 5 for a city from the Studio — the preview shows exactly the shelf a customer in that city sees (UC-516), including the sold-out state (UC-520) and the low-inventory note (UC-521). The operator view is read-only for ranking: there is no manual reorder, no paid boost, and no "pin to #1" control (UC-517) — changing a Drop's rank happens only by changing its underlying signals (a new claim, a pickup, a repeat purchase, a new Drop going live), never by dragging it.
- **UC-528** No fake badges, no fake counts — every ranking badge comes from real orders/pickup data, and every ranking change is logged (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. No badge, count, or label on the Top 5 shelf, on a matcher answer, or on a "Because you picked X" card is ever faked: "Selling fast" appears only when sellout velocity (UC-517a) crosses a stated threshold (PROPOSED: 50% of units claimed within the first 24 hours live — TO CONFIRM, open item 27c); "Only X left" is the live atomic inventory count (UC-105/UC-405); "Sold out" is the live inventory state (UC-520); a repeat-buyer or reliability claim is never shown as a customer-facing number unless it is computed from real orders. Every ranking change (a Drop entering/leaving the Top 5, or moving rank) is written to the audit log (UC-449) and to the structured logs (UC-437) with: Drop, old rank, new rank, triggering event (claim, pickup, sellout, new Drop live, window end), and the signal values at the time — so a ranking dispute ("why did we drop from #2?") is answered from the log. A ranking change with no log entry is a ranking change that never happened, as far as DealsPro is concerned.
- **UC-529** Tie-breaks, new-Drop entry, and mobile Top 3 — how the shelf behaves at the edges (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. (a) Tie-break: when two live Drops have the same ranking score (UC-517), the higher rank goes to the Drop with the earlier pickup window, then to the Drop that went live earlier — never to a paid or manual choice. (b) New-Drop entry: a newly live Drop enters the ranking at once (UC-518 recompute) with its recency boost (UC-517d) — it does not wait for a nightly batch, and it does not start at #1 by default: it ranks where its signals put it. (c) Mobile Top 3: on mobile the shelf shows 3 cards first (UC-516) — the 3 shown are ranks #1–#3, in order; ranks #4–#5 are one scroll away, in order, never reordered for mobile. (d) Navigation: tapping a Top 5 card opens the Drop page (UC-376); going back returns to the same Top 5 order and scroll position — the shelf is not re-shuffled on navigation, and the matcher state (UC-522) is not lost if the customer goes back from a Drop page. (e) The Top 5 shelf, the matcher, and the "Because you picked X" row all respect the cuisine filter (UC-467) and the city (UC-468) as AND: a cuisine filter active on the homepage filters the Top 5 to that cuisine (showing fewer than 5 if fewer fit, UC-521), and the matcher answers are always within the selected city.

## §47 Food-ready notifications & customer check-in (NEW, PROPOSED)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided.** This is the food-ready notification layer on top of the pickup flow. It refines, and does not replace: §44 (UC-489–UC-502 pickup state, redemption record, reminders), the §45 draft (UC-503–UC-515 restaurant order management — the Ready tap UC-509 is the trigger for UC-530/UC-531; §47 assumes that tap exists and does not re-specify it), §26 (UC-270–UC-282 claim → confirm → Ready, UC-277 timeline, UC-278 countdown), §32 (UC-326–UC-333 messaging compliance & fatigue), §37 (UC-382–UC-393 identity & opt-in recognition), §40 (UC-410–UC-417 operator alerts), and §42 (UC-435 monitoring, UC-449 audit log, UC-453 P0 alerts). Source: deep research 'Food-ready pickup notifications' (Nov 15, 2026) + page artifact food-ready-pickup-notifications — one-tap batch-ready SMS (Twilio, ~$1.18–$1.33 per 100 ready texts, verified live), WhatsApp utility fallback (price unverified), two-way check-in, live 2-state status page. **Dependency, recorded honestly:** UC-509 lives in the unmerged §45 draft — if §45 is not merged, UC-509 must be specified as a case before UC-530/UC-531 can be built (open item 28f).

- **UC-530** One-tap per-order Ready sends one personal ready message (P0, Phase 3) — NEW, PROPOSED. When staff tap Mark Ready on one order in the private pickup-link view (UC-509, §45 draft), the system sends that customer ONE personal ready message on their channel (SMS first, UC-535): first name + restaurant + dish + order/QR link + pickup window. The message is sent by the status change itself — there is no second 'send SMS' tap. The Ready tap is the only trigger; a ready message is never sent on a timer alone (no major system in the research sends 'ready' on a timer — Toast, Square, DoorDash and Uber Eats all send on a staff tap or a KDS bump).
- **UC-531** One-tap batch Ready sends personal 1-to-1 messages, staggered (P0, Phase 3) — NEW, PROPOSED. 'Mark batch ready' on a Drop / pickup window sends a personal 1-to-1 ready message to each order in the batch (name + order/QR link each), as a server-side loop, staggered/rate-limited so carriers do not flag a burst — never a group thread (a mass text must go as individual 1-to-1 threads, never a group thread, per bulk-SMS practice in the research). The staff experience is one tap; the customer experience is personal. No named restaurant product was found offering batch-ready as a feature — this design is inferred from bulk-SMS mechanics, not copied.
- **UC-532** Batch send confirm screen (P0, Phase 3) — NEW, PROPOSED. Before any batch ready message is sent, a confirm screen shows the exact count ('Send ready to 47 customers?') and the Drop/window, and requires an explicit confirm; cancelling sends nothing. The confirm also asks 'Is the food bagged?' (UC-537) — the confirm is the staff's statement that it is. A batch tap fires 40–100 messages at once, so a mistake spams everyone and brings a crowd to food that is not bagged; the confirm screen is the guard.
- **UC-533** Ready is idempotent — one ready message per order per Drop (P0, Phase 3) — NEW, PROPOSED. A ready message is sent at most once per order per Drop. Re-marking an order Ready (e.g. moved back to Active and marked Ready again — Toast re-sends the SMS in exactly this case) sends nothing unless staff explicitly choose 'Re-send ready message', a separate, logged action with a reason. A duplicate ready message is spam, not a reminder.
- **UC-534** Ready message template — 160 characters max, no offers (P0, Phase 3) — NEW, PROPOSED. The ready message is one fixed template (120 characters in the research demo): 'Hi [Name], your [Dish] from [Restaurant] is ready for pickup! Show this QR at the counter: [link]'. It carries first name, restaurant, dish, and the order/QR link; it never carries an offer, a promo code, or a marketing line — an offer inside a utility message reclassifies it as marketing under Meta's template rules (UC-545) and breaks the transactional consent basis.
- **UC-535** Channel order — SMS first, WhatsApp fallback, RCS later only (P0, Phase 3) — NEW, PROPOSED. Ready messages go by SMS first (Twilio + A2P 10DLC brand/campaign registration — brand $4.50/$46, campaign vetting $15, campaign $1.50–$10/mo, review 10–15 days, per secondary sources at research time), with a Meta-approved WhatsApp utility template as the second channel/fallback. RCS is NOT a primary channel — only a later upgrade, always with automatic SMS fallback (Twilio RCS dollar pricing was unverified at research time). WhatsApp US utility pricing (~$0.0034–$0.004/template, secondary consensus — Meta's official page returned 403 at research time) is unverified: re-check before budgeting WhatsApp as cheaper than SMS (Twilio US SMS $0.0083/segment + carrier $0.0035–$0.0045 = ~$0.0118–$0.0133 per 1-segment SMS, verified live; 100 ready texts ≈ $1.18–$1.33).
- **UC-536** Delivery receipts per order, one auto-retry, no retry of permanent failures (P0, Phase 3) — NEW, PROPOSED. Every ready message records its delivery state per order (sent / delivered / failed) via the provider callback (Twilio StatusCallback). A failed/undelivered message retries once on the other channel (WhatsApp-first → SMS). Permanent failures (invalid number) are never retried, and a failed message costs $0.001 (Twilio failed-message fee). A failed ready message is visible on the operator board (UC-547) — a blocked sender halts marketing sends and alerts the operator (UC-435/UC-453). 'Sent' is not 'delivered': delivery is not guaranteed by the send call.
- **UC-537** Ready is tapped only when the food is bagged and staged (P0, Phase 3) — NEW, PROPOSED. Staff tap Ready only when that order's food is physically bagged, labelled with the order code, and on the pickup shelf. A 'ready' sent before the food is bagged is a trust failure — documented in Chipotle/DoorDash customer reviews: '35 minutes late after we got the notification it was ready for pick up', 'Waited almost 45min after I got notification my order was ready', 'Food was cold even though I picked it up myself'. The batch confirm screen (UC-532) asks 'Is the food bagged?' — the tap is a statement that it is. A Ready tap used to clear a queue or dispatch early (DoorDash's own guidance says don't wait for a Dasher before tapping ready — a delivery optimisation that backfires for pickup) is a misuse of this case.
- **UC-538** In-store ready board — Preparing → Ready, the always-on fallback (P0, Phase 3) — NEW, PROPOSED. The restaurant's counter shows a ready board — the same private-link order list, or a screen/tablet at the counter — with orders in Preparing → Ready columns by name and/or number (Flybuy Order Ready Board / INFI Display pattern). The board is the always-on channel: a customer whose text failed, or who has no smartphone, still collects by showing their QR or giving name + order code (UC-500 identity check: first name + last 4 phone digits, proposed). The board never needs a message to work, and it costs nothing per message — the screen/tablet the restaurant already has plus the dashboard build.
- **UC-539** Check-in link in the confirmation message — 'On my way' / 'I'm here' (P0, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. The order confirmation message (UC-267) carries a no-login check-in link. Tapping it opens a page with two buttons — 'On my way' and 'I'm here' — no login, no app, no location sharing. The link is tied to the order (QR/phone token), never a shared link. This is Popmenu's 'reply 1' + Olo's 'I'm here' + Fresh KDS's 'I'm Here' link, adapted to counter pickup — the highest-value innovation for a 2–3 hour window in the research.
- **UC-540** Check-in re-sorts the counter queue by arrival (P1, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. A check-in tap marks that order Arriving / Here on the restaurant's private-link dashboard, and the dashboard sorts the handoff queue by arrival, not by order time — staff work by who's arriving next (Flybuy's rule: 'staff work by who's arriving next, not by order time'). The customer's name/number is shown with their arrival state; a 'Here' tap moves the order to the front of the handoff queue. The queue is never sorted by order time once a check-in exists for that order.
- **UC-541** Check-in is a prioritisation signal, never a precondition (P1, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. Food is staged by batch (UC-531/UC-537) whether or not the customer checks in. A check-in only reorders the handoff queue (UC-540) — it never gates pickup, never delays bagging for non-check-ins, and a customer who never taps anything still collects normally. Low adoption is expected (many customers will ignore the link and just turn up), so the kitchen cannot rely on check-in for timing; a flood of 'here' taps in the last 15 minutes must not overwhelm a counter whose bagging was not staged.
- **UC-542** Optional auto-reply on check-in (P1, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. When a customer taps 'I'm here', the system may send one short auto-reply: 'We're bagging yours now — come to the counter.' The auto-reply is optional per Drop (PROPOSED default: on — TO CONFIRM, open item 28e), counts inside the UC-544 message cap, and never carries an offer. The auto-reply is a status update, not a marketing message.
- **UC-543** Live 2-state status page — Preparing → Ready, never a minute ETA (P1, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. The confirmation message states the window and a no-login status link (QR/phone token). The page shows only two states — Preparing → Ready (plus 'window opens at …') — and never a minute ETA, never a countdown. The page reads the same Ready state as the board (UC-538); it is the answer to 'is it ready yet?' without another text. Do not promise minute-accurate ETAs: Domino's own accuracy depends on staff inputs + ML the platform does not have (DomOS AI tracker, 2026).
- **UC-544** Message cap — max 2 per order per Drop, plus at most 1 reminder (P0, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. A customer gets at most 2 messages per order per Drop (confirmation + ready), plus at most 1 reminder (from the UC-492–UC-496 schedule). No offers are ever added inside a utility message (Meta reclassifies it as marketing, UC-534/UC-545). The cap is enforced server-side per order; a third message is never sent by a staff tap, a re-send, or an auto-reply — UC-533 re-sends and UC-542 auto-replies both count against the cap.
- **UC-545** Consent separation — ready alerts are transactional, marketing is separate (P0, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. A ready alert is a transactional message sent on the checkout-phone consent (prior express consent under TCPA — the customer gave their number for this order; marketing needs prior express *written* consent, a higher bar). Marketing opt-in stays separate, unchecked by default, per-channel (UC-385–UC-388), and is never bundled into a ready message or asked inside one. A number given for order updates is NOT consent for marketing. A utility template that adds a promo code is reclassified as marketing (higher price; US marketing templates were reported paused Apr 2025–Aug 2026 in one secondary source — verify before any marketing use). This case states the design rule, not legal advice — get counsel/provider review before launch.
- **UC-546** Quiet hours, STOP/HELP, one consistent sender (P0, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. No marketing message goes out 9:00 PM–9:00 AM local (PROPOSED; stricter 8am–8pm in FL/WA/MD/OK per secondary sources — TO CONFIRM with counsel/provider, open item 28c). Transactional ready alerts during a live pickup window are sent at any hour the window runs (the transactional quiet-hours exemption is described in one secondary guide as exempt — verify before relying on it). Every message carries the restaurant name + order reference, comes from one consistent DealsPro sender (one Twilio number / one WhatsApp business profile), and honors STOP/HELP immediately (CTIA: confirmation must state HELP, opt-out method, recurring nature + frequency; never use rented/sold/shared opt-in lists).
- **UC-547** Operator sees per-order message state (P1, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. The Studio board shows, per order, the ready-message state (not sent / sent / delivered / failed) from UC-536, alongside the pickup state (UC-489/UC-501) — so the operator can answer 'did they get the ready text?' from the board, not from a guess. A blocked sender halts marketing sends automatically and alerts the operator (UC-435/UC-453, P1 for messaging). The operator can re-send a failed ready message once, logged, inside the UC-544 cap — a re-send is never automatic and never silent.

## §48 Five-step pickup timeline — Claimed → Accepted → Preparing → Ready → Picked Up (NEW, PROPOSED)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided.** This is the single canonical pickup state machine for every order, from the Nov 16, 2026 Apple-style seamless-pickup research (deep research 'DealsPro seamless pickup — Apple-style', Nov 16–17, 2026 + page artifact dealspro-seamless-pickup). Mahendra's instruction (Nov 16, 2026): "I like the 5 step process claim to picked up add it the file studio use card to build". The five states are the customer-facing timeline and the restaurant's working states in one model — they do not replace §26, §44, or §47, they unify them: §26 is the Claim → Accept leg, §44 is the pickup-state record + reminders, §47 is the Ready message layer. Where §48 and an earlier case disagree, the conflict is recorded here, not silently resolved — see open item 29. Source: Starbucks Mobile Order, Chick-fil-A Mobile Thru, Sweetgreen/Panera Rapid Pick-Up, Domino's Tracker, Apple Store pickup (all in the Nov 16–17 research report); Apple HIG eight principles (Purpose, Agency, Responsibility, Familiarity, Flexibility, Simplicity, Craft, Delight — mirrored text, verify live before quoting verbatim). **No POS, no app, no full-menu sync** — the timeline lives on one no-login web page + SMS, per the standing no-POS rule.

- **UC-548** One pickup state per order — five states, server-side, timestamped (P0, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. Every paid order has exactly one pickup state, held server-side (never on the phone alone): **Claimed → Accepted → Preparing → Ready → Picked Up**. Each transition is timestamped (claimed-at, accepted-at, preparing-at, ready-at, picked-up-at) and audit-logged (UC-449). The state moves only on a real event: payment → Claimed; restaurant Accept tap → Accepted; kitchen starts the batch / first bag → Preparing; restaurant Ready tap → Ready; QR scan or manual-code handover → Picked Up. No state is ever set by a timer alone, and no state is ever skipped — an order cannot go Claimed → Ready without passing Accepted and Preparing. Picked Up is terminal: never re-redeemed, never cancelled, never refunded as unclaimed (UC-502). Unclaimed / No-show is a terminal branch off Ready, not a sixth state (open item 29d).
- **UC-549** Customer timeline — one live page, five states, no login (P0, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. The customer sees the same five states on one no-login web page (short link from SMS + confirmation): Claimed ✓ (paid, window + address + order code, e.g. A-37) → Accepted ✓ (restaurant name + timestamp, "They've got your order — no need to come early") → Preparing (live "Ready by ~…" estimate that moves only if the restaurant taps Running late, UC-556) → Ready (full-screen change, green, "READY FOR PICKUP", QR top half + code + first name, address, pickup-by time) → Picked Up (confirmation + thank-you, timeline closed). One primary action per state, thumb-reachable at the bottom; no "check status" button — the page updates itself. The timeline never shows internal states ("fired", "bagged") and never shows a minute ETA — only the five states + timestamps, Domino's/Apple pattern.
- **UC-550** Restaurant timeline — same five states, same timestamps, one phone page (P0, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. The restaurant sees the same five states on its private pickup-link page (no login, no POS, no printer): an Accept queue (Claimed orders, Accept / Accept All, accept-deadline countdown — UC-270–UC-282, UC-394), a kitchen board (one batch card per Drop, not one ticket per order — dish × N, waves, bagged count — UC-531/UC-537), and a handover view (Ready orders, Scan mode). Customer and restaurant always see the same state and the same timestamps for an order; if they ever disagree, the server state governs and the disagreement is a P0 incident (UC-436).
- **UC-551** Preparing — what it means, when it starts (P0, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. Preparing starts when the kitchen starts that order's batch/wave — not at Accept, not at payment. The customer sees "Preparing now" and the Ready-by estimate; the restaurant sees the wave in progress. Food is cooked in 2–3 waves across the 2–3 hour window (UC-531), never all at window start — a 2–3 hour window exceeds the 2-hour room-temperature food-safety limit if cooked at the start. Preparing never sends a message by itself; only the Ready tap sends the Ready SMS (UC-530/UC-535, §47).
- **UC-552** Ready — tap-only, SMS Ready, QR on top (P0, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. Ready is set only by the restaurant's Ready tap (per-order or Batch Ready, UC-530/UC-531) when the food is physically bagged, labelled with the order code, and on the staff-only rack. The tap sends ONE personal Ready SMS (UC-535: first name + restaurant + dish + QR link + pickup-by time) — never on a timer, never before bagging; a false Ready (text sent, bag not ready) is a trust failure and an incident (UC-547 board flags it). The Ready screen shows the QR at top half, black-on-white, short payload, 4-module quiet zone, auto-brightness, with the 3-character pickup code + first name in large type underneath as the fallback (DoorDash last-3-characters pattern). The QR is single-use and dies on scan (UC-553).
- **UC-553** Picked Up — QR scan or code fallback, single-use, terminal (P0, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. Handover is by QR scan in Scan mode: staff phone camera open, customer shows QR → green flash + chime + "A-37 · [First name] · [Dish] ×2 — HAND OVER" → bag handed from the staff-only labelled rack behind the counter (never an open self-serve shelf — the Starbucks/Panera/Chipotle wrong-person failure). If the scan fails, staff type the 3-character code; if the phone is dead, staff search by first name + code from the Ready list — name alone is never enough. On scan the QR/code dies: a second presentation shows red "Already picked up [time]". Picked Up is terminal (UC-548/UC-502) and the handover is logged with timestamp + redeemer.

- **UC-555** Every state change is audited; operator board shows the timeline (P1, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. Every transition (UC-548) is audit-logged with actor (customer / restaurant / system), timestamp, and order/Drop (UC-449). The Studio operator board shows, per order, the current state + all five timestamps + message state (UC-547) + time remaining in the window, with a "not yet picked up" filter (UC-501). The operator can answer "where is order A-37?" from the board in one glance, without calling the restaurant.
- **UC-556** Running late, early arrival, dead phone — handled, not improvised (P1, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. Running late: one tap "+10 / +20 min" re-times the Ready-by estimate and texts not-yet-ready customers the new time *before* the old time passes; a delay over ~30 minutes offers one-tap "Keep waiting / Refund me" (restaurant fault = refund). Early arrival: the timeline says "Preparing — ready by ~…"; staff give the same time; no early release, no counter queue. Dead phone / no data: printed/email confirmation with code, or staff search by name + customer states the 3-character code (UC-553 fallback); never turn a customer away for a dead battery. "I'm on my way" is an optional one-tap kitchen signal only — it never triggers cooking, and no geofencing is built (unreliable, §1.3 of the research).
- **UC-557** Per-Drop pickup scorecard — ready on time, counter wait, first-scan, no-shows (P1, Phase 2) — NEW, PROPOSED. Within 1 hour of window end, DealsPro auto-generates a one-page per-Drop scorecard from the UC-548 timestamps + UC-536 delivery receipts: % Ready on time (PROPOSED target ≥95%), median counter wait (PROPOSED <60s, p90 <3min), QR first-scan success (PROPOSED ≥95%), no-show rate (PROPOSED <3%), wrong-person incidents (target 0), order accuracy (PROPOSED ≥99%). Targets are PROPOSED, to validate in the first 3–5 Drops (open item 29e). The scorecard goes to the restaurant and to the operator; a Drop under 95% Ready-on-time is reviewed before the next Drop with that restaurant.

---

## §49 DealsPro MCP server (NEW, PROPOSED)

**Status: PROPOSED, not decided.** The MCP server is the open door to DealsPro: any assistant (general assistant, advisor's copilot, future agents) can call it, while DealsPro keeps control of truth and money. Source: the Nov 19–20 MCP brainstorm + `dealspro-mcp-section-matcher-cut-proposal.md` (PROPOSED amendment, Nov 20, 2026). Mahendra's instruction (Nov 21, 2026): "add all these tools to the studio and requirements file including code base" — the tool catalog below, including the Code Mode candidate, is added at his instruction. The MCP-open decision itself (agreement 0.2 — MCP server open to all assistants, general assistant in front, DealsPro's domain micro-agents behind) is still UNCONFIRMED; §49 is written as if the door is open and is re-scoped if he decides otherwise. The Sep 28 settlement stands: the general assistant owns conversation and discovery; DealsPro owns availability truth, inventory reservation, checkout/payment, fulfillment status, QR issuance, redemption, refunds/chargebacks, and settlement/reporting. **No auto-publish, ever** — the two human gates (restaurant confirms the exact offer; Mahendra authorizes every publish) are never automated, and no tool — including run_code — can publish a Drop, move money, or bypass them.

- **UC-558** Tool catalog — small and distinct (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The MCP server exposes a small, fixed tool catalog: search_drops, get_drop, claim_drop, get_order, cancel_order, get_pickup_status, list_restaurants — plus run_code as a CANDIDATE 8th tool (Code Mode, see below). Tools are distinct enough that a model never confuses two of them. New tools are added only with a distinct name and purpose; the catalog never grows past the point where tool choice degrades (past ~30–50 available tools, models pick wrong ones; DealsPro stays far below that by design). run_code (CANDIDATE, not locked): Code Mode — instead of the model making tool calls one by one, it writes one short script that loops the MCP tools as plain functions; the script runs in an isolated sandbox with no open internet access, talking only to the MCP server under the caller's existing permissions, audit-logged, and the model gets back only a short summary (e.g. "reconciliation complete: 42 orders, 0 mismatches"). Cloudflare's demo of the same pattern (31 calendar events, one script) used 81% fewer tokens — that figure is Cloudflare's claim, not a DealsPro measurement. Code Mode is a pattern, not a Cloudflare product: it needs no Cloudflare and runs on DealsPro's own stack (Vercel). Bulk jobs it covers: Scout/Hunter scanning many merchants and dishes, the Connector routing one Drop to many customers, Payout & Reconcile running the daily Stripe-vs-Supabase check, ranking the Top 5. It changes how calls are made, not what is allowed.
- **UC-559** Deferred loading / just-in-time tools (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. Tools load only when the conversation turns to food: a tool-search step expands only the 3–5 matching tools into full definitions. DealsPro's tools stay out of the way until needed — same "just in time" idea as the Drop itself.
- **UC-560** Model proposes, server disposes (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The model never touches Supabase, Stripe, or Twilio. It writes a structured note ("please run search_drops" / "please run claim_drop"); the MCP server executes it — atomic inventory check, Stripe charge, QR issuance — and returns text. The model can only ask; the server acts and enforces the rules.
- **UC-561** Server placed on Vercel; host on provider servers (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The DealsPro MCP server runs on Vercel as the gatekeeper between the host and Supabase/Stripe/Twilio. The host (Muse/ChatGPT/Claude), the model, and the MCP client run on the assistant provider's servers; the phone is only the screen. The server is the only thing that talks to Supabase, Stripe, and Twilio, and the only thing that enforces the rules. It must be remote, not on the customer's phone, because it touches live inventory and money.
- **UC-562** Server-enforced ordering (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. An agent may call tools in the wrong order; the server must not allow it. The §48 state machine (Claimed → Accepted → Preparing → Ready → Picked Up) is enforced server-side: a claim cannot precede a live Drop, a payment cannot precede an atomic inventory decrement, a QR cannot issue before payment, a Ready cannot be sent before the restaurant taps Ready, a pickup cannot record before a QR/code scan. Out-of-order calls are rejected with a plain-text reason, never silently repaired.
- **UC-563** Identity handshake — phone is identity (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. How does the server know which customer the agent is acting for? Proposed rule: the checkout phone is the identity (per §37, UC-382), E.164-normalised. The handshake design itself is NOT settled — per-user OAuth2 with attribution (the Stack Overflow / MCP explainer pattern Mahendra asked to remember, Nov 19) is the candidate pattern. This case records the requirement; the handshake mechanism needs a separate design decision before build (open item 30d).
- **UC-564** Tool-call audit (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Every MCP tool call is audit-logged (§42, UC-449): who called (agent/host), which tool, which Drop/order, inputs, result, timestamp. A money move with no log entry is a money move that never happened. Logs feed the daily Stripe-vs-Supabase reconciliation.
- **UC-565** RAG as a cross-cutting rule (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The assistant only states retrieved facts about live Drops — dish, price, Serves, pickup window, units left — from the live catalog, never from training data and never invented. If it isn't retrieved, it isn't said. A wrong answer breaks trust, so grounding is non-negotiable; it is plumbing, not a feature.
- **UC-566** Contract-first before parallel building (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. Before build workstreams parallelise, one shared schema/API contract is written and agreed (the gRPC lesson from Nov 19: steal the contract-first .proto idea, not gRPC itself — gRPC pays off only at thousands of calls a second, and one-Drop validation scale is REST/HTTP). The Drop page and MCP server stay HTTP/REST. No workstream changes the shared contract unilaterally.
- **UC-567** No agent wrapping an agent (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. Never an agent wrapping tools wrapping an agent. If the job is "do X reliably" → tool. If it's "decide what to do / talk to a human" → agent. The Gmail middleman-agent lesson (Nov 19): agents already call APIs directly; the APIs themselves must be designed well for agents.

## §50 Mobile light loading — claim first (NEW, LOCKED)

**Status: LOCKED (Mahendra, Nov 22, 2026).** The Drop page is built for a phone on a weak connection: it loads only what the claim needs, when it needs it, and the claim loads first. Source: the Nov 22, 2026 GTA 6 engine discussion (Mahendra: "For DealsPro everything is on mobile so light loading as needed helps to be fast" — "So let's make this as a requirement"). The 2-second budget is LOCKED as proposed (open item 31 RESOLVED).

- **UC-568** Mobile light loading — the claim loads first (P0) — NEW, LOCKED (Mahendra, Nov 22, 2026). Everything in DealsPro is on a phone, so the customer Drop page loads light and as needed: only what the claim requires, when it requires it. The claim comes first — the Drop's essential facts (dish, price, Serves, pickup window, units left) and a tappable Claim button render and work before the hero video, the photo, and any non-essential detail finish loading. A customer on slow data can claim while the rest streams in behind it; nothing that is not needed to claim may block the claim. This is load order, not visual order: the hero video stays the visual hero (UC-228) — the rule is that the Claim button is usable before the video finishes loading, and the video never auto-plays before the claim is usable. Images and detail sections lazy-load after the claim is interactive. LOCKED budget: claim interactive within 2 seconds on a throttled 4G connection in staging, measured by UC-461 monitoring (open item 31 RESOLVED). Refines the mobile-first lock (UC-243) and the one-thumb rule (UC-225) for the customer Drop page. Same "load only what you need" rule as the agent side (UC-559 deferred loading), applied to the customer's phone. The frame that matters loads first.

## §51 Drop timing — publish, claim cut-off, pickup (NEW, PROPOSED)

**Status: PROPOSED (Nov 23, 2026, at Mahendra's instruction — "add this requirements to the living spec").** The Drop timing playbook from the Nov 22–23, 2026 deep research (`dealspro-drop-timing-playbook` page; report `~/workspace/research_notes/dealspro-drop-timing-playbook-20261123-0541/report.md`). Three schedules, ranked: C Hybrid first, A same-day second, B 24-hour cut-off for complex dishes only. The early-lock hypothesis is PARTIALLY SUPPORTED: dinner is a same-day decision, but the decision window is broad and late (roughly 2–5 PM), not a sharp 2–3 PM hour — a 3 PM cut-off alone would miss the peak deciders, which is why the Hybrid opens claims two days early. **Two conflicts, handled here, not hidden:** (1) Option A (same-day publish) conflicts with the LOCKED 48-hour minimum lead (open item 18b) — it runs only as a time-boxed second test via the locked operator override (UC-396: explicit, reasoned, audit-logged). (2) UC-577 (15-minute grace) is the proposed single unclaimed rule that would settle open items 26(b) and 29(d), revising UC-497's no-grace wording if adopted — do not build both. The §51 cut-off (claim/cancel before prep) is distinct from §25's Ready-based cancel rules (§25 governs changes after prep/Ready). See open item 32.

- **UC-569** First Drop uses the Hybrid schedule — Option C (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Publish 2 days ahead (e.g. Mon 2 PM for Wed 6–8 PM pickup); claims open from publish; final-call SMS 1:30 PM day-of; hard claim cut-off 3 PM; batch prep 3–5:30 PM; pickup 6–8 PM; 15-minute grace to 8:15 PM; window closes. Cap 25 for the first validation. Success metric: sell ≥20 of 25, ≥90% pickup rate, prep finished by 5:30 PM with no rush-hour cooking.
- **UC-570** Claim cut-off is 3 hours before pickup-window start (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Hard stop — no claims, no extensions after cut-off; the final claimed count is locked and money reconciles to that count. Cancellation with full refund also closes at the cut-off (UC-575).
- **UC-571** Cut-off moves to 24 hours if the restaurant cannot do morning mise en place (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. The 3-hour cut-off (UC-570) applies only if the restaurant does morning mise en place (marinate, chop, soak rice, par-cook) and confirms the exact offer the day before, so 3 PM is assembly + final cook, not a cold start. If it cannot, the cut-off moves to 24 hours before pickup (Option B, UC-573). Do not use a 1–2 hour cut-off for a first Drop — no evidence a kitchen can batch-cook 20–30 family trays from scratch in that time. Cross-ref: UC-403 (prep lead time).
- **UC-572** Same-day Drop — Option A, second test (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. Publish + SMS 10 AM day-of; final-call SMS 1:30 PM; cut-off 3 PM; prep 3–5:30 PM; pickup 6–8 PM + 15-min grace. Used as the second validation test, or when the restaurant can prep from on-hand stock. EXCEPTION to the locked 48-hour lead (open item 18b): same-day publish runs only as a time-boxed second test via the locked operator override (UC-396 — explicit, reasoned, audit-logged). The 48-hour lead rule itself stands.
- **UC-573** 48-hour advance with 24-hour cut-off — Option B, complex dishes only (P2) — NEW, PROPOSED. Publish 48 hours ahead; claims close 24 hours before pickup; unrushed prep the day of; pickup 6–8 PM. Used only when the bundle needs an ingredient order, a long prep (e.g. 48-hour brine/marinade), or the restaurant insists on a full day of prep. Do not use for the first validation of demand — it confounds "do people want this?" with "do people plan dinner 2 days ahead?"
- **UC-574** Claim window is roughly one day of effective decision time (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Not one hour (very short limits feel coercive — a 1-hour offer cut spending 68.3% vs no limit in the reactance experiments, vs +56.6% for a 1-day offer) and not one week (destroys the Drop premise). Hybrid C's ~46 hours is a deliberate stretch of the ~24-hour window to buy prep time, mitigated by the hard cut-off, the live claimed count (UC-582), and the same-day final-call SMS.
- **UC-575** Cancellation — full refund until the claim cut-off, no refund after (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Full refund if cancelled by the claim cut-off (3 PM day-of in Options A/C; 24 hours before in B). After cut-off, no refund — the food is being cooked for that customer. Stricter than Too Good To Go's 2-hour cancellation rule, because TGTG resells surplus already made and DealsPro cooks to order. Refines §25 (UC-254–UC-269) for the Drop-timing context; does not override the restaurant-direct model (UC-479–UC-488).
- **UC-576** Transfer — send someone else with the QR, no charge (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. The customer may send someone else with the QR code to pick up at no charge. This is the cheapest no-show reducer.
- **UC-577** Grace — 15 minutes past the pickup window (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. 15 minutes past the window (to 8:15 PM for a 6–8 PM window), stated in the SMS so it is a promise, not a negotiation.
