No source prescribes a grace period for hot pickup; 15 minutes is a judgment call, bounded by the 30-minute delay threshold in Too Good To Go's store terms and by the 4-hour holding clock (UC-580). If adopted, this is the single unclaimed rule that settles open items 26(b) and 29(d) and revises UC-497's no-grace wording — do not build both rules.
- **UC-578** No-show after grace — no refund, no credit, no exchange (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Record it, reconcile the money, and do not re-sell a hot bundle that has sat past its holding limit. Mirrors Too Good To Go (no refund for missed collection) and the bakery no-refund-for-unpicked-orders rule. Refines §25/§44 (UC-497) for the timing context.
- **UC-579** Restaurant fault — full refund (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Full refund if the restaurant cancels, is closed, runs out, or the food is unsafe/substandard. If pickup is delayed more than 30 minutes by the restaurant, offer a refund. Mirrors Too Good To Go's store terms.
- **UC-580** Food safety — hold hot at ≥135°F, 4-hour danger-zone clock (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The batch is actively held at ≥135°F (57°C); food spending more than 4 cumulative hours in the danger zone (41–135°F) is discarded — reheating does not reset the clock. The batch is time-marked; temperatures logged every 2 hours. A 2-hour pickup window is safe only if the batch is actively held hot or was cooked late enough that the 4-hour clock does not expire inside the window. Confirm the exact local (Dallas/Frisco/Prosper) health-department adoption — cities may amend the state code (Texas adopts the 2017 FDA Food Code, 25 TAC Ch. 228).
- **UC-581** SMS cadence — at most 3 texts per Drop (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. Announcement (at publish), optional slow-claims reminder (day before, 6 PM, only if claims are slow), final call (same day, 1:30 PM, 90 minutes before cut-off). 2–4 marketing texts per month per customer. TENSION, to be settled by open item 14(d): UC-250 proposes 1 marketing message per customer per week — if the 1/week cap is adopted, this cadence must shrink (announcement + final call only). List-level texts (this case) are distinct from per-order messages (UC-544: max 2 per order + 1 reminder). Refines §32.
- **UC-582** Operating rule — show the real claimed count live (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Show the real claimed count / remaining live (e.g. "14 of 25 claimed") — never a fake count or a resetting countdown. Falling availability causally lifts sales (Cui et al. 2019, Amazon Lightning Deals field experiment: 10% rise in past claims → +2.08% cart adds in the next hour); a deep discount without social proof underperforms. Refines §11/§46.
- **UC-583** Operating rule — state the rules in the first SMS, before payment (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The cut-off, pickup window, grace period, and no-refund-after-cut-off rule are stated in the first SMS, before payment — not discovered after it. (Too Good To Go's disputes cluster around its 2-hour cancellation boundary; DealsPro's boundary is the 3 PM cut-off.)
- **UC-584** Batch-prep window — 3 hours, three conditions (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. A 3-hour prep window (3:00–6:00 PM) is plausible but tight, and only on three conditions: (i) morning mise en place before the cut-off, so 3 PM is assembly + final cook, not a cold start; (ii) cooking in 2–3 parallel batches/trays, not one giant vessel; (iii) the exact bundle confirmed the day before so ingredients are on hand. Budget: ~30 min staging + 60–90 min cook/steam + 30 min pack/hold ≈ 2–2.5 hours, leaving ~30 min buffer before 6 PM. Verify with the first restaurant, in its own kitchen, before promising a 3 PM cut-off in the SMS — vessel/burner capacity is the unmeasured variable. If the restaurant needs 4 hours, move the cut-off to 2 PM (final SMS to 12:30 PM) or move pickup to 7 PM — do not compress prep to fit the marketing timeline. Cross-ref: UC-403.

## §52 Location gate — confirmed location before claim (NEW, PROPOSED)

**Status: PROPOSED (Nov 23, 2026, at Mahendra's instruction — "add the location gate and differentiation conclusions too"; the gate itself was DECIDED Nov 22, 2026 — Mahendra: "we should use combinations of this without this location detection we should not allow to move forward after or before hitting the claim").** The location-detection deep research (`dealspro-location-detection` page; report `~/workspace/research_notes/dealspro-user-location-detection-20261123-0330/report.md`) and the Nov 22, 2026 customer-preview build (location bar + Detect + Explore TX, gate wired into the claim flow) are the source. The gate is a claim-time rule, not a browsing rule: exploration stays open; the claim is what needs a confirmed location. The combination of methods is the decision — no single method is the gate on its own. See open item 33.

- **UC-585** A customer must have a confirmed location before claiming a Drop (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. No claim is created, no payment is taken, and no inventory is held until the customer's pickup city is confirmed. The gate sits at the moment of claim, BEFORE the claim is created — not after the tap. Browsing is never gated: a customer may explore Drops in any city without a confirmed location.
- **UC-586** The gate uses a combination of methods, not one (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. GPS detect (browser Geolocation API, user tap only, never on load), manual pick (any of the live Drops cities), saved home location (tied to the phone identity), IP guess (labelled a guess — "Frisco? Change" — never stored as fact), and the state → metro → city drill-down picker. No single method is the gate: if one fails, the customer falls through to the next; denial of one method never dead-ends the claim.
- **UC-587** GPS detect snaps to the nearest Drops city (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. When the customer taps Detect, the browser's Geolocation API returns latitude/longitude + accuracy; the system snaps to the nearest city where Drops are live and shows the actual detected place name when the reverse-geocode answers. Out-of-area → the nearest live city is offered, never silently substituted. Permission off or denied → the manual picker is offered immediately, with no re-prompt loop (browsers remember a denial and will not re-prompt).
- **UC-588** A confirmed location carries through the session (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. Once a city is confirmed, later claims in the same session do not re-gate; the Drop page shows a live location line (not confirmed / confirmed-matching / confirmed-different) and the location bar shows the confirmed status.
- **UC-589** City mismatch warns, never blocks (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. If the confirmed city differs from the Drop's city, the gate warns with the distance + the pickup window and requires an explicit "claim anyway" tap (twice-confirmed) — it never blocks the claim. The customer chooses with the facts in front of them.
- **UC-590** A second gate check guards the payment step (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The confirmed location is re-checked at payment: if the location was cleared or changed between claim and payment, the payment step re-gates before any money moves. The gate is enforced server-side, not only in the page.
- **UC-591** IP geolocation is a provisional guess only (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. IP-derived city is used only as a silent provisional default on first paint, always labelled a guess, never stored as a fact. IP cannot separate cities 6–13 miles apart (Frisco/Prosper/Plano): city accuracy is ~66% within 50 km on fixed networks and far worse on mobile networks (median error 179–207 km in a May 2026 study) — so an IP guess is never treated as a confirmed location.
- **UC-592** First-use flow — guess, then tap, then picker, then save (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. First paint from the IP guess with a tappable city chip → in-context "Use my location" → manual picker fallback → save the confirmed city to the phone identity. The resolved city is stored, not the raw coordinates.
- **UC-593** Precise geolocation is sensitive data — consent required (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Precise location (within 1,750 ft) is SENSITIVE data under the Texas Data Privacy and Security Act (TDPSA, effective Jul 1, 2024): consent is required before collection, and a data-protection assessment is required. The privacy notice states in plain language what location is collected, why (matching the customer to the right pickup city), and that raw coordinates are not stored. Coarse IP-derived city is outside the 1,750-ft definition. Confirm the TDPSA small-business exemption question with counsel — it is a legal determination, not established by the research.
- **UC-594** Native app vs web — same GPS, different reliability (P2) — NEW, PROPOSED. A permitted web page already gets 3–10 m GPS accuracy — a native app has no different satellites; its edge is reliability, control, and background location, not precision. Mobile web has NO background location. The gate is built for the web first; no native-only location behaviour is assumed.

## §53 Differentiation — why a customer buys from DealsPro (NEW, PROPOSED)

**Status: PROPOSED (Nov 23, 2026, at Mahendra's instruction — "add the location gate and differentiation conclusions too").** The differentiation deep research (`why-buy-from-dealspro` page; report `~/workspace/research_notes/dealspro-differentiation-strategies-20261123-0424/report.md`). The headline: DealsPro cannot beat DoorDash (~65–67% US share) on selection or habit; smaller platforms that survived narrowed to one job DoorDash does badly. The five strategies below are the defensible stack, ranked. The evidence grades are carried here: peer-reviewed field experiments are stated as such; vendor and secondary claims are marked UNVERIFIED and must not be quoted as fact. See open item 34.

- **UC-595** Lead with a verifiable dollar saving on a meal that visibly feeds the group (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Every Drop shows the singles total, the bundle price, the dollars saved, and the portion guarantee ("feeds X") — the saving is computed from real single-item prices, never a fake compare-at price. This is strategy #1: the reason to switch. DoorDash pickup is the regular menu price (and app menus are marked up ~19% on average over in-store prices), so a real bundle saving is a genuine price advantage, not a coupon.
- **UC-596** Real scarcity, slow-window only (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Stated quantity + end time, honoured: the cap is real, the window closes when stated, timers never reset. Limited-quantity scarcity significantly drives impulse buying; limited-time alone is insignificant directly (Fathia 2023, n=160). Amazon field experiment (23,665 Lightning Deals): +10% past sales → +2.08% cart add-ins in the next hour. Fake or resetting timers risk FTC §5 action and destroy trust. Refines UC-582.
- **UC-597** Top-5 curation, not a catalogue (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Discovery is the Top 5 list only (UC-516) — the deal finds the customer; there is no browse-everything catalogue. The overload evidence is mixed and must not be overstated: the jam study showed 30% vs 3% purchase (6 vs 24 jams) but a meta-analysis of 50 studies found a mean effect of d=0.02 (Scheibehenne 2010); overload appears only when options are complex, preferences uncertain, and the customer is minimising effort (Chernev 2015) — which fits dinner-for-2+. Do NOT quote 10× as the expected lift. Refines §46.
- **UC-598** SMS-first, one-tap claim, no app (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The Drop arrives by SMS, claims in one tap, and is picked up by QR — no app, no account, no browsing. SMS open rate ~98% (industry estimate, UNVERIFIED) and 21–35% click-through vs email 2–3%; California Fish Grill SMS opt-ins visited every 120 vs 175 days, +69% lifetime value (Olo). Explicit opt-in (TCPA); more than 2 texts per week drives >15%/month unsubscribe. Refines §32/§51 (UC-581).
- **UC-599** Frictionless QR pickup as a time promise (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. Pickup is a stated time promise: claim → QR → hand-over, with the pickup window and grace stated up front (§51). No direct conversion test for QR pickup was found — vendor claims only (UNVERIFIED). DoorDash pickup already drops the delivery fee and tip, so DealsPro must beat it on total price + speed, not on pickup alone.
- **UC-600** Video-first to de-risk an unknown bundle (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. The hero video is the dish story: it shows the food, the portion, and the restaurant, so the customer can judge an unknown bundle before claiming. Vendor claim of 2.5× static engagement and 55% more likely to visit is UNVERIFIED; the only academic evidence is small (n=81, R²=0.37 for intention) — weak, and no Drop-page test exists. Video is a publish blocker (no Drop without a real video) and the video never carries price, date, or Claim text burned in. Refines §3/UC-228.
- **UC-601** Local-independent as tie-breaker, quantified honestly (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. "Support local" is a tie-breaker, never the lead reason: Mintel found 52% visit independents to support local and 56% will pay up to 10% more, but AYTM found only 10% definitely pay more and 39% pay nothing extra, and a Penn State study found a +18% local premium chosen but a +36% reversal. ChowNow's same-order test ($23.14 vs $31.86) shows price, not local sentiment, is the differentiator. Never present local sentiment as the reason to buy.
- **UC-602** Retention without subscription — endowed progress + first access (P2) — NEW, PROPOSED. No subscription, no points currency. Retention is goal-gradient endowed progress (a customer who has claimed gets a head start toward the next goal) plus first access to Drops. Evidence: Kivetz 2006 — 2 bonus stamps cut completion from a median of 15 to 10 days; but post-reward collapse follows if there is no next goal, so every reward must name the next goal. Streaks are UNVERIFIED for restaurants. Refines §19.
- **UC-603** AI chat and short video are distribution only (P2) — NEW, PROPOSED. Assistants and short video surface Drops; they do not sell them. DoorDash and Uber Eats are already in ChatGPT, most flows hand off to the app, and OpenAI scrapped Instant Checkout in March 2026 for failing to drive purchases. DealsPro's MCP door (§49) is the distribution mechanism; the claim, payment, and pickup stay in DealsPro. Refines §49.
- **UC-604** Do NOT copy Groupon (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Never: unlimited 50%-off (merchant kept ~25%), peak-hour displacement (discounts displacing full-price sales), daily deal-blast spam, or coupon-only customers who never return (Harvard D3; Wharton). The Drop rules that prevent the Groupon failure are the slow-window-only rule (UC-596), the capped quantity, the one-Drop-at-a-time queue, and the two human gates. Any proposal that re-introduces one of these four is a regression, not a feature.
- **UC-605** Do NOT copy DoorDash's lock-in (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. Never: catalogue breadth as the strategy, a DashPass-style $9.99/month lock-in, or hidden fees and menu markups (average 19% app premium over in-store, Gordon Haskett). DealsPro competes on the verifiable bundle saving (UC-595), the Top 5 (UC-597), and the one-tap claim (UC-598) — not on breadth, subscription, or fees.
- **UC-606** The bundle sweet spot is undecided — do not hard-code a percentage (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. The evidence conflicts: 12–18% vs 20–30% (practitioner claims) vs McDonald's 15% (Extra Value Meals at ~15% off singles). No sweet spot is locked. The first Drops test the saving in dollars (UC-595) and measure repeat purchase; the percentage is set from that evidence, not from a practitioner rule of thumb. The bundle must save money vs buying singles — that is the lock; the percentage is the open number.

## §56 Customer preview — greeting, dish sub-chips, Texas explorer, 4:30 PM alert, shareable Drop cards (NEW, PROPOSED)

**Status: PROPOSED (Nov 24, 2026, at Mahendra's instruction — "add the missing pieces to the spec").** The source is the Nov 22, 2026 customer-preview build (`dealspro-customer-preview` space): the home page, the 4:30 PM alert module, and the Drop and order pages. These cases write the preview's behaviour as requirements; the preview itself is a tap-through demo with sample data — no real orders, payments, or texts happen there. See open item 35.

- **UC-613** Greeting — time-of-day greeting with the customer's name (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. The homepage opens with a time-of-day greeting — "Good morning" / "Good afternoon" / "Good evening, [Name]" — using the name from the phone identity (UC-382) when it is known. A customer with no stored name sees the greeting without a name — never "Guest" and never a placeholder. The line under it names the frame: "All Indian, all bundles — what is the table craving tonight?" The greeting never blocks the feed, never asks a question, and never gates anything.
- **UC-614** Dish sub-chips — a second filter row under the cuisine chips (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. Under the cuisine chips (UC-467) sits a second row of dish-group chips: All Bundles, Biryani Bundles, North Indian, South Indian, Tandoori Grill, Veg Bundles, Family · Serves 4+, Fastest Pickup, Biggest Saving. Tapping one filters the Live and Coming bundle lists to that group; only one is active at a time; tapping the active chip again clears it. A group with no live or coming bundle in the selected city is not shown — the same hide-when-empty rule as UC-465. The list of groups is operator-set, like the cuisine list (UC-466); a restaurant cannot add a group.
- **UC-615** 16-city row — the Texas explorer city row (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. The explorer shows one row of cities: Near me, Frisco, Prosper, Plano, Dallas, McKinney, Allen, Fort Worth, Arlington, Irving, Denton, Richardson, Celina, The Colony, Little Elm, Grapevine, Southlake — 16 cities plus Near me. Tapping a city switches the homepage to that city under the UC-425 rule (header, Live Now feed, Coming Soon, and restaurant list all change to that city). "Near me" uses GPS detect (UC-587) and snaps to the nearest live city. A city with no live Drops shows the UC-374 / UC-427 0-live empty state, never another city's feed.
- **UC-616** Explore TX — the Texas explorer entry (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. "Explore TX" opens the explorer: the 16-city row (UC-615) under the frame "DFW Metroplex · Pickup only — no delivery". Browsing any city in the explorer is open; only the claim is gated (UC-585). The explorer is the way a customer looks beyond their own city; it never lists a Drop that cannot be picked up in the city shown.
- **UC-617** 4:30 PM Drop alert — one text at dinner-decision time (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The Drop finds the customer at 4:30 PM CT — right when they are deciding dinner. One text at 4:30 PM CT with tonight's bundle, the real saving, and how many are left. One tap to claim — location is confirmed first, exactly like every claim (UC-585). One text per customer per day at most, and only to customers who opted in to Drop alerts (UC-214, UC-385). The alert is a marketing send under the UC-250 cap — the weekly marketing cap (open item 14) governs how many days a week a customer can receive it; the 4:30 time is the proposal, the cap is the open decision. The alert is never sent to a customer with STOP on file, and never sent after the Drop has sold out.
- **UC-618** 4:30 PM alert content — bundle, price, saving, Serves, window, units left (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. The alert text names the bundle, the bundle price and the crossed-out singles total, Serves, the pickup window, and the units left — e.g. "Tonight in Frisco: Hyderabadi Biryani Family Bundle — $28 (was $44). Serves 4 · Pickup 5:00–8:00 PM · 7 left." The saving is the real singles-vs-bundle saving (UC-595), never a fake compare-at price. The alert states the next alert time ("Next alert: Today at 4:30 PM CT"). The tap lands on the Drop page and the claim gates there (UC-585) — no link in the text skips the location gate.
- **UC-619** Share this Drop — the Drop page share (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. Every Drop page has "Share this Drop — send it to the table." Sharing sends the Drop card — the dish, the saving, and the pickup window — to who the customer is eating with ("Feeds the table — pass it on. This bundle is built for 2+ people."). The shared link opens the Drop page; a claim from it follows the normal claim flow and the location gate (UC-585); attribution follows last-tagged-link-wins (UC-248).
- **UC-620** Share your Drop — the order page share (P1) — NEW, PROPOSED. The order page has "Share your Drop — feeds 4, pass it on." A customer who claimed a bundle built to share can send the Drop card to the table — they see the dish, the saving, and the pickup window. Sharing never shares the QR ticket or the order's claim code — only the Drop. The share is available until the pickup window ends.
- **UC-621** Indian-only bundle view — every Drop is an Indian bundle for 2+ (P0) — NEW, PROPOSED. The bundle view shows Indian bundles only: every Drop below is a bundle from an Indian restaurant, built for 2 or more people to enjoy together — never a single portion, no other cuisines, no solo meals. Each card proves the saving the same way: (1) what's inside — every item, listed, quantities included; (2) singles vs bundle — each item's single price adds up to the crossed-out total, the bundle price sits next to it; (3) the saving — dollars, % and per person ("Save $16 (36%) · $7.00 per person · Serves 4"). Live bundles are ordered earliest pickup first; coming bundles are Indian bundles not yet claimable, with windows opening 48+ hours ahead (UC-395 lead).
- **UC-622** Indian Restaurants shelf — Indian kitchens only (P2) — NEW, PROPOSED. The restaurant shelf in the bundle view lists Indian kitchens only — every Drop they run is a bundle for 2 or more. A restaurant with no live or coming bundle shows the "Next Drop coming soon" state with alert signup (UC-174 edge case), never an empty card.

## Reconciliation note — Studio §33 (cancel / claim-accept) is NOT re-added as cases

The Studio's §33 (Studio UC-343–UC-369, 27 cases, titled "the agreed policy") covers the same ground as file §25 (UC-254–UC-269, cancel policy) and file §26 (UC-270–UC-282, claim → confirm → Ready). Every one of its 27 cases maps to a file case: UC-343→UC-255, UC-344→UC-256, UC-345→UC-257, UC-346→UC-258, UC-347→UC-255/UC-256/UC-186/UC-187, UC-348→UC-262, UC-349→UC-262, UC-350→UC-255/UC-267, UC-351→UC-267, UC-352→UC-259, UC-353→UC-260, UC-354→UC-261, UC-355→UC-263, UC-356→UC-264, UC-357→UC-264, UC-358→UC-264/UC-265/UC-267, UC-359→UC-265, UC-360→UC-266, UC-361→UC-269, UC-362→UC-267, UC-363→UC-270, UC-364→UC-272/UC-274, UC-365→UC-271/UC-273, UC-366→UC-279/UC-281, UC-367→UC-277, UC-368→UC-276/UC-278, UC-369→UC-280. Re-adding them would duplicate the policy layer with different wording. File §25/§26 therefore REMAIN the policy layer; the Studio's §33 numbering (UC-343–UC-369) does NOT carry over into this file — in this file UC-343–UC-369 are the §34/§35 cases above. Two corrections to the Studio's wording are recorded here: (a) §33 is titled "the agreed policy" in the Studio, but the cancel policy and the claim-confirm flow are PROPOSED, not agreed — Mahendra has not tapped agree (open items 15 and 17); (b) the Studio's in-section detail (e.g. "Cancel button replaced by 'Need help with this order?'", "a cancel with no message is a failed cancel") is design detail for the build, not a separate use case, and is noted here so it is not lost.

## Traceability notes for Claude Code

- **Old → new ID map (§13–§18):** 121→125, 122→126, 123→127, 124→128, 125→129, 126→130, 127→131, 128→132, 129→133, 130→134; 131→135 … 140→144; 141→145 … 150→154; 151→155 … 160→164; 161→165 … 175→179; 176→180 … 190→194. §12 keeps UC-121–UC-124.
- **Prototype status labels are NOT build status.** "Managed here" in the prototype = a working control in a static mock. "Preview" = screen/flow shown, production enforcement is server-side. The 9 Preview items are listed in open item 12 above.
- **Design locks that must not be re-litigated in the build:** red DROP badge/buttons stay; preview = live (one shared component); video is a reusable dish asset with no price/date/Claim text burned in; no POS integration; no consumer app; takeout, not delivery; depth over breadth (limited restaurants, multiple Drops each, sell out); Dallas only until one Drop validates. - **V2.6 research note — customer identity & opt-in recognition (§37).** Deep search done Nov 8, 2026 before writing §37. Sources used (all web, retrieved Nov 8, 2026):
  - Twilio Consent Management API (https://www.twilio.com/docs/messaging/features/consent-api): stores consent per contact (phone in E.164), per sender, with status opt-in/opt-out, source (website / offline / opt-in-message / opt-out-message / others), and date_of_consent; cross-channel consent sync across RCS, SMS, MMS; STOP creates TWO opt-out records (Messaging Service level + sender level) and both must be cleared on re-opt-in, or sends keep failing with error 21610. This is the model for UC-388/UC-389.
  - Shopify community — SMS authentication for customer accounts (https://community.shopify.com/t/sms-authentication-for-new-customer-accounts/572185): Shopify has NO native SMS authentication for customer accounts (email/password or passwordless email link/code only); phone+OTP login comes from third-party apps. Implication for DealsPro: phone+OTP is a build, not a platform default — UC-383 proposes it as a build, not as a Shopify-style toggle.
  - IndiSell one-click checkout (https://indisell.io/features/checkout): "the phone number becomes the identity, an OTP replaces the password, and a returning shopper's address is already filled in"; OTP on WhatsApp with SMS fallback; per-phone rate caps against OTP flooding; returning shopper sees saved details immediately. Model for UC-382/UC-383/UC-384.
  - SimplyClub fast login (https://dropins.simplyclub.co.il/content/login/fast-login/): phone + OTP only, no storefront account; "Identity … is phone-only — there is no email or customer-id fallback"; OTP with 120-second TTL; new member auto-created phone-only, returning member recognised by phone lookup (`isNewUser: false`). Model for UC-382/UC-383 (120-second OTP expiry) and UC-384 (returning recognition by phone lookup).
  - Consent-record practice (FGCHH TechLabs SMS opt-in proof, https://fgchhtechlabs.com/twilio-compliance-proof.html; and the TCPA/CTIA compliance pack): each SMS purpose has its own optional consent, none pre-checked; consent is enforced server-side, not just on the page; BOTH answers are stored (including declines) with timestamp, disclosure-text version, and IP — "a record that only exists when someone agrees cannot tell 'they said no' apart from 'nobody asked them'". Model for UC-385/UC-388/UC-390.
  - What was NOT found: no authoritative source for Instagram-DM opt-in as a marketing channel equivalent to SMS/WhatsApp — hence open item 19(d) leaves Instagram DM opt-in unspecified.
